# SPECTRA spec — the live reconciliation surface

This is the thing that did not exist before 2026-08-15, and its absence is why the Admiral kept
finding capability gaps himself minutes after someone reported nothing was missed: there was
nothing to reconcile against. This file is that reference. It is not a design doc, not a
tutorial, not a status report — it is a **ledger**: every SPECTRA-relevant capability, its
honest current state, and every open decision that blocks something.

**Read [Maintenance protocol](#maintenance-protocol) before editing this file.** Everyone who
touches SPECTRA touches this file when one of three trigger conditions fires — that is what
keeps it live instead of becoming the next stale artefact nobody trusts.

Origin: Admiral-authorised 2026-08-15 (`data/backlog.md` `spectra-live-spec`), built from
`data/spotfx-capability-audit/report.md` (46 capabilities, code-cited, dated 2026-08-14),
`data/captain.md` (his three spec answers), and `data/captain-shared.md` (standing order 6:
write for agents).

---

## Maintenance protocol

**Who:** any agent working on SPECTRA — crew, Captain DJ, firstmate. Nobody owns this file
exclusively; whoever hits a trigger condition below edits it in the same change.

**When — exactly three trigger conditions, no others:**
1. **A PR proving a capability lands** (merges to `master`) → flip that row's state, cite the
   PR number, delete any now-stale "unverified" note.
2. **The Admiral answers an open question or states a new decision** → see *Translating an
   Admiral edit* below. This is the one that needs care.
3. **An audit or code check finds this ledger wrong** — a row claims a state the code no longer
   matches → fix the row, tag it `(code-verified <date>)`.

Do not edit this file to tidy prose, re-narrate the audit, or record day-to-day PR review/CI
status — that churn belongs in `data/backlog.md` (fleet-managed, changes hourly). This file
tracks **capability state and decisions**, which change far less often than task-queue status.

### Translating an Admiral edit (read this before touching his text)

When the Admiral edits this file directly — a sentence dropped into a row, a correction, a
comment — **his words are a statement of intent, not a patch to merge as-is.**

1. **Do not leave his sentence sitting next to the structured parts unconverted.** It will rot:
   nobody will translate it later, and the next agent will either misread it as binding prose at
   the wrong altitude, or ignore it and lose the intent entirely.
2. **Find or create the correct structured slot for it** — a ledger row's State/Notes, a new row
   in [Open questions](#open-questions), a new or amended line under
   [Standing decisions](#standing-decisions) — and write the intent into that slot, in the
   document's own vocabulary (the state legend, the table columns). Translate, don't transcribe.
3. **Record that his edit drove it.** Append `(Admiral edit, <date>: "<short paraphrase or
   verbatim clause>")` inline at the point of change. This is provenance, not decoration — it is
   what lets a later agent tell "he decided this" apart from "we inferred this from code."
4. **If his sentence doesn't cleanly resolve into an existing slot** — it's ambiguous, or answers
   a question nobody had asked — don't force-fit it. File it as a new row in
   [Open questions](#open-questions) naming what's ambiguous, with a note that it came from a raw
   edit, and ask him directly rather than guessing.
5. **Never delete his raw sentence before doing 1–4.** If you genuinely can't resolve it in the
   same sitting, move it verbatim into
   [Unprocessed Admiral notes](#unprocessed-admiral-notes) at the bottom rather than losing it or
   leaving it stranded mid-document. That section should be empty in steady state — its presence
   at all is a signal something is owed.

---

## Landed vs. deployed (a real gap this document does not close)

Every state below describes what's in git (`master`), not what `spectra.service` is actually
running on his box right now. **Those two can diverge, and it already has.** PR #57 (§44) was
proven live on his real hardware — 11,877 triggers migrated, UPDATE glide confirmed — **while
the PR was still unmerged**; PR #69's success-case proof (§51) ran against commit `bb1b759` on
his live room before that commit belonged to a merged PR at all. In both cases, a routine sync
or resync of the deployed box at that moment would have silently reverted a capability this
ledger would otherwise call `BUILT`.

**What this means for anyone reading a `BUILT` row: it says the capability exists in `master`'s
history. It does not say it's what's currently running live.** Checking actual deployed state
means checking the live box itself (`systemctl --user status spectra`, the commit it's running),
not this file and not `git log`. This document tracks landed state only and does not attempt to
track deployment state — that is a distinct, real gap, named here rather than silently assumed
away.

---

## Standing decisions

### D1 — The fidelity rule

> "Exact where there is a direct equivalent, free where the mechanism differs in kind."
> (Admiral, 2026-08-14, `data/captain.md`)

Where SPECTRA has a like-for-like control — a button, a mode, a thing doing the same job as the
old SpotFX one — **any difference he can feel is a defect, not a design choice.** Read the old
implementation's real numbers before writing. Where the mechanism is genuinely different in kind
(continuous colour drift replacing on/off triggers), build for the same intent and let him
correct it after living with it.

This is why the ledger's `BUILT` state carries a fidelity qualifier (`full` / `extended` /
`partial`): `full` claims exact fidelity to a direct legacy equivalent; `extended` claims the
free half of the rule (same intent, different — and here, richer — mechanism); `partial` is not
a fidelity claim at all, it's an honest "narrower, and nobody is currently building the rest."

Worked failure case on file: the ambient release shipped 2026-08-14 copied legacy's fade numbers
exactly but omitted its second phase, an ~8s eased catch-up — he caught it by watching. Fixed in
PR #61; see §6.

### D2 — The day-one switchover bar is four items, not three

> "Add colour-set authoring and the trigger migration to the bar."
> (Admiral, 2026-08-14, widening his own earlier 3-item bar)

Blockers to switchover: **dark/light mode (§9), colour-set and group authoring
(§10), the trigger migration (§44)**. Force Scene (§31) and Ambient
(§6) are DONE. Current state of all four: [Day-one bar](#day-one-bar) below.

### D3 — Step-through triggers: closed, not building the mini-timeline

> "The flare-and-return moment is what I valued." (Admiral, 2026-08-14)

The ordering/choreography of legacy's multi-step triggers was incidental; what he valued is the
moment everything flares and snaps back. Extend the cheap flare-and-snap-back mechanism (named
flare kinds, momentary hold — §11) instead of building the Sequence/Parallel
mini-timeline-inside-a-scene. **Closed as answered — do not raise it again unless he does.**

### D4 — Legacy retirement is gated, not scheduled

The legacy SpotFX authoring world (events/triggers/choosers) retires **only** on the Admiral's
satisfaction with SPECTRA plus his explicit destructive word on the day
(`data/backlog.md` `spotfx-legacy-retirement-gate`, corr=821f1ec08f6cb466). Every `GAP` and
`OLD-WORLD` row below is safe today only because legacy is still the thing actually driving his
room — S3 hardware handover ([`docs/SPECTRA_HANDOVER.md`](SPECTRA_HANDOVER.md)) is built and
proven but gated off. Nothing in this ledger implies urgency beyond what the day-one bar (D2)
states.

### D5 — Release doctrine: SPECTRA fades and lets go; Home Assistant owns the restore

Settled 2026-08-15 (reported via firstmate, following PR #67's live-fixture proof — see §49).
Release-to-HA's job is to relinquish the room cleanly, not to hand it back lit: SPECTRA fades
each Hue bulb on the bridge, then powers it off, and its contract ends there. Bringing the room
back up afterward is the Admiral's Home Assistant automation's job, not SPECTRA's. **Do not
build a "restore to previous state" step into release** as a future "fix" — a dark bulb after
release is the correct, intended outcome, not an incomplete one.

### D6 — A success response from his Hue bridge proves acceptance, never application

Measured 2026-08-15/16 (`data/spectra-hue-burst-drop-and-false-unlit/`, PR #93 merged — full
detail at §61). **What is established**: 30 captured raw responses during deliberately-forced
load show his bridge returns a clean HTTP 200 with an empty `errors` array and no rate-limit
header of any kind, whether or not a command actually took effect — the bridge cannot signal a
silent discard at the protocol level, at all. **What is NOT established, and must not be
repeated**: an earlier draft of this finding claimed a specific burst-rate cliff (fast writes
dropped, paced writes survive). That claim traced to a bug in the investigation's own probe
script, was retracted in full once caught, and a corrected 48-trial sweep across both bridges
found zero drops at any pace tested — **there is no known drop-rate threshold, and do not imply
his hardware drops commands under load.** The practical consequence stands regardless of pacing:
read-back verification (`ambient.py`'s `_hold_and_confirm`, §51; `ambient_music_gate.py`'s
`verify_now()`, §58) is necessary, not cautious, because a 2xx alone proves nothing. What actually
caused the real incidents that prompted this investigation remains an open question — see
[OQ-8](#open-questions) — separate from the (now-fixed) detect-but-don't-repair and false-unlit
defects §61 closes. **§64 is a full audit of every SPECTRA site that writes to the Hue bridge
against this rule** — most already complied (ambient.py, or were structurally exempt); one gap
was found and closed (the release path's off-write, §64).

## Capability ledger

### State legend

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `BUILT` | A SPECTRA equivalent exists and runs today. Carries a fidelity qualifier: `full` (D1's exact-match claim), `extended` (D1's free-but-same-intent claim, sometimes richer than legacy), or `partial` (narrower than legacy, no known further work scheduled). |
| `AGREED` | The Admiral has explicitly authorised or asked for this; code may or may not exist yet. Carries a PR number when in flight. |
| `GAP` | No SPECTRA equivalent, and no authorised work is currently scheduled to build one. |
| `OLD-WORLD` | Legacy SpotFX covers this and SPECTRA is not expected to — either because nothing has moved it there yet and it's low-priority, or because it's the kind of thing (device/hardware management) meant to stay in the legacy app even after handover. |
| `RETIRED` | The Admiral's explicit 2026-08-12 retirement decision (`data/spectra-gap-inventory/decision-legacy-retirement-picks.md`). Not being rebuilt anywhere; nothing deleted from legacy either. |
| `N/A` | Architecturally inapplicable to SPECTRA by design — not a gap. |

Numbering follows the audit's own tier order so a lookup there needs no translation. The audit
counts 46 capabilities by also numbering four sub-pieces of §1 separately; this ledger folds
those into §1's note instead of giving them their own rows (no information lost — see §1).
**§43–§73 are SPECTRA-native builds that emerged after the audit's 2026-08-14 cutoff** — not in
the original 46, added here because this ledger is the live surface and they didn't exist yet
when the audit ran.

### Tier 1 — foundational

| § | Capability | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| §1 | Live audio cross-correlation sync engine | `BUILT` (partial, PR #71 merged) | Admiral's direct ask — "the xcorr sync from SpotFX was very good and should be ported almost directly," plus a reported live degradation. **Live-measured root cause, not "still adapting" as he suspected**: `spectra/services/bridge.py` silently dropped the `timing` sibling spot-effects already broadcasts on every WS "state" message, so `trigger_engine.tick()` fired on the raw, uncorrected Spotify position while spot-effects' own engine has always compared against `effective_now = now_ms + effective_offset_ms`. Measured live against his room, 7 samples over ~40s of confirmed `is_playing=true` playback: a stable `+7052ms` `shape_offset_ms` for the song then playing, not drifting/adapting at all across the window — the correction was simply never wired through, not converging. Fix: `bridge.py` now parses `timing`, exposes `effective_position_ms()` (raw + `shape_offset_ms`, spot-effects' own formula), and `services/engine.py`'s trigger poll feeds that instead of the raw position. **Deliberately NOT ported**: the audio capture + correlation computation itself (stays in spot-effects — duplicating it into SPECTRA's own process would fight the same PipeWire monitor exclusivity `fx/audio_ingest.py`'s own docstring already documents as a starvation problem within ONE process, report §2.2) and `effective_offset_ms`'s `ledfx_trigger_buffer_ms`/`ledfx_rtt_ms` terms (compensate for spot-effects' own LedFX-HTTP write-transport latency, a mechanism SPECTRA's executor doesn't share — genuine incompatibility, not an oversight). Still folds in the 4 audit sub-pieces at partial fidelity: mid-song drift monitor / fast-lock / DJ-mix search escalation are inherited transitively (SPECTRA consumes whatever spot-effects converges to, live) rather than run natively in SPECTRA; the sync-lock status badge is now visible via §23's `LockBadge` port, spot-effects' own state through a SPECTRA page, same reuse pattern as §13/§14. |
| §2 | Scene Groups (rotate/cycle a scene pool) | `GAP` | No authorised build found. Blocks the Drop-into-Group payoff half of §32 and the retired per-trigger overrides in §42. |
| §3 | AI-assisted structural trigger placement | `BUILT` (partial) | `spectra/services/midsong_generator.py` seeds one generic fire-scene trigger per section boundary; no genre-tuned profiles, no per-role placement (Song-Start/Bass-Entry/Lull/Charge/Drop/Quiet/Flare), no bass-onset snapping, no auto-tuning. Deliberate scope per its own docstring, not a bug. |
| §4 | Triggerless & Dinner Party automatic show | `GAP` | Dinner Party is a pause-only signal today (`spectra/services/scene_sequencer.py:411`); the sequencer that could substitute ships dark by design. Explicitly kept, not dropped, per the 2026-08-12 signoff (same one that keeps §12). |
| §5 | Ambient — Hue entertainment-area selection | `BUILT` (full), PR #109 merged (`6ecfbc5`), room-unproven | He asked twice; it sat on a hold waiting for a word he was never told he needed to say — built without waiting for a third ask. Read legacy first, per instruction: `services/ambient_mode.py` held per GROUP (`state.ambient_groups`, one Hue device id per entertainment config/bridge), exposed via a long-press checkbox picker on the front-page Ambient button (`web/src/nowplaying/AmbientButton.tsx`, `GET /control/ambient-groups`, `POST /control/ambient-mode?groups=<id>`) — no separate device-CATEGORY tier existed above that picker in the sense he asked about; legacy's `ambient_target_category` only pre-filtered which devices were eligible groups at all, and SPECTRA has no LedFX-category concept to port that tier to (every live Hue device already IS a candidate group). Ported the group tier only: `RoomControlState.ambient_hue_group_ids` (`[]` = every live Hue device, today's unmodified default — nobody's room changes on deploy), `spectra/services/ambient.py`'s `reconcile()`/`verify_held()` both take the resolved target set — the hold path only touches devices IN scope; the release path additionally releases a device that's currently frozen but fell OUT of scope (a group just deselected while Ambient stays engaged), gated on a new read-only `frozen` property on `fx/devices/hue.py` (VENDOR.md deviation #11) specifically so an out-of-scope device that was NEVER frozen is left completely untouched — `set_frozen(False)` on an already-unfrozen device forces a real stream reconnect it never needed. Whole-room OFF stays unconditional (unchanged behaviour, ignores the current selection — turning Ambient off releases everyone). UI: `AmbientGroupsPicker.tsx` (checkbox list, reuses `FavoritesPicker.tsx`'s proven phone-safe modal shape) behind a "Hue areas" button on `RoomControlsBar.tsx`, `GET /api/room-controls/ambient-groups` for names. **The picker's data source is config-backed, not a live runtime call — this is a structural difference from legacy, not a cosmetic one**: legacy's own `resolve_groups()` (`services/ambient_mode.py:343`) round-trips a live HTTP call to LedFX on every open and has its own documented 2026-08-12 incident scar — "a genuinely-empty target category and 'LedFX unreachable' are indistinguishable here... during a transient outage" — worked around by serving stale cached names past their TTL rather than showing an empty picker. `spectra/services/ambient.py::list_groups()` has no equivalent failure mode to work around: it reads `live_host.live.host.devices`, an in-process dict SPECTRA populates from `storage/spectra/fx-live/config.json` at activation, not a network call that can time out or 404 — "No cache (unlike legacy, which round-tripped an HTTP call to LedFX) — reading `live_host.live.host.devices` is a free in-process dict lookup, not worth caching" (the function's own docstring). The picker is legitimately empty in exactly one case — SPECTRA doesn't currently own the live stack, or the room has no live Hue device at all (`list_groups()`: "a UI picker with nothing to show, not an error") — which is an ownership-state fact, not a transient-fetch failure, and needs no cache to paper over. Verified by read-back, same discipline as the rest of Ambient: `tests/test_ambient.py`'s new group-selection section proves a selected group's REST writes never reach an unselected device's bulb, an unselected-but-never-held device receives zero calls of any kind, a group deselected mid-hold is genuinely released (fade → catch-up → unfreeze) while the other stays held, and `verify_held()` scoped to a selection never reads back the other device's lights. Phone-verified at a real 390×844 viewport against an isolated instance (spare port, two fake Hue devices, no real bridge credentials, real spot-effects/LedFX ports pointed at nothing so the process can't reach them) — screenshot + a live checkbox-uncheck-then-Save round trip through the real API, confirmed via `GET /room-controls`. **Not yet room-proven against his actual two bridges** (`hue-lights` 10 bulbs, `dining-hues` 7) — his room stayed off-limits for this task per its own hard limits. |
| §6 | Ambient — release/handback fidelity (the OFF path) | `BUILT` (full) | PR #61 merged. Colour-matched fade → verified unfreeze → live-pixel-buffer catch-up tween, matching legacy's two-phase handback (`spectra/services/ambient.py`). Room-proven 2026-08-14, `docs/spectra-room-window-proofs-2026-08-14.md` Proof 2 (PASS). **DELTA since audit**: the audit read this as a gap; PR #61 closed it the same day. See §51 for the ON-path companion fix (confirmed-vs-attempted light hold), a distinct defect found the next day. |
| §7 | Device Categories (organise/import lights) | `OLD-WORLD` | `spectra/api/registry.py:21-36` reads categories read-only; nothing writes to `device_categories.json` from SPECTRA. No authorised build found — use the legacy app. |
| §8 | Shape Maps (odd-shaped matrix geometry) | `OLD-WORLD` | Render engine understands shape maps (`fx/virtuals.py`); no router/dialog under `spectra/`. No authorised build found — use the legacy app. |
| §9 | Global Dark/Light Mode | `BUILT` (full) | Day-one bar item (D2). PR #70 merged (`7aaf01d`). The per-trigger `display_mode` field on the ported legacy-proxy dialog (`spectra/web/src/timeline/types.ts:18`) is untouched and still unread by `spectra/*.py` — that field belongs to the retired per-node Light Mode Chooser (§36), not this feature; don't conflate them. What shipped: `RoomControlState.dark_mode_enabled` (room bar checkbox) toggles the SAME LedFX-side `dark_lock` clamp legacy's own `services/display_mode.py` drives (the mechanism is vendored into `fx/` verbatim), over two new ownership-routed primitives on `spectra/services/fx_seam.py` (`get_virtuals`/`set_virtual_config`, alongside `apply_writes` — same HTTP-pre-handover/in-process-post-handover routing, so this needed no ownership logic of its own). `spectra/services/dark_light.py` is the reconcile logic and carries the full fidelity reasoning, incl. two deliberate D1 "differs in kind" calls made from the legacy code, not re-asked of him: (1) a bool, not legacy's three-state Default/Dark/Light cycle, because "Default" only ever meant "defer to a lower cascade level" and every lower level (trigger → scene group → scene → Set Color action → colour cards) is the retired §36/§42 machinery — with nothing left to defer to, Default and Light are the same state; (2) restores from a live pre-dark snapshot (captured via `fx_seam.get_virtuals()`, persisted to survive a restart, replayed via `fx_seam.apply_writes()`) rather than legacy's "re-fire the last Color Set," since that concept belongs to the same retired authoring world; (3) added post-review 2026-08-15, prompted by a live Ambient incident on a different feature that surfaced the same class of mistake: the light-transition repaint is gated on `bridge.is_playing()` — while music plays, the stale pre-dark snapshot is deliberately not forced back (`dark_lock` still clears; nothing stays forced black), left to the room's own live show instead, surfaced as `repaint_skipped: "music_playing"` and a room-bar badge (same lesson §52/§53's music-precedence fix teaches for Ambient, reached independently). Shielding (`dark_light_shield_categories`, default `["Singles"]` — legacy's own default; `dark_light_shield_virtuals`) is ported verbatim via the shared read-only category registry. **Composes with `ambient_mode`'s three settings (§52/§53), not a boolean** — rebased 2026-08-15 onto PR #76: whenever a Hue device is actually frozen right now (`ambient_mode="always"` unconditionally, or `"auto"` while confirmed not-playing), it's driven by direct bridge REST and invisible to `dark_lock`; the moment it isn't frozen (`"off"`, or `"auto"` while confirmed playing), it's LedFX-rendered and responds normally — a property of the write path, not a rule either feature encodes about the other. Also composes with §10's Colour Set Groups (PR #87, merged the same day): dark/light's `dark_lock` clamp and repaint act on whatever effect a virtual is CURRENTLY running, regardless of whether a scene fire that landed it there resolved a plain Colour Set or a Group member — the two features never reference each other's fields, same "compose for free by construction" shape as the Ambient case. Measurement note (mirrors AGENTS.md's "Reading real Hue bulb state" entry): dark/light's own mechanism is LedFX-side, verified via `fx_seam.get_virtuals()` (`dark_lock` + effect config on `/api/virtuals`) — never the Hue CLIP light resource, which is Ambient's own instrument for a different subsystem. Verification reads real state back from LedFX after every push (`unconfirmed` in the API response), never trusts the write. Offline proof: `tests/test_dark_light.py` (11 tests), incl. a frame-level test against a real headless dummy host (`fx/headless.py`) proving the actual vendored clamp engages, the restore repaints the exact pre-dark background, and the music-aware gate holds both ways (confirmed True skips; confirmed False or unresolved/None proceeds) — not mocked. **PROVEN AT HIS FIXTURES** (`data/backlog.md` `spectra-dark-light-mode-bar`, checked off 2026-08-15: "Done requires his-room end-to-end proof per standing order 11" — met). **The composition gap this same invisibility created, found and now ruled on**: while `dark_mode_enabled` is on and a Hue device is frozen (`ambient_mode="always"`), `dark_lock` correctly has no effect on that device (it isn't LedFX-rendered) — but from where he stands, toggling something he calls GLOBAL dark/light leaving his main Hue lights bright reads as broken, not as two features each doing exactly what he asked (`data/backlog.md` `spectra-dark-light-vs-ambient-ruling`, filed 2026-08-15, three readings offered: dark overrides the hold / ambient-always wins / the two compose). **His ruling, verbatim, matches the third reading**: "dark mode during ambient should choose a different color that I pick so we have one color for regular ambient light mode or hybrid mode and then one color for dark mode for now make them the same but let me be able to pick it with the color picker." Dark does not bypass Ambient and Ambient does not block dark — **dark changes WHAT Ambient holds**. See §63 for the fix built to his ruling (PR #91, merged and room-proven). `data/backlog.md` `spectra-dark-light-mode`. **THREE-STATE REBUILD (2026-08-16, branch `fm/spectra-display-mode-build`, room-unproven)**: a scout investigation (`data/spectra-display-mode-three-state/report.md`) found the two-state build above was a mislabel, not an honest smaller version — `dark_mode_enabled=False` was called "Light" in the code, the API `status` field, and the help text, but behaved as legacy's Default (nothing forced); legacy's actual Light (a configurable, always-on, immediately-repainted forced background) was never built, so he had no dark-vs-light before/after to test against (his scenes all author dark colour sets, so Default and Dark look identical). Rebuilt to all three states: `RoomControlState.dark_mode_enabled: bool` → `display_mode: "default" | "dark" | "light"` (his word "hybrid" = `"default"` internally, labelled "Hybrid" in the UI — his standing ruling). **Hybrid confirmed, plainly**: Hybrid is the state that leaves backgrounds alone and lets each scene's own authored colours show — SPECTRA forces nothing, the same "nothing to force" reasoning the earlier two-state build's own Default already rested on (above: "with nothing left to defer to, Default and Light are the same state" — that observation about the OLD build is exactly why the new build's `"default"` needed no new mechanism, only a rename to the word he actually uses). Dark forces `dark_lock`; Light forces `display_light_bg_color`/`_brightness`; Hybrid forces neither. **Migration, load-bearing** (`room_controls.load_room_controls`): `dark_mode_enabled: true` → `display_mode: "dark"`; `dark_mode_enabled: false` → `display_mode: "default"`, **never** `"light"` — the old field was named light but behaved as default, so mapping false→light would have silently turned on a forced background on his live room the moment this migration ran. Nobody's room changes on this deploy; Light is a new option he can pick, never something he's moved into. New Light mechanism (`spectra/services/dark_light.py`'s `"light"` branch): `display_light_bg_color`/`display_light_bg_brightness` (new `RoomControlState` fields, legacy defaults `#201830`/`0.3`, same `ColorGradientPicker` the ambient swatches use) are written as `background_color`/`background_brightness` onto every non-shielded virtual's CURRENT live effect config (read via `fx_seam.get_virtuals()`, merged, written via `fx_seam.apply_writes()` — running effect type and every other/foreground param untouched), **unconditionally** — not gated on `bridge.is_playing()`, unlike the Default/Hybrid repaint, because a fresh forced write is authoritative the instant it lands (same reasoning as Dark's own unconditional clamp) and gating it would have defeated the point: watching it work while his music plays. Shielding applies to Light exactly as it already did to Dark. The pre-dark snapshot is cleared on every transition away from `"dark"` (to `"light"` or `"default"` alike), since Light's own write makes the snapshot stale. `DarkLightResult.status` and the room-bar badges now read `"default"`/`"dark"`/`"light"` honestly (the old build's `status: "light"` meaning default is retired). UI: `RoomControlsBar.tsx`'s checkbox replaced with a Hybrid/Dark/Light select plus the light-bg colour/brightness picker, phone-verified at 390×844 against an isolated instance (spare port, no live devices — not his room). Tests: `tests/test_dark_light.py` (18, incl. new Light frame-level + migration-mapping proofs), `tests/test_room_controls.py`, `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` updated for the field rename; full suite 550 passed / 3 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated (needs his seeded live storage, see `AGENTS.md`'s environmental-failures note) / 2 skipped. Not yet room-proven — his room is untouched by this build. |
| §10 | Colour Set Groups (rotating/synced pools) | `BUILT` (full) — **room-proven** 2026-08-16, one honest asterisk | Day-one bar item (D2) — built and headlessly proven (25 tests, `tests/test_color_set_groups.py`; full suite otherwise green — one pre-existing environmental failure unrelated to this change, needs his live-seeded `storage/spectra/scenes.json`, absent in any disposable worktree). Read his real data before designing, per the task's own instruction: `storage/color_sets.json` (2026-08-15) has 8 authored groups — 100% `mode="cycle"`, `exclude_current=True`, `palette_sync=True`; 5/8 carry real override entries; none use weighted mode or the dark/light mode-lane fields, confirmed not assumed. `spectra/services/color_sets.py`'s `ColorSetCard` now carries `members`/`mode`/`cycle_behavior`/`exclude_current`/`palette_sync` (previously `extra="ignore"`-dropped — structurally invisible to every consumer); `spectra/services/color_set_groups.py` is the ONE resolution point — cycle (wrap/bounce)/weighted+exclude_current picking, Palette Sync anchored on `color_journey`'s own room state (D1's free case: reuses SPECTRA's existing `active_set_id`/`wheel_position_deg` rather than a parallel legacy-style tracker), the group's own override `entries` merged onto the picked member per virtual — ported from spot-effects' `trigger_engine._select_color_set_member`/`_execute_set_color`. Wired into every explicit choke point that was silently mishandling a Group id (`scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id` — THE single scene-fire path both the sequencer and SPECTRA-native triggers funnel through; `trigger_engine._default_select_color_set`; `POST /room-color/apply`; the editor's baseline endpoint; `SpectraTriggerDialog.tsx`'s colour-set pickers, which excluded groups outright) — those `kind == "set"` filters were load-bearing, per the task's own warning, and are now widened rather than removed. Deliberately NOT ported: dark/light "mode lane" variants (§36 RETIRED, zero real usage) and the scene-group colour-ref sentinels (§42 RETIRED, depends on §2 which doesn't exist in SPECTRA). Deliberately NOT wired: the sequencer/journey's own automatic wheel-travel roll (`color_wheel.wheel_positions`, `scene_sequencer._default_eligible_sets`) — a Group has no chromatic wheel position of its own, so that mechanism stays set-only by design (D1's free half — the mechanism genuinely differs in kind: legacy drove groups by per-trigger advance/direction, SPECTRA's automatic picks are curve/wheel-travel-driven and no real group needs to be reached that way). Authoring UI: `spectra/web/src/colorsets/ColorSetsPage.tsx` (`/colorsets`, new nav "Colours") — create/edit/delete either kind; "▶ Apply to room" is the live proof surface (fires the same `POST /room-color/apply` a trigger would, toasting which member a Group picked — press repeatedly to watch it rotate). Writes go straight through spot-effects' own already-general `/api/color-sets` (the same pattern the existing opt-out toggle already used) — SPECTRA's own backend still only ever reads this storage, never writes it. **Room-proven, 2026-08-16, against his real deployed checkout** (PR #87 already merged and running as `spectra.service`; this pass, PR #95, authored, fired, and cleaned up through the live authoring surface, not a spare instance). Method: a throwaway Group (`ZZ-Proof-Group`) plus three throwaway solid-colour Sets (Red/Green/Blue), all created through the real `ColorSetsPage.tsx` at `/spectra/colorsets` — form fills and the real colour picker, not a raw POST — scoped to `tv-mapper` (a real WLED-backed virtual: `sconce-kitchen-left`, `sconce-kitchen-right`, `tv-backlight`; confirmed by reading `storage/spectra/fx-live/config.json`'s segment list before picking it, specifically to avoid `radial-dummy`, which shares `tv-mapper`'s category but is a `type: "dummy"` placeholder with no physical light behind it). Fired repeatedly via the same "▶ Apply to room" button → `POST /room-color/apply` a trigger would use. **Measurement**: `tv-mapper` is WLED, not Hue, so entertainment-configuration status doesn't apply to it — the instrument used was `GET /spectra/api/engine/status`'s `executor.recent_writes`, read fresh after every fire, matched against the toast's own "picked X" text. All five documented mechanics confirmed by deterministic or statistical sequences of real writes, not by trusting the toast alone: cycle/wrap (Red→Green→Blue→Red→Green, exact); cycle/bounce, same group switched live (Blue→Green→Red→Green→Blue, exact reversal at both ends); weighted+`exclude_current` (Blue weight=10 vs Red/Green=1, 16 fires → 7 captured picks, Blue×3/Red×2/Green×2, zero consecutive repeats); Palette Sync, both anchor directions (applying Green then firing the group landed Blue — anchor(Green)+1; applying Red then firing landed Green — anchor(Red)+1 — matching `_pick_member`'s anchor-then-advance behaviour exactly, not just "a plausible colour"); override-entry merging (a group-level `background_brightness: 0.05` stayed fixed across two different picked members — `#0000ff`+0.05 then `#ff0000`+0.05 — while each member's own foreground colour kept varying, proving the merge layers per-field rather than replacing the whole entry). **Deliberately playback-independent**: `/room-color/apply` is a manual room-control surface, not a trigger/track-position path — his music stopped mid-proof (`bridge.is_playing()` went false partway through) and every fire after that landed identically, confirming the mechanism has no dependency on a live track. **The one honest asterisk**: Hue sat frozen under `ambient_mode="always"` for the entire proof window (his own resting state, never touched) — by the composition rule (§9/§52/§53: a frozen Hue device is driven by direct bridge REST, invisible to whatever LedFX/SPECTRA scene logic would otherwise land on it), no fire in this pass could have streamed onto a live Hue bulb regardless of target, so Group resolution specifically reaching a Hue-scoped virtual was not independently observed this pass — the resolution code path (`color_set_groups.resolve_for_fire`) is scope-agnostic and identical regardless of device type, but that's an architectural argument, not a room observation, and is named as such rather than folded into "proven." **Room left exactly as found, not as assumed**: all four throwaway cards deleted (`DELETE /api/color-sets/{id}`, confirmed back to the pre-test count of 58); the room's `active_set_id`/`wheel_position_deg` — which the proof's own direct set-applies had pointed at throwaway ids — were restored not by replaying a value captured at the start of the session, but by completing the drift conductor's own already-in-flight, already-declared destination leg (read live: `journey.destination` was mid-transit toward "Mid - Purple Blue Waves" at the moment cleanup began, having already drifted on its own from 290° to 207.5° across the proof window under its normal ~20s-leg pace, unprompted) — applying that same destination directly finishes the transition the room was already committed to rather than injecting an arbitrary "original" value that had since gone stale. Verified after cleanup: `GET /liveness` — `state: "live"`, zero `activation_gaps`; `GET /room-controls` — `ambient_mode/ambient_color/scene_change_mode` byte-identical to the values read before touching anything; `ambient` status — still `held: true`, 17/17 lit, freshly reverified. `data/backlog.md` `spectra-colorset-group-authoring`. **TIERED-LIST REWORK (2026-08-17, branch `fm/spectra-colour-groups-as-overrides`, not yet merged)**: his ask, verbatim: "in our color group list let's tier it, so that color sets are listed under a color group that contained them. Now what color groups are, are just overrides for the values of all the color sets below it. this gives me a way to bulk edit groups of color sets. Apply the current overrides that I have from back when it made more sense in spot FX." Four things established from his real data before building, per his own instruction: **(1) live layer, not destructive write** — `resolve_for_fire`'s override overlay was ALREADY non-destructive (never rewrites a member Set's own stored `entries`, verified in this module and legacy's `_execute_set_color` alike), so "bulk edit" names the EFFECT, not a mechanism to build; his legacy overrides are already live in `storage/color_sets.json` and already wired — there was nothing to migrate. **(2) what breaks: nothing** — 0 of his ~21k SPECTRA triggers/scenes target a Group id today (his "not really calling color groups like we were in SpotFX" is literally true here, though legacy's `storage/events.json` shows 5/8 groups WERE fired as pools there), so the pool-pick mechanism is kept fully intact (`SpectraTriggerDialog.tsx`'s select-colour-set action still lists Groups; `POST /room-color/apply`/`baseline/{id}`; ▶ Preview) and only visually de-emphasized under a "Rotation" heading in the editor, never removed. **(3) a Set CAN sit under >1 Group** — his real data has 4 that do (First Group ∩ Blues) — so the tiered list is a many-to-many index (a Set renders under every Group listing it, cross-referenced "also in: …"), not an invented single-owner tree. **(4) applying his overrides changes nothing rendered today** — they're already applied, already scoped exactly as before (only when the Group itself is the resolved fire target — verified this has NEVER applied to a Set fired by its own id, in legacy or here); broadening that to every direct Set reference was considered and explicitly rejected — computed against his real data, it would silently change rendered output for 27 of 28 (group, member) override pairs, with zero precedent in either codebase to justify it as a "port." Built: `ColorSetsPage.tsx`'s left list nests every Set under its Group(s) (▾/▸ collapse, "Ungrouped" section for the 8 sets under none), UI-only — no backend/storage/API change; the right-pane Group editor reorders to Overrides (foregrounded, "bulk edit for this group's colour sets") → Colour sets in this group → Rotation (demoted, with an honest note on when it fires). Phone-first: the page previously had no responsive breakpoint at all (`docs/SPECTRA_SPEC.md`'s own §67/§68 already named this gap) — added the same `useIsPhone()` drawer pattern `ScenesPage.tsx` already uses, verified at a real 390×844 viewport (chrome-devtools-axi, isolated instance on spare ports 8200/8210, his real `storage/color_sets.json` copied read-only into the worktree's own storage — his live `:8000`/`:8010` untouched): no page-level horizontal overflow, tiering/collapse/multi-group cross-refs/search-flattening/independent per-Set editing all confirmed working. **DIRECT-SET OVERRIDE BROADENING (2026-08-19, PR fm/spectra-group-overrides-on-direct-call)**: his explicit follow-up, verbatim — "[the overrides] need to apply when the colour set is called. It still needs to use the overrides from its parent group." This is exactly the broadening the tiered-list rework above investigated and rejected as invented behaviour with no precedent — he has now asked for it directly, so it's authorised. `color_set_groups.resolve_for_fire` on a `kind=="set"` card now looks up every enclosing Group carrying non-empty override `entries` (`group_ids_by_set`'s reverse index) and chains them onto the Set's own entries — same `_overlay` merge, same "later layer wins per field per virtual" rule the Group-fire path already used; a Set in no overriding Group returns unchanged (identity, the common case). Wired at every existing choke point for free (all already call `resolve_for_fire`/`resolve_for_fire_mode_gated`/`resolve_ref` on whatever kind they're handed: `scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id`, `trigger_engine._default_select_color_set`, `POST /room-color/apply`, the editor's baseline endpoint) plus one fix: `room_preview.py`'s own `_resolve()` had a redundant `if card.kind != "group": return card` short-circuit predating this change that would have silently kept Preview on the old behaviour for a direct Set — removed, so ▶ Preview on a Set now shows its Group's override too, same as a real fire. **Multi-group precedence** (his real data still has exactly 4 sets under both First Group and Blues): chains every enclosing override-bearing Group rather than picking one — picking a single "owning" Group would invent the exact primary-owner concept the tiered list explicitly rejected ("a many-to-many index, not an invented single-owner tree"), and would contradict the "never one wins" precedent §76's likelihood-curve chaining already set for this identical "Set in >1 Group" shape. Order is deterministic — ascending by Group name (case-insensitive), tie-broken by id — so the alphabetically-last enclosing Group wins a field conflict; a Group with no override entries contributes nothing. Today's data renders identically under any tie-break (Blues carries no entries, so its 4 shared members' real overrides all come from First Group regardless of order) — the order only starts to matter the day he authors conflicting fields on two Groups sharing a member. Still a LIVE overlay only, never a destructive write, unchanged from the Group-fire path. **Re-measured the tiered-list rework's own "27 of 28" consequence figure against live data** (fetched over the network from the running `:8010`/`:8000` processes — this worktree's `storage/color_sets.json` and `storage/device_categories.json` are both gitignored and were stale copies from worktree creation, confirmed by size mismatch against the live GETs before trusting either): **still 27 of 28 (group, member) override pairs change rendered output, unchanged from the original measurement two days on.** The 28th (unchanged) pair is `Lines` → a member id (`2deeaa37-c86f-491b-9a4f-f96e53da34ec`) that no longer exists in his live `color_sets.json` at all — a stale membership reference, not a "no visible difference" case (already surfaced in the UI as "⚠ missing set" and unaffected by this change either way). Under the actually-shipped chained resolution, all 27 of the 27 real sets sitting in ≥1 override-bearing Group now render differently when fired directly. No case looks unrecognisable: checked field-by-field, no Group override entry ever sets `color_kind`/`color_value` on a scope a member's own entries already cover, so a Set's own authored foreground colour/gradient is never replaced — only brightness/background/`bg_mode` fields shift, and/or additional virtuals the Set never scoped gain colour from the Group (e.g. firing any of the three `Calm - *` sets directly now also lights the Singles category in the Group's shared `#ffd37d` fill, which none of the three Sets' own entries ever touched — the single most visible consequence of this change, worth watching for on the room). Tests: 7 new cases in `tests/test_color_set_groups.py` (direct-call broadening, no-enclosing-group passthrough, no-override-entries passthrough, reaching virtuals the Set doesn't cover, non-destructive storage, multi-group chaining with alphabetically-last-wins, multi-group non-conflicting fields both landing) — full suite 712 passed / 3 pre-existing environmental failures (identical on pristine master, unrelated to this change) / 2 skipped. **The list-nesting half of his ask was already fully built** by the tiered-list rework above (PR #117, merged) — nothing new needed. Confirmed still live and correctly rendering at 390×844 through the real proxy path (`:8000/spectra/colorsets`, byte-identical bundle whether fetched via the proxy or directly from `:8010`): collapsible Group headers, indented member rows, "also in: …" cross-references for the First Group/Blues overlap, the "⚠ missing set" stale-reference warning, and the "UNGROUPED" section all present and rendering with zero page-level horizontal overflow. |

Runtime/xcorr-timing side of Set Lists (`Setlist` model, `active_setlist_id`, `setlist_offsets` — a completely different field) is **not** retired, still fully live in both legacy and SPECTRA. **Where his data lives (untouched, not moved, not migrated):** `storage/profiles/*.json`, one file per song profile, field `setlist_triggers: dict[str, list[MusicTrigger]]` (`models/song_profile.py:77`, keyed by `Setlist.id`) — readable/restorable exactly as before, nothing deleted, nothing rewritten. **What was disabled (a flag flip away from restoring, not a rebuild):** `services/trigger_engine.py::TriggerEngine.SETLIST_SLOTS_ENABLED = False` (class attr right above `_user_triggers()`) — when `True`, that method resolves `state.active_setlist_id` against `self._profile.setlist_triggers` and returns the slot's list if the active Set List has one; with the flag `False` it always returns `self._profile.triggers` (Default), so the engine never fires slot timestamps. `routers/control.py::active_triggers()` (`GET /api/control/active-triggers`, the Now-Playing-marker mirror of the same logic) gates its own "setlist" source branch on the identical `engine.SETLIST_SLOTS_ENABLED` flag, kept in lockstep so the frontend never reports a source the engine isn't honouring. Authoring UI disabled the same way, in **both** copies (`web/src/builder/` — spot-effects — and its byte-identical SPECTRA port `spectra/web/src/timeline/`, since they read/write the same backend): each of `components/ModeBar.tsx` (the slot picker `<select>` + its sticky-slot restore effect), `components/ImportDialog.tsx` (slot entries in the "Copy from" list), and `components/ShiftAllControl.tsx` ("Commit ALL" writing shifted timestamps into `setlist_triggers`) carries its own local `const SETLIST_SLOTS_ENABLED = false;` gate — flip all of them (1 backend flag shared by `trigger_engine.py`+`control.py`, plus 3 frontend flags per tree × 2 trees = 7 flags total) back to enabled/`true` to restore in full; every gate is a boolean, no code was deleted, no migration or rebuilt UI is needed to bring it back. **Checked and confirmed nothing else depends on `setlist_triggers`**: the only other runtime users of `state.active_setlist_id` (`trigger_engine.py`'s xcorr-offset resolution at lines ~207/252, `auto_offset_service.py`, `capture_alignment.py`'s post-recapture Δ-shift migration, `websocket_manager.py`, `timing_viz_router.py`, `audio_shape_router.py`) all key off `AudioShapeMeta.setlist_offsets` — a different field entirely, xcorr timing not trigger content — and are untouched; `capture_alignment.py` deliberately still keeps `setlist_triggers` timestamps shifted in sync on a recapture (data hygiene, not the retired feature) so a future re-enable doesn't inherit stale-by-recapture data. Help text (`web/src/help/helpContent.ts`'s "Set Lists" entry) updated to describe the current state instead of the retired instructions. |

### §43–§81 — SPECTRA-native builds since the audit (2026-08-14 → present)

Not in the original 46; added because they exist now and this ledger is the live surface.

| § | Capability | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| §43 | Device preview strip (favourite devices, live) | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven against his real hardware — PR #85, #86, #96 (gap found), fm/spectra-preview-facade-source (fix), PR #115, fm/spectra-preview-smoothness (choppy-render fix, canvas paint) | **New SPECTRA-only build, not a legacy port** — confirmed absent from legacy by the audit's own dedicated search. Approved 2026-08-14, his own words: *"Great, move forward, but add a pause button to pause the preview and conserve resources."* Plan: `data/spectra-device-preview-plan/report.md` (+ a pause-requirement section appended 2026-08-15). PR #85 merged. Built exactly as planned: `spectra/services/device_preview.py` (one upstream WS client to LedFX's own `visualisation_update` feed, throttled to 8fps at the relay independent of LedFX's own config, favourites store with genuinely-driven-virtual auto-population capped at 4), `spectra/api/device_preview.py` (favorites GET/PUT, status, pause/resume, a dedicated `/device-preview/ws` fan-out — NOT the general `/api/ws` channel, so pixel frames never load pages that aren't looking at them), frontend `DevicePreviewStrip.tsx` + `FavoritesPicker.tsx` mounted in the shared `TopBarStrip.tsx` beside §50 (outside `<Routes>`, confirmed by direct navigation in a smoke check that the strip and its WS connection survive a route change without re-subscribing). **The pause requirement is the part that was checked hardest, per his own stated reason (conserve resources), not asserted**: `DevicePreviewRelay._consume`'s receive loop polls a cleared `_active_event` on a 0.2s timeout instead of blocking forever on `ws.recv()`, so pause breaks out of `async with websockets.connect(...)` — the block whose exit is what actually closes the socket — promptly; proven against a REAL fake-LedFX WebSocket server (not just a local flag) in `tests/test_device_preview.py::test_pause_actually_closes_the_upstream_socket_not_just_the_display`: the fake server's own live-connection count drops to zero on pause and `frames_received`/`frames_relayed` both freeze, not just the display. Re-proven live in a chrome-devtools-axi smoke check against an isolated instance (spare ports, fake local LedFX — his real room/8010/8888 untouched): `GET /api/device-preview/status` read `connected:false` immediately after clicking Pause and stayed there, frame count frozen, badge read "paused" with dark swatches (not stale colour). **One real bug found and fixed by that same smoke check, not by the tests alone**: a status push only fired from the explicit pause()/resume() API handlers, but the actual reconnect after resume() completes asynchronously — an already-open tab could sit on "reconnecting…" indefinitely despite the server being fully live again. Fixed by pushing a status broadcast on every `connected` transition (`DevicePreviewRelay._set_connected`), not just explicit calls; regression-proven in `test_status_change_broadcasts_on_every_connect_transition_not_just_explicit_pause_resume`. Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts` topic `device-preview`. **OQ-7 decided (2026-08-15) — built the report's own "yes" recommendation**: a hidden browser tab now auto-pauses the feed, auto-resuming with no click needed. Rejected the obvious shortcut of reusing pause()/resume() for this — those are relay-wide, so a hidden TAB pausing would have blacked out every OTHER open tab too. Instead `DevicePreviewRelay._wants_upstream()` gained a third, independent requirement — `has_viewers()`, wired to `preview_ws_manager.client_count() > 0` — so the upstream LedFX connection only stays open while at least one downstream `/device-preview/ws` client is connected; the frontend (`api/devicePreviewWs.ts`) closes its own socket on `visibilitychange`→hidden and reopens it on visible, calling `relay.viewers_changed()` from the API's connect/disconnect paths to re-evaluate demand. This composes correctly with multiple tabs for free (one visible tab keeps the feed alive for all of them) and never writes the persisted `paused` flag, satisfying his own tradeoff note verbatim. **Held to the same bar as the original pause**: `tests/test_device_preview.py::test_last_viewer_leaving_closes_the_upstream_socket_and_a_viewer_returning_reopens_it` proves the fake-LedFX server's own connection count drops to zero when the last viewer disconnects and comes back to one when a viewer returns — not a local flag. **Visibly distinct, both nameable**: the badge shows a fourth state, blue "idle — tab hidden" (`api/devicePreviewWs.ts`'s `tabHiddenPause`, local knowledge that THIS tab closed its own socket on purpose), never the gray "paused" his own sticky Pause button shows — driven independently, so a manual pause and an auto-pause can never be confused for each other. Re-proven live in a chrome-devtools-axi smoke check against a second isolated instance (spare ports 18010/18888, its own storage dir, fake local LedFX — his real room/8010/8888 untouched throughout): with a real tab-hidden signal fired at the page, `GET /api/device-preview/status` read `connected:false` and the fake LedFX server's own `active_connections` dropped from 1 to 0 within the same second, `frames_relayed` froze (424 → 424 → 424 across two 1s checks) while the badge read the distinct blue "idle — tab hidden" (`.badge-blue`) rather than the sticky pause's gray; firing the visible signal back reopened the upstream connection (`active_connections` 0→1, `frames_relayed` resumed climbing) and the badge returned to "live" with no click. Separately proved his own sticky Pause survives a hidden→visible cycle unclobbered — clicking Pause, then toggling tab-hidden and back, left the badge on gray "paused" throughout, never flipping to "idle." **Still unverified against his real browser/hardware** — every proof above ran against the offline fake-LedFX harness and a fake local instance; his room stayed off-limits for this task. **GAP FOUND 2026-08-16 room-proving against his real stack, then FIXED same day (`fm/spectra-preview-facade-source`)**: the relay above always assumed the external LedFX process was the writer. Wrong in his normal S3 operating state — `ledfx.service` is deliberately stopped once light ownership moves to `"spectra"` (his live default since the handover; the ownership reconciler alarms if it comes back), so the strip sat on "reconnecting…" forever against nothing (`AGENTS.md`'s device-preview section has the full incident history). **Fix — ownership-routed source, not always LedFX**: `device_preview._source_mode()` reads the SAME light-ownership record `fx_seam.py` routes writes by — `spot-effects` owns → the original LedFX-websocket relay (`_consume_ledfx`, unchanged, correct whenever LedFX really is the writer); `spectra` owns → `_consume_facade`, a direct in-process subscription to `fx.events.Event.VIRTUAL_UPDATE` on the live `spectra.services.live_host.live.host` — the SAME event the frame-freshness tap already listens for, the literal pixel buffer just flushed to the device, zero network hop, zero LedFX; anything else (handing-over/released/not-yet-active) → honestly `connected:false`, `source:"none"`. Same wire shape both paths (`_facade_frame_payload`), so the frontend decoder needed no change. **The three carry-forward requirements re-proven against the new source, not assumed to transfer**: (1) pause — `_consume_facade` removes the `Event.VIRTUAL_UPDATE` listener from the live host's own registry, proven in `tests/test_device_preview.py::test_facade_pause_removes_the_listener_not_just_a_local_flag` by firing a frame DIRECTLY at the real event bus while paused and showing it never reaches the relay (not just that `connected` reads false) — plus a live chrome-devtools-axi proof against an isolated instance (spare port 18010, a real `fx.headless` dummy render thread, his real :8000/:8010 never touched): clicking Pause froze `frames_relayed` at 58 and held there, swatch went to the dark placeholder, Resume reopened it and frames climbed again; (2) hidden-tab auto-pause — `test_facade_last_viewer_leaving_removes_the_listener_too` plus the same live proof: a dispatched `visibilitychange`→hidden froze frames at 96 with the badge reading the distinct blue "idle — tab hidden" (never touching the sticky `paused` flag), and toggling visible auto-resumed with no click, frames climbing to 106; (3) licence position — re-examined fresh, not inherited from the LedFX-protocol reasoning: `fx/` is already GPL-3.0-vendored and already imported throughout spectra/, so this is no new incorporation; `ledfx/core.py`'s own throttle/serialize logic was explicitly dropped from that vendoring, so it is NOT copied here — `_facade_frame_payload` is an independent implementation reusing this module's own pre-existing SpotFX-authored throttle bookkeeping instead (full reasoning: `device_preview.py`'s module docstring). Favourites resolve identically on both paths (`room_topology.genuinely_driven_virtual_ids()`, transport-independent virtual identity — confirmed, not assumed). Tests: `tests/test_device_preview.py` section 5 (5 new: source-mode resolution, real-frame relay with no websocket, the two genuine-stop proofs, status().source) against a real `fx.headless` render thread — 19/19 pass in this file, full suite 517 passed / 5 skipped / 5 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated (`test_crystal_activation_verify.py` ×3, `test_settings_agent_cli.py` ×2 — missing `mcp` package/activation-gap fixtures, same class already documented at §61/§60). **Still room-unproven against his actual hardware** — the isolated-instance proof above used a dummy device, never his real bulbs/strips; his room stayed off-limits for this task, per its own hard limits. **PHONE LAYOUT FIXED 2026-08-16, his own report after using it once (`fm/spectra-preview-phone-matrix`)**: "the preview stretches out in a line super far I don't see any Matrix for The Matrix previews... Also in collapse mode it still doesn't quite fit." Root cause established first, per his own instruction to say which: NOT a payload gap — `device_preview.py` has always emitted `shape: [rows, cols]` correctly for both the facade and LedFX paths (`_facade_frame_payload` reads `virtual.rows`; the LedFX relay passes `msg["shape"]` through unchanged) — the row count reached the frontend from day one. The defect was entirely in `DevicePreviewStrip.tsx`: expanded mode decoded every device to one flat row of fixed-3px pixel spans regardless of `frame.shape`, so his favourite `crystal-mapper` (a real 72×37 matrix, 2664 pixels, confirmed against his live config) rendered as one 2664-pixel-long line — both of his symptoms, one device. Fixed by reading `shape[0]` (rows): `rows > 1` renders `device-preview-matrix`, a CSS grid (`repeat(cols, 1fr)` columns via a `--cols`/`--rows` custom property, `aspect-ratio: cols / rows`, no JS measurement) that reads as a genuine grid at 100% of the available width; `rows === 1` renders `device-preview-pixel-strip` with `flex: 1` pixels sharing exactly 100% of the container width (not a fixed per-pixel width), so his 7/10/17-pixel strips read as clean lines without looking broken or stretched, and an arbitrarily long strip (e.g. `crystal`, 976px, not currently a favourite) would stay contained too. Expanded devices stack vertically (`device-preview-chips.expanded` switches flex row → column) — "expand" now grows the page downward, never sideways. Collapse-mode's separate complaint was `.device-preview-strip`'s `white-space: nowrap` with no `flex-wrap`, forcing the whole label+swatches+badge+3-button row onto one line that ran off a phone's right edge; now wraps. Verified phone-first, per his own correction that the prior build was evidently never checked at a real phone width with his real device list: a static harness reproducing his exact favourites (`crystal-mapper` 72×37, `hues` 17, `hue-lights` 10, `dining-hues` 7) rendered via chrome-devtools-axi (Playwright Chromium, headless) at 390×844 and 360×780 — `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` equalled `window.innerWidth` at both widths (no horizontal overflow) in both collapsed and expanded states; the expanded screenshot shows `crystal-mapper` as a legible 72-column grid and the three short devices as full-width lines. Not room-proven against his real live pixel colours (his `:8010` instance was read-only off-limits, no writes and no restart) — the harness proves the layout mechanism (shape-driven grid vs. line, containment, vertical growth) against his real dimensions, not live colour fidelity, which was never in question. **UI tightened 2026-08-16, his own words (`fm/spectra-topbar-tighten`)**: "it doesn't need to say the word preview I know what it is... we don't need a button for pause and resume and also an indicator for if it's paused or running or reconnecting. make the button the indicator and don't put text use icons." All subtractive, no behaviour change: the `device-preview-label` "Preview" span is gone; the separate `.badge` status text ("live"/"paused"/"idle — tab hidden"/"reconnecting…"/"unavailable") and the text Pause/Resume button collapse into ONE round icon-only `device-preview-status-btn` that both shows state and toggles it on click — same `togglePause()` call as before. His own manual pause vs. the automatic hidden-tab pause (the OQ-7 distinction above) was named a blocking requirement and stays fully distinguishable: colour AND glyph both differ per state (⏸ purple = live, ▶ gray = his own pause, ⏾ blue = automatic tab-hidden idle, ↻ amber = reconnecting/unavailable), with the full explanation moved into the button's `title`/`aria-label` rather than dropped. Verified with a static visual harness (same technique as the phone-matrix fix above — plain HTML reproducing the component's exact class names against the real compiled `tokens.css`, chrome-devtools-axi/Playwright Chromium at 390×844, his real `:8010` untouched): all four state/colour/icon combinations render distinctly, the accessibility tree carries a distinct `aria-label` per state (confirmed via `snapshot`), and `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth` (no horizontal overflow) at phone width. `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean. Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts` topic `device-preview` rewritten to match (no more "Preview" title, badge language replaced with the single-button description). **Choppy-preview fix (2026-08-17, PR #115, `fm/spectra-preview-smoothness`, his own report): "The preview mode in spectra is not anywhere near as smooth as ledfx version. ledfx was very good, and should be the standard. copy as much as makes sense."** Read the real LedFX source first, per standing instruction (`/home/javi/ledfx-src`): the analogue of this component is `frontend/src/components/PixelGraph/*.tsx` (not the audio visualiser `VISUALISER_CONTEXT.md` names) plus `ledfx/core.py`'s `setup_visualisation_events` and `ledfx/api/websocket.py`'s dual-path sender. LedFX ships FIVE preview render variants; the DOM-per-pixel one (`PixelGraphBase.tsx`, `variants: 'original'`) is kept only as a slower legacy fallback behind a settings toggle — `'canvas'` (`PixelGraphCanvas.tsx`: a raw WebSocket subscription callback writing straight to a canvas ref via `ctx.putImageData()`, no React state in the hot path, `imageSmoothingEnabled=false`) is the shipped default (`store/migrate.ts` migration 20 forces it). Backend-side, LedFX throttles per-vis_id to `visualisation_fps` (default 30, config range 1–60) AND caps total transmitted pixels at `visualisation_maxlen` (default **81**, aspect-preserving PIL bilinear resize, `utils.shape_to_fit_len`/`resize_pixels`) before whole-frame base64 encoding; its `WebsocketConnection._sender` uses a per-vis_id single-slot mailbox (latest frame always overwrites an unsent older one) so a slow client is never handed a backlog. **Measured all three layers before changing anything, against his real device shapes** (`crystal-mapper` 72×37 = 2664px, plus 17/10/7px strips) via an offline `fx.headless` render thread (never his real hardware): **SOURCE** — `Event.VIRTUAL_UPDATE` fires at ~62fps regardless of device size (crystal-mapper and the strips alike), nowhere near the bottleneck. **TRANSPORT** — `DevicePreviewRelay`'s facade throttle (`RELAY_TARGET_FPS=8`) relays at a measured 7.66fps, matching design intent for every device size; the one real disparity is payload bytes — crystal-mapper's full undownsampled frame serializes to **~29.4KB**, the strips to **185–296B** (100–150×), because `_facade_frame_payload` (unlike LedFX's backend) never caps total pixel count. **RENDER was the actual bottleneck**: a real-React harness reproducing `DevicePreviewStrip.tsx`'s exact JSX (one `<span>` per pixel, one shared `frames` state object re-rendering the WHOLE strip on ANY favourite's frame, not just the one that changed) measured reconciling the 2664-cell matrix at avg 6.5–8.7ms / p95 26–31ms / max 38–51ms on a idle desktop headless Chromium — and avg 43–47ms / p95 55–65ms / max **86–98ms** under a phone-class CPU-throttle proxy (chrome-devtools-axi `emulate --cpu 4`) — a third to most of the entire 125ms budget at 8fps, before any other page activity. The equivalent `canvas.putImageData()` write measured **0.04–1.2ms across both conditions** (30–1000× cheaper), and JSON.parse + `decodePixels()` of the full 29KB crystal-mapper payload cost <1ms even throttled — confirming payload size, unlike render cost, was never actually load-bearing. **Carried from LedFX**: the canvas-ref-and-`putImageData` render path exactly as `PixelGraphCanvas.tsx` does it (`DevicePreviewStrip.tsx` now paints imperatively via `canvasRefs`/`swatchRefs`, a frame never touches React state, `shapes` state is retained only to pick matrix-vs-strip CSS layout and is change-guarded so it doesn't itself trigger a per-frame re-render), plus `imageSmoothingEnabled=false` + CSS `image-rendering: pixelated` for the crisp per-LED block look. **Named incompatibility, deliberately NOT carried**: LedFX's `visualisation_maxlen≈81` downsample cap — measured decode cost proves it buys no smoothness once canvas removes the render bottleneck, and it would directly conflict with his own prior explicit ask (this same row, the phone-matrix fix: *"I don't see any Matrix for The Matrix previews"*) — capping crystal-mapper to ~81 points would turn Expand into a blur instead of his actual matrix shape, so `spectra/services/device_preview.py` still ships the full frame. Also NOT changed: `RELAY_TARGET_FPS` stays 8 — SOURCE/TRANSPORT measurements confirm 8fps was never the choppiness cause (a deliberate, already-documented resource-conservation choice, module docstring's "a glance preview doesn't need 30fps"), and LedFX's own per-vis_id mailbox-coalescing wasn't ported either since the relay's existing per-vis_id `_throttle_ok` gate already serializes frames before a broadcast task is even created — no observed pile-up to fix. **Live pipeline re-proven end to end** on an isolated instance (spare port, temp storage, `fx.headless` multi-virtual host built to his real shapes, never his live `:8010`/`:8000`): `canvas.width/height` read back as exactly `72×37` for crystal-mapper and `17×1`/`10×1`/`7×1` for the strips (native resolution, confirmed not downsampled); two `getImageData` samples 2s apart on the same pixel returned different real colours (genuine live animation, not a static placeholder); clicking Pause froze the canvas at the dark placeholder `rgb(40,40,40)` and the status button read "Paused, click to resume", Resume brought live colour back — the backend genuine-stop guarantee itself is unchanged and untouched by this fix, still covered by the existing `tests/test_device_preview.py` proofs. Tests: `tests/test_device_preview.py` 19/19 pass unchanged (backend untouched); broader spectra/fx-filtered run 100 passed, 1 pre-existing environmental failure unrelated to this change (`test_mid_group_scenes_animate_on_the_harness`, needs seeded live STAR scene data — documented pre-existing gap, not caused by this PR); `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean. **Still room-unproven against his actual browser/phone** — every proof above ran against an isolated dummy-device instance; his room stayed off-limits per this task's own hard limits. **Transport fix (2026-08-20, `data/preview-frame-rate-is-still-bad-over-rem-dhvp/`, his report: "the frame rate on the preview is still terrible. Remember, I'm always on a remote computer, but LEDFx previews were really good")**: this row's own "payload size was never actually load-bearing" finding just above was a LOCAL CPU-decode measurement (`JSON.parse`+`decodePixels()` timing) — never a measurement of bytes actually crossing a bandwidth-constrained link, which is his stated permanent condition and the one this report names directly. Re-examined the transport LedFX itself uses on the same link: `ledfx/config.py`'s `transmission_mode` defaults to `"compressed"` — base64 of interleaved r,g,b bytes (`ledfx/core.py::handle_visualisation_update`) — and the LedFX-relay path here forwards that unchanged; but `_facade_frame_payload` (his live default source since the S3 split, `spectra/services/device_preview.py`) had always emitted the OTHER shape, a JSON list of three per-channel lists ("uncompressed") — structurally fine (the frontend's `decodePixels()` already parses both, written to match LedFX's two modes) but ~3-4x heavier per byte of actual pixel data than base64. Fixed: `_facade_frame_payload` now base64-encodes, matching LedFX's own default exactly — same resolution, same shape, zero downsampling (the `visualisation_maxlen≈81` cap stays un-ported, same still-standing reason: his "I don't see any Matrix for The Matrix previews" ask a few paragraphs up). No frontend change needed. Measured, not asserted (`scripts/check_device_preview_remote_transport.py`): exact bytes/frame from the real serialization code — crystal-mapper 36,624B → 10,758B (3.40x smaller), hues-strip 340B → 169B (2.01x), hue-lights 244B → 141B (1.73x), dining-hues 202B → 128B (1.58x) — plus a real throttled-loopback-TCP-socket delivery trial (actual paced socket I/O, not arithmetic) at two representative constrained-link profiles: a modest link (2 Mbps/60ms latency) delivered crystal-mapper frames at 4.76fps old vs 9.59fps new (target 8fps); a poor link (768kbps/120ms) at 1.96fps old vs 4.27fps new. **Explicitly labeled a remote-EQUIVALENT proxy, not a field measurement** — his real connection's actual bandwidth/latency profile is unknown and his room/browser were never touched, per this task's own hard limits; the throttled-socket numbers show the same mechanism (fewer bytes deliver faster under a real constrained pipe) holds under genuine paced network I/O, not just byte-count arithmetic. `tests/test_device_preview.py::test_facade_relay_receives_real_frames_with_no_websocket` updated to assert the base64 shape instead of the retired list shape; the rest of the suite (pause/auto-pause/source-routing) is untouched since only the pixel encoding changed, not the relay's control flow. Not deployed — landed on its own branch, his live `spectra.service` untouched, per this task's own scope. |
| §44 | UPDATE trigger action + trigger migration completion | `BUILT` (full) | Day-one bar item (D2). PR #57 merged (`09d0992`), builds the Admiral's own definition of UPDATE — "a major change within the scene, bigger than a flare, overrides the drift, ramps in" (`data/spectra-trigger-migration-scoping/RULING.md`, quoted verbatim) — as `SceneV2.update_kind` + `ResponseEngine.on_update`, glide-not-jump on an 800–3000ms ramp. **Live-proven**: 10,698 authored + 1,179 generated = 11,877 triggers migrated on his real hardware; UPDATE proven as an 800ms glide landing on target params (`data/backlog.md` `spectra-trigger-migration-scoping`, done). Ran live **while the PR was still unmerged** — see [Landed vs. deployed](#landed-vs-deployed-a-real-gap-this-document-does-not-close). **Scene-id pool question (formerly OQ-1) is RESOLVED, not open**: his ruling was Option B, `scene_id=None` handed to the room's own chooser for all 3,546 pool-based triggers, accepted with open eyes that the chooser may pick a mismatched-mood scene since it doesn't know his hand-built pools — "if he hears it in his room, he will say so, and it is reversible" (`RULING.md` item 1, implemented in `spectra/services/legacy_trigger_migration.py`). Drop-scene triggers get intensity forced to MAX per the same ruling (`RULING.md` item 2). Dinner Party Scenes (the pool, not the §4 capability) dropped from the migration entirely at his word (`RULING.md` item 4) — do not conflate with §4 above, a different capability with its own separate keep-not-drop decision. **No longer inert (code-verified 2026-08-15)**: his room's `scene_change_mode` is now `"full"` — the note above describing `"analysed"`/inert was accurate when written and has since been overtaken by his own room setting, not by a code change. **Analysed-trigger backfill** (Admiral ask, `data/backlog.md` `spectra-analysed-trigger-import`, "not every song has authored triggers from spotfx, but they should all have analysed triggers that can be imported"; PR #75): the authored migration above only covers the 312 songs the legacy world had triggers for; `scripts/import_analysed_triggers.py --apply` ran `midsong_generator.generate_for_song` (front 3, §3) for every one of the other analyzable songs, back-filling 514 of them (16,345 generated triggers; 5 already had partial coverage from `maybe_auto_generate`'s reactive first-play auto-generation and correctly no-opped; 1 song has a single whole-track librosa section with no internal boundary to seed from — an analysis-quality gap, not a migration gap). Idempotency and authored-corpus integrity proven by re-running the script against the live store a second time (0 added/updated/deleted) and a byte-for-byte diff of every authored trigger against a pre-run backup (0 changed). Room-proven: an imported trigger fired at his real lights during this PR's own review (`JHAYCO, J Balvin, Bad Bunny - No Me Conoce - Remix`, previously zero-authored, repeated `fire_scene` fires observed via `GET /spectra/api/engine/status`'s `triggers.last_fire`). **13 more were caught and excluded, not silently counted** (firstmate review, same day): a handful of `storage/audio_shapes/*.json` sidecars — and their own legacy profile files — carry a synthetic `ledfx:<artist>:<title>` `spotify_uri` because the real Spotify id was never resolved at capture time (12 real Bad Bunny tracks; one, "ScriptTester - OneEventTest", is a 60-second test fixture, not a real song). `trigger_store` is keyed by the exact string the live bridge presents, which is always `spotify:track:...` (828/828 other analyzable songs, 317/317 pre-existing keys, zero exceptions) — a trigger written under the synthetic key can never fire. The script now filters to `REQUIRED_URI_PREFIX = "spotify:track:"` and asserts it again at the write call; the 523 already-written dead triggers were deleted from the live store (verified: zero non-`spotify:track:` keys remain). Current live total: 10,698 authored + 17,631 generated = 28,329 triggers across 831 songs. |
