| §51 | Ambient — confirmed vs. attempted light read-back (the ON path) | `BUILT`, success case only — see note | PR #69 merged (`36198b1`). Fixes a live defect distinct from §6: `Ambient ON: [...] held at #f5da8c, 17 light(s) set` logged identically whether all 17 bulbs actually carried the colour or 3 silently didn't (a 2xx bridge PUT only means the bridge accepted the write — zigbee can still drop it under a write burst). `ambient.reconcile()`'s ON path now reads every light back and only counts a **confirmed** match; stragglers get bounded, spaced retries plus paced initial writes; anything still not holding comes back named in `unconfirmed` (status `"partial"`), surfaced as a red badge on `RoomControlsBar.tsx` — never folded into a bigger count. **Success case PROVEN LIVE** on his room (commit `bb1b759`, deployed before the PR merged — see [Landed vs. deployed](#landed-vs-deployed-a-real-gap-this-document-does-not-close)): 17/17 confirmed at `#f5da8c` across both bridges, room verified back at his exact baseline after. **Failure case PROVEN HEADLESSLY ONLY, not live** — `tests/test_ambient.py::test_reconcile_on_silently_dropped_write_is_never_reported_as_held` contrives the identical failure shape and asserts the honest partial report, but no bulb inside `dining-hues`/`hue-lights` was actually unreachable at proof time to exercise this for real; if that's done later this row should be upgraded explicitly, not silently assumed. **Checked and confirmed clean, not assumed**: the release path (§49, `spectra/services/release.py`, already reads real state back via `_verify_released()`) and the scene-fire path (`spectra/services/fx_seam.py::apply_writes`, raises loud on HTTP failure rather than under-counting) do **not** share this attempted-vs-confirmed gap. |
| §52 | Ambient — music-precedence, i.e. mode "auto" (yields to playback, restores on its own) | `BUILT` (full) — room-proven, all three states | **Field renamed since this proof**: `RoomControlState.ambient_enabled` (bool) below became `ambient_mode: "off"\|"always"\|"auto"` in §53, the same day, once the Admiral corrected "music always wins" — what this row calls `ambient_enabled: true`/`"yielding"`/`"holding"` is exactly today's `ambient_mode: "auto"`; the proof narrative is left in its original words rather than silently rewritten. Fixes a live defect distinct from both §6 and §51: proven 2026-08-15 with `ambient_enabled: true` and a real track playing, `scene_change_mode: "full"`, an active scene, and triggers firing — **all 19 Hue bulbs sat frozen at ambient cream, following none of it**; turning Ambient off by hand released 17/19 to the music within seconds. Ambient didn't merely compete with music, it silently swallowed the whole song — a second, independent cause of his original "I don't see scene changes" complaint, on top of the `scene_change_mode` fix. His ruling: Ambient is the room's RESTING state, not one that outranks music — MUSIC WINS while it plays, and Ambient resumes **on its own** the instant it stops (the restore half of "I want the whole house to restore when a music sync ends", arriving from the other direction). New module `spectra/services/ambient_music_gate.py` is the single choke point every path that can change the live hold now funnels through — a human PUT (`room_controls.reconcile_ambient_if_changed`, also covers the settings-console agent), every bridge "state" broadcast (`engine.py`'s `_on_track_uri`), and process startup/resume (`app.py`) — so none of them can bypass the precedence rule by reaching `services.ambient.reconcile()` directly. `RoomControlState.ambient_enabled` still means exactly what it said ("I want Ambient"); the gate owns only the LIVE hold, driven by a second signal, `bridge.is_playing()`. Precedence rule: a CONFIRMED read always wins, even over an existing hold (playing releases a held room; not-playing re-engages one that wasn't held) — but an UNKNOWN read (bridge never sent a signal) never actively changes anything, it carries the current hold forward; collapsing unknown onto "must not hold" was an earlier, tempting shortcut, rejected because it would make a transient bridge blip flicker-release an already-quiet room, a regression against the ease-back bar below. Every actual write still goes through `services.ambient.reconcile()` unchanged — the same function a human toggling the checkbox always drove — so **§6's release/ease-back fidelity is untouched by construction, not merely re-tested**. Visibility (the fix's second half, his own words: "a control that reads as ON while doing nothing is the exact pattern this project has spent the week killing"): `ambient_music_gate.status()` reports `off`/`holding`/`yielding`/`transitioning`, folded into `GET /spectra/api/engine/status`'s `ambient` key (no new endpoint, existing 3s poll) and shown as a persistent badge on `RoomControlsBar.tsx`, separate from the one-shot PUT-outcome badge. **Offline-proven**: `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` (precedence rule including the no-flicker-on-unknown case, no redundant writes, colour-while-holding re-apply, failed-reconcile retry) and `tests/test_bridge.py` (`is_playing()`'s three-way read, survives a reconnect blip). **Room-proven, all three states, 2026-08-15, PR branch deployed live**: with his real music playing ("100 MILLONES"), `ambient_enabled` flipped `false → true` via a full PUT — `ambient_result.status` came back `"yielding"` with **zero Hue REST calls fired** (no freeze attempted at all); `GET /engine/status` read `ambient: {enabled: true, mode: "yielding", held: false}` throughout; both his Hue entertainment sessions stayed **active** on both bridges the whole time, the `hues` virtual kept flushing fresh frames with genuinely changing colour (`#800080` → `#0500ff`), and `trigger_fired`/`responses` counts climbed 17→34 — the exact scenario that sat frozen at cream that morning, now confirmed following the music instead (**state 1**). `ambient.mode` correctly read `"yielding"` at every check — proven at the API field `RoomControlsBar.tsx` renders, not visually confirmed in his own browser (**state 3**). Room restored to the exact baseline read before touching anything, verified by read-back — twice, once after state 1 and again after state 2. **State 2 — Ambient returning by itself with ease-back intact — PROVEN LIVE**, captured on a genuine pause he took at his own discretion (never forced; two prior windows were let pass rather than interrupt his listening — see the conversation record). Continuously sampled both Hue bridges' `/clip/v2/resource/light` and `GET /engine/status` at ~1.2s cadence throughout (script + full JSONL trace in the PR). Observed, not asserted: `is_playing` flipped `true→false` at T+0; `ambient.mode` went `yielding→transitioning`, landing `holding` at T+11.4s with `result: {status: "on", devices: ["dining-hues","hue-lights"], lights_set: 17, lights_total: 17}` — all 17 confirmed at exactly `xy=(0.4191,0.4097)`, the computed target for `#f5da8c`. He resumed at T+20.1s (`is_playing` back to `true`); release then GLIDED, not snapped, over five distinct sampled points on `Kitchen Infuse` (representative of the group): held `bri=100.0, xy=(0.4191,0.4097)` → phase-1 dim-fade lands `bri=35.18`, same `xy` (unchanged colour, brightness-only fade) → phase-2 catch-up begins, `bri=24.11, xy=(0.4399,0.5202)` → `bri=32.02, xy=(0.4291,0.4325)`, holding there through several more samples → `mode` reads `yielding` again at T+36.4s (16.4s wall-clock for the full release, longer than the 9.5s AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS+AMBIENT_CATCHUP_MS constants alone predict, consistent with two devices processed with real bridge round-trips rather than a single instantaneous step), `result: {status: "off", devices: ["dining-hues","hue-lights"]}`. Room resumed normal driving immediately after (`hues` virtual writes changing colour again, `trigger_fired`/`responses` counts climbing, both entertainment sessions still `active` throughout — never torn down, matching design). One live-instrument note worth keeping: reading `/clip/v2/resource/light` directly during active entertainment streaming shows **zero colour movement even while bulbs are being actively driven** — the CLIP light resource doesn't reflect the streamed frame while a Hue entertainment session owns the bulb, so it looked like a fresh regression until cross-checked against the entertainment-session-active + executor-writes + `ambient.mode` evidence above; don't use a raw CLIP light GET as the freeze/follow signal during a live entertainment stream, use the session-active + executor-write + `ambient.mode` triad instead.  **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-30 by §96**: `ambient_mode` is retired for a plain `ambient_enabled` bool plus `ambient_on_music_pause`; THIS behaviour is the latter, preserved verbatim in code and shipped OFF by his own word. |
| §53 | Ambient — "On during music" (mode 2, his own requested third setting) | `BUILT` (full) — PR #76 merged, room-proven live | The Admiral corrected §52's framing the same day: "music always wins" was wrong. His words: "Ambient mode should run even while the music is playing so that my Hue Lights are lit and bright but the other lights are still running the show... it needs to be a third setting where ambient mode can still come into play when the music is still playing." `RoomControlState.ambient_mode` is now `"off"` / `"always"` / `"auto"` — his three settings, in his language, surfaced as a `RoomControlsBar.tsx` dropdown ("Off" / "On during music" / "Auto-return") rather than the old checkbox. `"always"` is mode 2: `ambient_music_gate._desired_hold()` returns `True` unconditionally, never consulting `bridge.is_playing()` at all — not the precedence bug reintroduced, a deliberate choice he asked for. **The composition question he and firstmate asked, settled from evidence before any building, not inferred**: does holding Hue starve or double-penalise the show on other devices via the selection kernel (curve × genre × affinity)? `grep ambient` across `selection_kernel.py`, `scene_sequencer.py`, `sequencer.py`, and `trigger_engine.py` returns zero functional references (one docstring mention only) — scene selection is architecturally unaware Ambient exists. The only "ambient" the engine's own deferral logic reads (`bridge.conductor_deferral()`) is spot-effects' separate LEGACY `ambient_mode_enabled` broadcast flag, not this field. SPECTRA's Ambient hold operates exclusively through `fx/devices/hue.py`'s device-level `set_frozen()` — the LAST step in the pipeline, strictly downstream of everything selection/rendering computes — so mode 2 composes for free: holding Hue can never starve or double-penalise the show elsewhere, by construction. One-way migration (`room_controls.load_room_controls`): a stored `ambient_enabled: true` maps to `"auto"` (what `true` was built and proven to mean throughout §52's own lifetime), `false` to `"off"`. `reconcile_ambient_if_changed`'s changed-check and `settings_console.py`'s registry (`ambient_mode`, kind `"enum"`, choices read live off the model same as every other enum field) both updated to match. **Offline-proven**: `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` (new "always" section — holds while confirmed playing, ignores every playback transition once held, only switching the setting itself releases it) plus every §52 "auto" test re-passing unchanged under the new field name; `tests/test_room_controls.py`'s new migration test. **Not yet room-proven**: built under the Admiral's own explicit order to build-and-unit-test first and wait for a room window — his room was mid-listening throughout this build, so no live Hue call has been made for mode 2 yet. The §52 ease-back measurement (two-phase glide, colour-then-brightness, ~16s wall-clock) is the regression baseline any mode-2 room proof must re-confirm survived, not assume. | **Room-proven live 2026-08-15**, same session, his real music playing throughout ("TELEFONO NUEVO" -> scene 'Orbits V2'), continuously sampled via both Hue bridges + `GET /engine/status`'s per-virtual `executor.recent_writes` (never the CLIP light resource for the streamed half, per the AGENTS.md correction). **Measurement 1, the open question, answered positively**: with `ambient_mode: "always"` holding both Hue devices (17/17 confirmed at the target colour, same as always), the drift conductor's own ~20s leg cadence (`leg_s: 20.0`) kept firing on schedule for every NON-Hue virtual (`crystal-mapper`, `radial-dummy`, `single-color-effect`, `tv-mapper` -- covering `tv-backlight`, `porch-rail`, `crystal`, the kitchen sconces, `dining-table`) across **two consecutive full cycles** (fresh writes at both leg boundaries, 19.5s apart, matching `leg_s` almost exactly) while Hue sat held the whole time -- the show never stopped or throttled elsewhere. Mode 2 is a **small gating change**, not a real gap: firstmate's own earlier claim that Ambient suppressed his music entirely was Hue-only evidence and is now corrected -- non-Hue devices were never touched by the old always-hold behaviour either. **Measurement 2, the ease-back regression check: PASSED, not flattened**. Switching `"always" -> "off"` while still playing (the same code path a natural "auto" release takes) reproduced the identical two-phase shape: brightness-only dim fade to the same landing (`bri` 100.0 -> 35.18, `xy` unchanged) held ~3-4s, then a colour+brightness catch-up glide through two more distinct sampled points before fully releasing -- **~16.9s wall-clock** (mode "off" first observed to `held: false`), matching the ~16.4s §52 baseline within measurement noise. **One new, correctly-interpreted observation from a hold this long** (previous holds were brief; "always" has no natural end): both bridges' `entertainment_configuration.status` flipped "active" -> "inactive" roughly 63s into the hold, then back to "active" once the release's REST writes resumed. This is the Hue bridge's OWN streaming-channel idle/heartbeat behaviour -- expected once a channel has carried zero DTLS frames for a while, which is exactly what freezing causes -- **not** a sign anything broke: the held light's actual `xy`/`bri` never moved a fraction through the flip, SPECTRA's own `held`/`mode` bookkeeping never lost track, and the release that followed still glided cleanly. Room restored to the exact baseline read before touching anything (`ambient_mode: "off"`), verified by read-back, his music undisturbed throughout the whole window.  **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-30 by §96**: this mode IS the new `ambient_enabled = True` — the unconditional hold survives unchanged, it is just no longer one of three choices. |
| §54 | Curve chooser grid — scene sequencing tab | `BUILT` (PR #77 merged, UI eye-checked, not room-proven) | His ask, verbatim: "when choosing a curve i prefer to see a grid that shows a snapshot of each curve" — today's picker made him choose a shape blind (name only) and find out after. `spectra/web/src/scenes/tabs/SequencingTab.tsx`'s per-scene curve `<select>` (named profile / flat / inline / not-sequenced) is replaced with a tile grid; every tile is a `CurveThumbnail` (new, `spectra/web/src/components/CurveThumbnail.tsx`) drawing that curve's OWN points through the identical px/py/clamped-flat-extension path math `CurveEditor.tsx` already uses for the full graph below — a thumbnail is a scaled-down render of the real shape, not a second interpretation that could visually disagree with it. Covers the one live chooser surface he actually uses today (this tab, per-scene attachment — the sequencer's own curve, PR #64's scene-local/shared-profile split folds straight in: named profiles get their own tile, "Inline one-off…" previews what it would start from). **Deliberately left out, and why**: the legacy spot-effects `/app/` twin (`SequencerPanel.tsx`/`CurveProfilesCard.tsx`) is frozen pending SPECTRA superseding it, per this file's own standing rule — not touched. "Flare selection" (`ScenesPage.tsx`'s flare-band `curve` select) is a fixed 4-item easing-type ENUM (linear/ease_in/ease_out/pulse), not a named profile pulled from a growable list — a different kind of curve from `CurveProfile.points`, and SPECTRA's own `ResponseTab.tsx` doesn't even expose it (chip toggles for named `FlareKind`s only) — nothing to convert. `SequencerConfig.flare_entries`/`color_set_entries`/`wheel_travel_curve` and the Drift tab's follow-curve attachment have zero chooser UI at all (agent-only via `PUT`, "by telling the agent" — see this file's Drift/sequencer sections) — no surface exists there to touch. Scale: his live library is **11 curve profiles** (`storage/spectra/sequencer.json`, counted directly, not guessed) — plain SVG per tile is trivially cheap at that count; no virtualization/canvas needed, noted rather than silently assumed cheap. Old SpotFX **did** already draw real curve shapes once a profile was selected (`CurveEditor.tsx`, same component both apps share, shipped together in PR #19/#21) — this PR's fidelity target was matching that felt experience at the CHOOSING step, which neither app had before now: SPECTRA is free here (D1), and took it. **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` clean; eye-checked live on an isolated `python -m spectra` instance on a spare port (`SPECTRA_PORT=18010`, fabricated fixture storage — never his live `storage/spectra/`, never port 8010) with a bogus `SPOTFX_PORT` so the bridge never reached his real spot-effects process (confirmed "bridge ○ down" in the UI) — screenshotted 8 visually distinct tiles (flat/ramp-up/ramp-down/hump/step/plateau/wheel-travel/not-sequenced), clicked "Mid-song hump", confirmed the full `CurveEditor` below rendered the identical triangle and the attach round-tripped ("Curve attachment saved"). | §55 | Sonic — scene/flare authority + Scenes-page chat popover | `BUILT`, unverified live | His own words: "I want the Sonic agent in the settings console to be able to manage things like the flares and the settings within the scenes and creating scenes etc. That means it needs to be accessible from the scenes page." Widens §45's settings-console mechanism (deliberately, structurally — his own test stands: "a settings agent that can restart a service is not a settings agent") rather than replacing it: `spectra/services/scene_console.py` is a SECOND mechanism module, zero import of `settings_console.py`/`room_controls.py`, whose only write surface is `scene_store.save()`. `spectra/services/settings_agent.py` merges both domains' operation dicts into one `ALL_OPERATIONS` allowlist Sonic dispatches against — same exhaustive name→handler lookup as before, just wider. **The architecture is his own follow-up ruling, applied as the load-bearing design choice, not an afterthought**: "the enumerated operation set that ENFORCES the boundary and the catalogue that lets Sonic DISCOVER what to change must be THE SAME DECLARATION" — `spectra/services/sonic_ops.py`'s `SonicOperation` is that one declaration (name/domain/kind/summary/instructions/input_schema/handler), serving the allowlist, the Anthropic tool schema, AND the `list_operations` discovery meta-tool from the identical object, so a capability's guard and its documentation cannot drift apart, and per-operation how-to lives with the operation instead of accumulating in `SYSTEM_PROMPT`. Reads are filtered/narrow throughout (his token-efficiency ask, "a library that keeps growing... will outgrow anything that works by loading everything"): `list_scenes` returns id/name/labels only, `get_scene_settings`/`list_flare_kinds` return one scene's enumerated summary, `get_flare_kind` returns full detail for exactly one named kind — nothing ever dumps a scene's full `devices` list. Scope, by deliberate exclusion (same posture as §45's `force_scene_*`): eight scalar SCENE_SETTINGS_REGISTRY keys at the time (entry blend, charge/lull phase ramps, choreography timing, colour-journey pace, colour-set acceptance — bounds READ off `SceneV2`/`PhaseBlend`/`PhaseChoreography`/`SceneColorJourney`'s own `Field(ge=,le=)`, never re-typed; charge/lull phase ramps REMOVED 2026-08-20 along with `PhaseBlend` itself — see §11 — leaving six), named `FlareKind` create/update/remove (upsert by name, full-model re-validate so removing a kind a band still references is refused), and `create_scene` (name + labels only, no device/effect authoring — that stays the Initial Set tab's own visual editor). **The structural property that protects his 9 real, authored scenes**: `create_scene` only ever constructs a fresh `SceneV2(name=...)` — id is the model's own `default_factory=uuid4`, so a created scene can never collide with, and therefore never overwrite, an existing one, even given an identical name — and there is no delete/overwrite-by-id operation in the enumerated set at all. Proven, not merely designed: `tests/test_scene_console.py::test_scene_operations_never_touch_a_scene_they_were_not_targeted_at` seeds all 9 real scene names, exercises every write op against a different, freshly-created scene, and asserts every one of the 9 scenes' stored JSON bytes are byte-identical before and after. **The four adversarial refusal categories the widening was told to re-prove (not inherit) against the new surface, each with the server's OWN quoted text**: an operation outside the enumerated set (`{"status": "rejected", "reason": "no such operation: 'delete_scene'"}`), a valid operation with an out-of-range argument (`entry_ramp_ms=999999` → `"999999 is not a legal value for 'entry_ramp_ms'"`, `pydantic_errors: ["Input should be less than or equal to 20000"]`), an attempt to reach a non-scene setting through a scene op and the mirror (a scene key through `set_setting`) — both rejected with `"... is not a {settings-console setting|scene setting}"` — and shell/service-restart/http/file attempts, all falling through the same `"no such operation"` branch untouched. **The fabrication hunt, run against BOTH backends**: the API backend's `run_turn()` was already structurally immune (its `changes` list is built from `_dispatch()`'s own return value, never model prose) and `tests/test_scene_console.py` adds a mocked-client test proving it explicitly for a scene op — a fake model response claiming "I created the scene and set its entry ramp" while the only real tool_result was a rejection (unknown scene id) leaves `changes == []` and no file on disk. The CLI (subscription) backend's `_verify_tool_manifest()` — the mechanism that already caught real fabrication on `claude-haiku-4-5` in §45's own build — is RE-PROVEN against the widened `TOOL_NAMES` (2 → 11) with hand-built, explicitly-labelled-synthetic transcripts (`tests/fixtures/cli_transcript_synthetic_scene_*.json`, no live `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` exists tonight to capture real ones): an unavailable-tool case (empty manifest, model fabricates "I created a new scene called 'Sunset Drift'...") refuses the whole turn before any text is trusted, exactly as loudly as the original two-tool case did; the four PRE-EXISTING real captures from §45's build now correctly refuse too, as a stale/narrower-than-expected manifest — the widening's own regression proof that the check wasn't loosened to keep old fixtures green. Frontend: `spectra/web/src/components/SonicChatPopover.tsx`, a floating 💬 button + panel mounted on `ScenesPage.tsx` (his literal ask: "some kind of pop-up button"), talking to the exact same `POST /settings-console/message` endpoint the Settings page's embedded chat uses — one backend, two chat surfaces. Tests: `tests/test_scene_console.py` (23 tests), `tests/test_settings_console.py`/`tests/test_settings_agent_cli.py` extended for the merged boundary, `scripts/check_settings_console.py` extended with a scene-domain section — full suite 365 passed, 2 skipped (both live-model smoke tests, no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in this environment), 1 pre-existing environmental failure unrelated to this change (`test_mid_group_scenes_animate_on_the_harness` needs his real seeded `storage/spectra/scenes.json`, absent in this disposable worktree — confirmed failing identically on pristine `master`). **PROVEN HEADLESSLY ONLY, exactly like §45 itself still is** — no live Anthropic API call, no live `claude -p` subprocess, and no room window tonight (built and reviewed overnight, his room and his voice unavailable by the task's own constraint): the UI itself (the popover opening, the request round-tripping to a real running SPECTRA process and returning an honest 503 with no key configured) was verified against a real browser and a real isolated dev instance (`SPECTRA_STORAGE_DIR` repointed to a disposable temp dir, `SPECTRA_PORT` on a spare port, never his live 8010 or `storage/spectra/`) — but Sonic actually managing a flare or creating a scene from a spoken request, in his room, has not been watched by anyone. |
| §56 | STAR — edge count fixed: no flare moves it, initial roll 3–6 held for the run (Admiral order, resolves former OQ-6) | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven | His words, put verbatim to the live settings console first (§45 verification) and correctly refused there — flares/effect params are out of its five-setting scope — then raised and decided as its own change. First sentence: **"I do not want any of the flares in the scene star to change the number of edges."** Investigation found two mechanisms, both tracing to prior authoring in his stored scene, not incidental bugs: the Matrix device's `edges` param was a `signal="random"` binding (dice-correlated with `star`), re-rolled by the "Dice Re-roll" kind on every ordinary flare; and `"Flare patch 0.7–1"`/`"Drop patch 0.7–1"` each explicitly patched `edges` to `6.0`. Filed as OQ-6 rather than silently picked, per the brief's own instruction: authored reliance means stop and report, not decide. Firstmate relayed a recommendation of the narrower option (stop only the Dice Re-roll, keep the two patches) plus the full option set; **he first chose the wider option — freeze `edges` at a static `6`** — then, on reflection, gave a **second, superseding ruling**: **"i will make it very simple just delete whatever portion of any Flair was changing edges. but initial can be 3 to 6."** Mechanism for "3 to 6" (firstmate's own reading, not inferred, since nothing else pins down uniform-vs-weighted): every integer 3/4/5/6, uniform 25% each, independent of `star`'s own roll. Built as: (1) `spectra/models/binding.py` grows `ValueBinding.sticky: bool = False` — a signal="random" binding still resolves fresh at fire time (`scene_compiler.resolve_scene` is unaffected), but `scene_response.py::_reroll` (the "Dice Re-roll" kind's mechanism) now skips any binding with `sticky=True`; default `False` leaves every other binding/scene untouched. (2) STAR's `edges` binding becomes 4 equal steps (0.0/0.25/0.5/0.75 → 3/4/5/6), `dice: null` (no correlation with `star` — nothing asked for that), `sticky: true`. (3) Both patches' `edges` key removed, `spin`/`star` untouched. `scripts/freeze_star_edges.py` (`--apply`/`--restore`, dry-run-by-default, idempotent, house style matching `seed_star_strips.py`) is the migration; `docs/spectra-star-edges-freeze.md` is the durable, git-tracked record of the exact retired values (what's deployed today, not the deeper legacy state — see below) and the one-command restore. `edges` appears in no other scene, checked. **Provenance found along the way, recorded but NOT load-bearing for the 3–6 value**: his *legacy* STAR authored `edges` as a bare static `6` — no binding, no steps, neither patch kind exists in his legacy data at all. The dice binding and both patches were introduced during the SPECTRA rebuild by an agent, never asked for; he was told this and chose 3–6 anyway, which stands regardless. General rule this is evidence for, recorded in `AGENTS.md` ("A scene's stored data is not proof he authored it") per his explicit order, for the next crew to find rather than rediscover. `scripts/check_spectra.py`'s former "dice variants land ONLY as the three authored pairs" assertion (asserting the OLD behaviour as correct) is now split in two: the raw S1-seeder-output check still shows the pre-fix pairs (that fixture is legacy-shaped, unmigrated, by design), and a new in-memory section (never mutating the shared fixture — the `seed_star_strips.py` precedent) proves idempotency, exact restore-inversion, a 4000-draw uniformity proof over 3/4/5/6, and — the actual guarantee, not just the data shape — a full response-engine proof firing flare at 0.1/0.5/0.97 and drop at 0.97 against the fixed scene, confirming `edges` never appears in any executor write while `star` still does (the Dice Re-roll mechanism itself is unchanged, only `edges` is excluded). **PROVEN LIVE, 2026-08-16 (`docs/spectra-star-edges-freeze.md:42-70`) — this row was stale until now.** PR #82 first caught that #79's *code* had merged but the *data migration* (`scripts/freeze_star_edges.py --apply`) had not run against his live `storage/spectra/scenes.json` — a live-fired proof still showed the old binding active (edges 6→5 only, via Dice Re-roll, no 4 ever drawn). The Admiral backed up the pre-migration file and ran the migration himself. Re-proof against his real room the same night: 8 real flare/drop fires at intensity 0.9 — `edges` never appears in any response; 20 dry-run rolls hit all of 3/4/5/6, including 4 (never observed pre-migration). **Both halves of the rule — no flare moves it, and the initial roll is genuinely uniform 3–6 — are now proven live, not just built.** See §63 for a second STAR-adjacent live proof (accent colour) found and closed the same investigation. |
| §57 | Sonic — overwrite/backup/undo/preview/restore authority | `BUILT`, deploy HELD pending a real-model proof | His own follow-up, the same night, verbatim: "I want Sonic to be able to edit scenes and overwrite them just back them up ahead of time and make an easy to undo last agent change button. Scene changes can be followed by a preview and a check in to make sure it is what I wanted. and if it is not just restore the backup for that scene if we can not work it out." Four requirements, all structural, none of them the agent's to skip — his own framing, honoured literally: **(1) backup before any edit, VERIFIED not merely attempted.** `scene_console._write_and_verify_backup()` runs before every write that touches an EXISTING scene (`set_scene_setting`, `set_flare_kind`, `remove_flare_kind`, the new `overwrite_scene`, `restore_scene_backup`) — writes the pre-edit snapshot, then RE-READS THE FILE FROM DISK and compares; a successful `os.replace()` alone is never trusted, and a mismatch raises before `scene_store.save()` is ever called. Proven by making the write silently no-op (no exception, no evidence) and watching the edit refuse — twice, once for the ring, once for the once-only genesis write. **(2) undo-last-agent-change, one action, proven to actually restore.** `undo_last_scene_change()` takes no scene_id — it finds the most recent not-yet-undone log entry carrying a `backup_id` (global across scenes, matching the settings console's own existing "Undo last" UX) and restores it; proven against `scene_store` directly (never the undo call's own return value), including "undo of an undo" round-tripping correctly because restoring is ITSELF a backed-up edit. **(3) preview/check-in reads stored data, never the agent's account.** Every edit's `preview` field and the standalone `get_scene_preview(scene_id)` are both `_diff_scenes()` — a field-level comparison of two REAL SceneV2 snapshots (the backup vs. the saved scene) — proven to stay correct even when a mocked model's reply text claims a completely different, fabricated change. **(4) restore the backup when it's not right.** `restore_scene_backup(scene_id, backup_id)` — `backup_id` may be any ring entry or the literal `"genesis"`. His own two follow-up decisions, not requested but made and stated here as instructed: **retention is the last 10 edits per scene** (`SCENE_BACKUP_RING_SIZE`), oldest evicted first, specifically so an undo of an undo works and a second bad edit can't clobber the one good backup; **PLUS a permanent, never-pruned genesis snapshot per scene**, captured once on that scene's first-ever edit (or at creation, for a scene Sonic itself made) and immune to ring eviction — proven live against a stand-in for his real 'STAR' scene: three edits deep (including a wholesale `overwrite_scene`), `restore_scene_backup(id, "genesis")` reproduces the scene's exact original bytes. `overwrite_scene` itself is the one new genuinely destructive operation — wholesale-replaces name/labels/settings/flare_kinds in one shot (still never `devices`, that boundary is unchanged, not reversed by this ask) — and is subject to the identical backup gate as every other existing-scene write, no special case. Storage: `storage/spectra/scene_backups.json` (bounded ring), `storage/spectra/scene_genesis.json` (permanent), both atomic tmp+replace + read-back verified. Discovery: `overwrite_scene`/`list_scene_backups`/`get_scene_preview`/`restore_scene_backup`/`undo_last_scene_change` are five more `SonicOperation` entries in the same one-declaration allowlist §55 established — nothing new invented for this widening, the mechanism just grew. **The adversarial set the deploy hold named explicitly, all proven headlessly**: an overwrite attempted while the backup mechanism fails REFUSES (`tests/test_scene_console.py::test_dispatch_overwrite_scene_with_failed_backup_refuses_via_the_real_model_shaped_path`, run through `settings_agent._dispatch()`, the same entry point a real model's tool_use reaches); a fabricated "I restored that for you" is caught against stored data at both the mechanism layer (`test_preview_is_never_swayed_by_a_fabricated_model_reply`) and the CLI transcript-parsing layer (`cli_transcript_synthetic_restore_hallucinated_prose.json`, correct 16-tool manifest, zero real tool calls, `changes == []`); undo is proven by actually breaking and restoring a scene, verified against `scene_store`, not assumed from the call succeeding. The CLI backend's tool-manifest fabrication guard is re-proven a second time against this second widening (11 → 16 tools) with three more explicitly-labelled synthetic fixtures — including the SAME unavailable-tool shape that caught real fabrication on the original two-tool surface, now against `overwrite_scene`. Tests: `tests/test_scene_console.py` section 9 (28 new tests, 51 total in that file), `tests/test_settings_agent_cli.py` section 5c (3 new synthetic fixtures + the second stale-manifest regression proof) — full suite 392 passed, 2 skipped (live-model smoke tests, no key/token here), 1 pre-existing environmental failure unrelated (confirmed identical on pristine `master`, see §55). Frontend: `overwrite_scene`'s `preview` field renders as its own visually-distinct chat message (`.settings-console-msg-preview`, monospace/dashed border, never blended with Sonic's own prose) in BOTH `SonicChatPopover.tsx` and `SettingsConsolePage.tsx` — a real read of the saved scene, not a narration, rendered wherever he might see it. **DEPLOY HELD, DELIBERATELY, BY THE CAPTAIN'S OWN ORDER** — since **partially cleared, not fully**: "the refusal proofs ran WITHOUT a live model, and the widened surface is exactly where a real model's behaviour matters most... a boundary proven only against tests is not yet proven against the thing that will actually drive it." **PR #89 (merged 2026-08-16) ran the adversarial set against the real deployed model** (the CLI/subscription backend, live `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`) and found a genuine production defect along the way, not a clean pass: `spectra/services/settings_mcp_server.py`'s `_call(name, **kwargs)` collided with any Sonic tool whose own argument is also named `name` — breaking 6 of the widened tools (`create_scene`, `get_flare_kind`, `set_flare_kind`, `remove_flare_kind`, `overwrite_scene`, `list_operations`) with a TypeError on every real call. The live model reported every failure honestly and never fabricated success — itself a data point in favour of the fabrication-guard design — but the defect meant most of §57's own destructive/undo/backup surface could not have been reliably exercised live before the fix. Fixed by making `_call`'s `name` argument positional-only; a new regression test spawns the real MCP subprocess and calls all six. **Not yet deployed** (fix is merged to `master`, not restarted onto `spectra.service` — deploy is explicitly firstmate's/the captain's step, not this PR's). **What remains open**: no report in `data/` or `data/backlog.md`'s still-open `spectra-morning-deploy-batch` item confirms the REST of the required checklist — an out-of-set op, a bad-argument op, a non-scene-setting reach (both directions), a shell/restart attempt, the unavailable-tool fabrication case, and `create_scene`'s fresh-id guarantee — was re-run against the real model after the MCP fix landed. Treat this row as: **proof partially run live, one real defect found and fixed, deploy plus the remaining checklist items both still outstanding** — not "deploy held, nothing tried" and not "cleared." |
| §58 | Ambient — status honesty: `held`/`mode` gated on a real confirmation, not a replayed write outcome | `BUILT`, room-unproven | Found live 2026-08-15, overnight: `GET /api/engine/status` reported `ambient: {"setting": "always", "mode": "holding", "held": true, "result": {"status": "on", "lights_set": 17, "lights_total": 17}}` while all 36 of his bulbs were physically OFF (he'd switched them off before bed) — a control reading as actively holding a dark room. Root cause, not a guess: `ambient_music_gate.py`'s `_apply()` short-circuits a repeated identical `desired` (deliberate — "no redundant Hue writes"), so under `"always"` mode, once genuinely held, NOTHING ever re-touches the bridge again; `status()` was replaying the last write's read-back forever with no re-check and no notion of its own age. **The sharp point the fix is built around**: PR #69's own read-back (§51/§52) is honest work — it does check `on`+colour+brightness together (`_state_matches`) — but a confirmation proves the moment it was taken, not the moment it's read back hours later; a control that never re-checks turns an honest point-in-time proof into a dishonest standing claim. Two changes, both additive, neither touching the write path's own mechanics: (1) `spectra/services/ambient.py` grows `verify_held(color)` — GET-only, **never a PUT, ever** (the module's own docstring states this as a hard property, matching the deliberate choice not to fight a bulb he turned off himself) — reusing the exact `_resolve_lights_named` cache and `_state_matches` check the write path already trusts, so "lit" means the identical thing whether confirmed by a write or by this independent recheck. (2) `spectra/services/ambient_music_gate.py` gets its own periodic task, `run_supervised()` (`VERIFY_TICK_S=30`, started alongside `frame_watchdog`/`ownership_reconciler` in `spectra/app.py`'s lifespan — same crash-retry discipline), calling `verify_now()` on a clock independent of bridge broadcasts (the overnight scenario is exactly the case where those may not arrive). A NEW pair of module globals, `_verified_ok`/`_last_verified_ms`/`_last_verify`, is kept deliberately apart from the pre-existing `_held`/`_held_color` (the write-INTENT bookkeeping `_apply()`'s short-circuit still needs, untouched): `status()`'s PUBLIC `held` is now `_held AND _verified_ok is not False`, gated on the most recent confirmation rather than the bare intent flag, so it can never keep reading true for a light that's actually off. A fifth `mode` value, `"partial"`, reports "claims a hold, last check found at least one light not lit (or nothing left to hold at all)" — distinct from `"yielding"` (which specifically means "auto-return standing aside for music," a different and wrong explanation for this case). `status()` always reports `verified_age_s` (seconds since whichever check — write or periodic — most recently ran) plus the raw `verify` detail whenever there's one, so a caller can tell "confirmed 4s ago" from "confirmed 20 minutes ago" instead of treating every result as equally live — the requested "say the age of the last one" alternative to a live check on every 3s poll. The SAME write-time honesty gap existed one layer deeper too, caught and fixed alongside: a `reconcile()` landing `"dark"`/`"no-hue-devices"` (SPECTRA not owning the live stack) previously still left `_held=True` with nothing to gate `held` against — `_apply()` now stamps `_verified_ok=False` immediately in that case too, via the same `_record_verify()` helper `verify_now()` uses. Deliberately NOT built, by design, matching the brief's own hard limit: the verifier never re-WRITES to force a broken hold back on — reporting reality, not fighting for control of it (mirrors `services/ambient.py`'s own stated choice not to re-light a bulb he turned off on the release path). **Superseded, narrowly, by §61 (2026-08-16)**: "detects but does not repair" turned out to be a real live defect on its own (two named stragglers sitting wrong for hours, never fixed) — the verifier now DOES re-write an ON-but-wrong-colour straggler it finds (`ambient.repair_stragglers()`), and still never re-writes a light it finds OFF, which is the one case this paragraph was ever actually protecting. UI: `RoomControlsBar.tsx`'s ambient badge grows a `partial` (red) state and a `verified_age_s`-derived "· Ns ago" / "· Nm ago" suffix plus the specific unlit bulb names in its tooltip; `types.ts` grows `AmbientVerify` and widens `AmbientGateStatus.mode`. Verified: `tests/test_ambient.py` (4 new tests: `verify_held` dark/no-devices/fully-lit/unlit-without-writing — the last explicitly asserts zero PUT calls happen during a recheck) + `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` (8 new tests: age tracking, short-circuit-doesn't-refresh-age, verify_now no-op/lock-skip, the flagship "a light found off downgrades held" proof, live-stack-gone downgrade, write-time dark-doesn't-report-held) — full suite **405 of 407 passed**, 2 skipped (pre-existing live-model smoke tests, no API key/token in this environment), 1 pre-existing environmental failure (`test_settings_mcp_server_starts_from_a_clean_cwd`, missing `mcp` package in this worktree's venv — confirmed identical on pristine `master`, unrelated to this change). Eye-checked live against an isolated `python -m spectra` instance (`SPECTRA_PORT=18010`, fabricated fixture storage, bogus `SPOTFX_PORT` so the bridge never reached the real spot-effects process — never his live 8010 or `storage/spectra/`): `PUT /api/room-controls` with `ambient_mode="always"` against a stack with no live Hue devices returned `ambient_result: {"status": "dark"}` as before, but `GET /api/engine/status` now honestly reported `ambient.held: false, mode: "partial"` (previously would have read `held: true`) with `verified_age_s` present and growing between polls, and the periodic verifier ticked on its own 30s cadence without error in the process log. `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` clean. **Room-unproven, by the brief's own hard limit**: he was asleep and his lights were off because he turned them off — building the exact bar-clearing proof (claim a hold, turn a bulb off out-of-band, watch status correct itself) against his real bulbs requires his room, and that was explicitly withheld tonight; the equivalent proof runs offline instead (`test_verify_now_downgrades_held_when_a_light_is_found_off`, `test_verify_held_reports_a_light_turned_off_out_of_band_without_writing`) against the same mocked-bridge harness `tests/test_ambient.py`/`tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` already use for every other Ambient proof in this ledger. |
| §59 | Per-song, genre-anchored intensity scale (port + headroom-reserve gate + manual mark) | `BUILT` (full), **room-proven 2026-08-16** | PR #88, merged (`f0eda7c`). Built and unit-proven; **room proof now taken (this PR)** — see the dated paragraph at the end of this row. No commit since has touched `intensity_scale.py`, `intensity_scale_marks.py`, or `midsong_generator.py` — this is still the terminal state, not superseded. Admiral's direct ask (2026-08-15): "do we still use the intensity scaling per song and per [genre]... we need to have something like that." SpotFX's own mechanism (`services/intensity_scale_service.py`) had been dropped in the rebuild — confirmed, not re-litigated. Ported to `spectra/services/intensity_scale.py`: genre slider → song-space base (`genre_to_song_scale`, calibrated 2026-07-29 — these are SCALING FACTORS, not target intensities: Dopamine≈1.20, Let It Be≈0.50, Soy Peor≈1.00 (100% = no adjustment, NOT "maximum" — a units error a 2026-08-15 report made, then retracted; see below), EDM 1.85/Rock 0.7/Trap 1.35 sliders) × a per-song bass-rank factor (0.9–1.1) from the same three signals SpotFX used, cached SPECTRA-side (`storage/spectra/intensity_scale_features.json`, never SpotFX's own cache file). Wired at the RENDER choke points only — `trigger_engine._fire`/`_fire_transition` and `scene_sequencer._roll`'s fire call — never at scene/flare/colour-set SELECTION (`selection_kernel.select`'s own `genre_mult` already factors genre into the pick; scaling intensity there too would double-count it, a defect this port deliberately avoids). **Then corrected same-day, his words, closed — not a curve-shape question**: "currently it is just a straight multiplication of a factor" was wrong; his replacement, `final = measured_intensity * 0.6 * song_scaling_factor` (`intensity_scale.combine_measured_and_scale`, the one seam every future equation change touches) — 0.6 is a DELIBERATE headroom reserve, not a fudge factor: "this allows me to gate certain high-intensities by requiring a high scaling factor on a track so we do not get loud hype scenes unless its a hype track." Clamp order is load-bearing and documented at the constant: the only clamp is on the FULL product, last — clamping the scale term early would silently defeat the gate. **His ruling on the ceiling (same day, BUILT, resolves the question this replaces — formerly OQ-6, closed not reused per the numbering convention)**: 0.75 STANDS as the automatic ceiling — `auto_scaling_factor()` never exceeds `SCALE_MAX=1.25`, so nothing automatic ever produces `final` above `0.6×1.25=0.75`. `spectra/services/intensity_scale_marks.py` is the release valve he asked for: a per-track manual mark (clamped `[0, 2.0]`, SpotFX's own manual-slider ceiling), checked FIRST by `song_scaling_factor()` and never re-clamped into the auto range — "he marks the track; automatic never does," restoring the exact control SpotFX's own manual slider gave him. API `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/intensity-scale/mark?uri=`; UI `IntensityMarkControl.tsx` on the shared `TopBarStrip` (help topic `intensity-mark`). **The Soy Peor discrepancy, corrected**: an earlier pass reported its auto factor (0.859) against his 1.00 target as a 0.141 miss and, testing whether library growth explained it, found the drift hypothesis FALSIFIED with direct measurement — Soy Peor has a pre-calibration capture (2026-07-08, `.librosa.json.bak`) whose bass features are within noise of today's (recaptured 2026-08-14); a reconstructed 340-song library as of 2026-07-29 gives the same rank (0.218) as today's 841-song library (0.219) — the auto formula would have said ~86% on 7/29 too, not drift. That correctly falsified the drift theory, but the CONCLUSION drawn from it — "his ear calls it a full-intensity track and the metric can't perceive it" — was a units error (100% read as "maximum" instead of "no adjustment") and has been retracted. On the corrected terms this is a modest, still-real 0.141 FACTOR mismatch: Soy Peor measures at the 7th percentile for bass WITHIN its own Trap/Reggaeton bucket (bucket median rank 0.488, near the library-wide median — the genre isn't systematically suppressed, this song specifically ranks low in it), and the genre base alone (0.91) already sits below his 1.00 target before the ±10% bass nudge even applies — reaching exactly 1.00 from that base would need a bass rank near the 99th percentile of the ENTIRE library, nowhere close to where it measures. Not resolved further; not treated as a code defect (the numbers are faithful to what this exact, inherited formula would have shown on 7/29 too). Also folds in his 2026-08-15 edge-trim fix to `spectra/services/midsong_generator.py`'s per-song min-max renormalization (a cold open/fade-out no longer sets the floor; first/last 15s clamp to [0,1] instead of being floored; a track under ~30s falls back to the pre-trim behaviour, his stated edge case, resolved not guessed). Regression tool carrying the 29 July calibration forward: `scripts/check_intensity_scale_reference_songs.py` (read-only, reports all three reference songs' current auto scale + final-at-peak against his targets — run against real `storage/audio_shapes`). Tests: `tests/test_intensity_scale.py`, `tests/test_intensity_scale_marks.py`, `tests/test_midsong_generator.py`; wiring proof in `scripts/check_triggers.py`. **UI tightened 2026-08-16, his own words, confirmed twice (`fm/spectra-topbar-tighten`)**: "the icon before Mark should be gone... I want a slider, but the icon before mark should be gone" — the `🎚` emoji on `IntensityMarkControl.tsx`'s label was purely decorative (confirmed against the code before building, per his own instruction) and is now removed, leaving a plain "Mark" label. Editing gained a real `<input type="range">` alongside the existing `<input type="number">`, both bound to the same draft value so dragging and typing stay in sync — the range alone is kept precise deliberately: a drag can't reliably land one specific percent, and this factor drives how loud the room gets, so the number field stays for the exact value while the slider gets you close fast. No backend/logic change — `intensity_scale.py`/`intensity_scale_marks.py` untouched, this is presentation only. Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts` topic `intensity-mark` updated to describe the slider and drop the emoji reference. `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean; visually confirmed via the same static harness as §43's addendum, at 390×844.

**Room-proven 2026-08-16, live playback on `Serenity`, this PR**: hype vs. calm, side by side. Two genuine EDM/trance tracks ("Strange World - 2000 Remake", Push, and "Everytime - Nalin & Kane Remix", Lustral/Nalin & Kane — both genre-bucketed "EDM - Dance/High Energy") resolved `auto_scaling_factor = 1.25` (`SCALE_MAX`, the auto ceiling) at every sample taken, with measured (raw) intensity ranging 0.74–0.98 across several reads — `final` (`combine_measured_and_scale`) worked out to 0.55–0.73, i.e. the room ran right up against the 0.75 automatic ceiling. Fixture evidence at those moments: `executor.recent_writes` showed brightness 0.75–1.0 on every live virtual, and the SPECTRA UI's own per-pixel device-preview strip (`DevicePreviewStrip.tsx`, facade-sourced — the literal pre-freeze render frame, see the device-preview section above) showed a dense, fully-saturated colour fill edge to edge. A genuinely calm track — "Let It Be - Remastered 2009" (The Beatles, genre-bucketed "Rock") — was played on the same device for the comparison: `auto_scaling_factor` resolved to 0.485, measured intensity sampled 0.23–0.42 across the song (its own natural range, not cherry-picked), giving `final` of 0.067–0.123 — the device-preview strip for the same duration showed a near-black frame with a single faint dim sliver, a stark, unmistakably-different visual against the hype tracks' full bright fill. **The headroom-reserve gate's actual guarantee, checked arithmetically, not just observed**: even at Let It Be's own theoretical peak (measured_intensity=1.0, never actually reached in the sample), `final` caps at `1.0 × 0.6 × 0.485 = 0.291` — under half of what the hype tracks reached at an ORDINARY moment (0.55–0.73) and nowhere near the 0.75 automatic ceiling either track pushed up against. The calm track genuinely cannot reach the loud scenes even at its own peak; the gate does what it was built for. Reference songs reconfirmed live via `scripts/check_intensity_scale_reference_songs.py` against real `storage/audio_shapes` (841-song library, 2026-08-16): Dopamine 1.208 (target 1.20, diff +0.008, final@1.0=0.725), Let It Be 0.485 (target 0.50, diff −0.015, final@1.0=0.291 — the SAME number the live API read for the actual playing track above, a live/offline cross-check), Soy Peor 0.859 (target 1.00, diff −0.141, final@1.0=0.515) — Soy Peor's factor is byte-identical to the value already investigated and explained in the paragraph above; no further drift since that investigation. **Scoping note, not a defect**: his room's own standing rest state (`ambient_mode="always"`, verified unchanged before/after — see the Ambient section above) held every physical Hue bulb frozen throughout this proof, by design, orthogonal to this gate (composability documented in the Dark/Light section above) — the visual evidence above is therefore the render pipeline's own computed output (`recent_writes`, the facade-tapped device-preview frame) — confirmed to differ exactly as described between the hype and calm tracks — and NOT a claim about what any physical fixture displayed: no independent physical-fixture read-back (camera, bulb-state GET, or equivalent) was taken for any non-Hue device in this proof, so "visibly responded" is not asserted for them. Separately, and worth naming so the next crew doesn't inherit the assumption: `radial-dummy` has no IP and no physical light behind it at all (confirmed against his live config) — it is a dummy virtual, not a stand-in for a real fixture, so it could never "visibly respond" regardless of what this proof found. Playback was borrowed for ~90s via a direct Spotify Web API call (his own OAuth token, already scoped `user-modify-playback-state`) to get a controlled calm sample, then restored to the exact track/context/position/shuffle state it was in before, verified via `GET /api/spotify/status` after restore. `room-controls` state read byte-identical before and after (`dark_mode_enabled=false, brightness_multiplier=1.0, ambient_mode="always", ambient_color="#f5da8c", scene_change_mode="full", force_scene_enabled=false`). Full suite: 537 passed, 3 failed — all three pre-existing and unrelated (2× `tests/test_settings_agent_cli.py` need the optional `mcp` Python package, not installed in this worktree's venv; 1× `test_spectra_engine.py::test_mid_group_scenes_animate_on_the_harness` needs seeded live `storage/spectra/scenes.json`, gitignored — see `spotfx-environmental-test-failures` — confirmed identical on a bare checkout, not a regression from this PR, which touched no code). |
| §60 | Sonic token usage on the review page (last query / this day / this week) | `BUILT` (full), **room-unproven** | PR #92, merged (`4babd42`). Built and unit-proven, **room-unproven — no live Sonic call made** (see below for why that's not treated as a blocking gap here). His own ask, verbatim: "on the review page I want to see how much token usage Sonic has used for the last query, the last day, and the last week." Put on the Review page as asked, not relocated to the settings console. **The bar this was built to clear**: every figure is REAL REPORTED USAGE read off the model runtime's own response for that call — `settings_agent.py` (the "api"/API-credits backend) sums the Anthropic SDK's own `response.usage` across a turn's tool-call rounds; `settings_agent_cli.py` (the "cli"/subscription backend, still gated off per §55's captain's ruling) reads the `claude -p --output-format json` final `result` event's own `usage`/`modelUsage`/`total_cost_usd` — both captured in `spectra/services/sonic_usage.py`, never estimated from word/character counts. A call the runtime genuinely doesn't report usage for records nothing (no fabricated zero) — proven against a fixture with no `usage` key at all (`cli_transcript_manifest_mismatch.json`) and against a test-double response with no `.usage` attribute. **Mid-build overrule, his words, binding, superseding this doc's own first draft (which had proposed rolling 24h/7d windows)**: "choose Monday at 10:00 p.m. Eastern as the cutoff so each day runs from 10:00 p.m. to 10:00 p.m and each week runs from Monday 10:00 p.m." His stated reason: a window aligned to his subscription's own quota reset tells him how much he has LEFT, not just how much he's spent — "how much is left" is the number that matters after a quota exhausted on him without warning earlier this week (`docs/SPECTRA_SPEC.md`/`AGENTS.md`'s own live-room notes on that incident). Built as two FIXED periods, `spectra/services/sonic_usage._period_start_ms()`: day = 22:00-to-22:00, week = Monday-22:00-to-Monday-22:00, both real `America/New_York` via stdlib `zoneinfo` (DST-aware — proven against both 2026 US transition dates, never a hardcoded UTC offset) and bucketed at READ time against stored per-call `wall_ms` timestamps (never pre-assigned at write time), so correcting the anchor later would re-bucket history correctly rather than lock old entries to a stale boundary — proven by `test_moving_now_across_the_boundary_re_buckets_the_same_stored_log`. **Durable across a restart, the other half of "before it becomes a problem"**: `storage/spectra/sonic_usage.json`, atomic tmp+replace, same discipline as `fire_history.py`'s show log — proven by reloading the module fresh mid-test and confirming the identical summary reconstructs from disk alone (no in-memory state to lose). API `GET /api/sonic-usage`; UI `spectra/web/src/review/components/SonicUsageCard.tsx` (mounted above the Review card), help topic `sonic-token-usage`. Tests: `tests/test_sonic_usage.py` (16 tests — period-boundary edge cases incl. both DST crossings, real-fixture extraction matching `cli_transcript_applied.json`'s own recorded numbers exactly, the API backend's fake-SDK-client integration incl. partial-usage-survives-an-exception, restart durability); full suite 507 passed, 2 skipped (pre-existing live-model smoke tests), 2 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated (missing `mcp` package in this worktree's venv — confirmed identical on pristine `master`, same class as §58's note). **Why room-unproven isn't a gap here**: no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` exists in this build environment, and the task's own hard limit was to keep any live Sonic call to the minimum the proof needed — zero were made. Instead proven through the REAL production HTTP path (`TestClient` against `spectra.app.create_app()`, no mocked routing) and, for the CLI backend specifically, against an ACTUAL historical captured transcript (not a hand-built one) — `cli_transcript_applied.json`'s own `usage`/`modelUsage`/`total_cost_usd` fields are the ground truth `test_cli_backend_records_the_exact_figures_a_real_transcript_reported` asserts against. Eye-checked live in a browser (chrome-devtools-axi, isolated `create_app()` instance mounted at `/spectra` on a spare port, throwaway `SPECTRA_STORAGE_DIR`, no lifespan — never touches the S2 bridge/LedFX/any Hue bridge) against two `sonic_usage.record()`-seeded entries (not a live model call): the card renders "Last query 19,812 tokens · SUBSCRIPTION · $0.010", "This day 31,764 tokens · since Sat, Aug 15, 10:00 PM", "This week 31,764 tokens · since Mon, Aug 10, 10:00 PM" — correct Eastern-anchored boundaries for the sandbox's own current date (2026-08-16). What his real window needs to add: a genuine live Sonic call (through whichever backend is actually configured on his instance) with the recorded entry compared to that call's own console/CLI-reported usage. |
| §61 | Ambient — burst-drop pacing, straggler REPAIR (not just detection), and the false-unlit fix | `BUILT`, room-proven | Dispatched to characterise "the fault behind everything we've chased this week": his bridges silently 2xx-accepting a write burst with nothing physically applied, §58's own retry NAMING a straggler forever without ever fixing it, and a lit-verification readout that invented failures. All three are real; only the first is NOT the sharp cliff it was first reported as — see below. **Burst threshold, measured not reasoned about**: 48 controlled, self-checked sustained-burst trials against BOTH real bridges independently (`dining-hues` 7 lights, `hue-lights` 10 lights), 0.08s–0.60s pacing near the documented Zigbee ~10 cmd/s ceiling, found ZERO drops anywhere in that range on either bridge — this does NOT reproduce the paired evidence's claimed "0.12s = 0/17 lit, 0.45s = 17/17" cliff; the most likely explanation is a target-tracking bug in whatever script produced that evidence, the SAME class of mistake this investigation's own first attempt made and only caught via a self-check gate (a single-round burst test initially "reproduced" 0/10 at every pace down to 60ms — a false readout from checking the wrong round's colour, not a real drop; retracted once caught, sustained-burst methodology re-run clean). **What IS real, independently confirmed**: the bridge gives ZERO signal on a silent drop — every PUT across every trial, including the historical 2026-08-15 incident this module's own docstring already documented (3/17 stragglers under a real burst), returned a clean `HTTP 200`/`{"errors":[]}`, no `429`, no `Retry-After`, no rate-limit header — a retry design has nothing to react to but a read-back, confirming `ambient.py`'s existing discipline (never trust a response code) is the whole answer, not one layer of several. `AMBIENT_WRITE_STAGGER_MS` raised 50ms→300ms anyway (margin inside the confirmed-safe band, not the fastest pace that survived — his own four-requirement brief's explicit ask) as defense against conditions one measurement session can't rule out, and because `spectra/services/ambient.py::_apply_hue` (the OFF-fade/catch-up paths) had NO pacing at all before this pass, a real gap regardless of where the ON path's cliff does or doesn't sit. **Straggler repair — the "detects but does not repair" fix**: `ambient_music_gate.py`'s periodic `verify_now()` recheck could name an off-target straggler correctly on every 30s tick, forever, and never once try to fix it (§58's own "Deliberately NOT built" — a design choice made to avoid fighting a bulb he turned off himself, but which also silently swallowed the ON-but-wrong-colour case it was never really about). `spectra/services/ambient.py::repair_stragglers()` reuses the exact paced/retried/read-back-confirmed engine (`_write_and_confirm`, factored out of the pre-existing `_hold_and_confirm`) the initial hold already trusted, restricted to just the named stragglers; wired into `verify_now()` so a detected miss gets one repair attempt before status reports "partial". Still NEVER re-lights a light that reads OFF right now (checked fresh, immediately before writing) — narrowed from "the verifier writes nothing" to "the verifier never writes to a light that reads off," the one case the rule was ever actually protecting. **False-unlit fix**: `_state_matches` (on+colour+brightness, confirms OUR OWN write) and `_color_matches` (on+colour only, the reporting-level check `verify_held()`/`status()` now use) are split — a bulb dimmed out of band (a wall switch, the Hue app, or his own choice) but still on and at the correct hue no longer reads "unlit." **Proven live against real hardware, not just the headless suite** (a real straggler forced on `Standing Lamp Side`: detected, genuinely repaired, independently read back confirmed fixed; a real 35%-brightness drift forced on `Loft Ceiling Uplight`: correctly reported LIT both before and after touching the module, proving the split isn't cosmetic) — room ends 17/17 at his exact `#f5da8c`, ground-truth verified directly against both bridges after every live experiment, never left mid-test. Also found and fixed live, incidentally: his room had silently drifted off-target for ~9 hours with zero write attempts in between (nothing had re-asserted it, by the pre-existing design this task's own repair mechanism now closes) — corrected via a real ambient off/on toggle during the investigation, independently confirmed. Tests: `tests/test_ambient.py` (+11: `_color_matches`/`_state_matches` split, `verify_held` brightness-tolerant, `repair_stragglers` rewrites/never-relights-off/reports-unconfirmed), `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` (+1 wiring proof, plus a pre-existing fixture bug fixed alongside — `hue_room` was rebuilding its mock bridge's state fresh on every `_bridge_client()` call, silently masking whether a later check reads real prior state or a coincidental default; the off-bulb proof happened to still pass by coincidence before the fix). Full suite: 503 collected, 498 passed, 5 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated to this change (`test_crystal_activation_verify.py` ×3, `test_settings_agent_cli.py` ×2 — missing `mcp` package/activation-gap fixtures, confirmed identical on pristine branch tip before this work started). **What this row does NOT establish, per its own text — see [OQ-8](#open-questions)**: the ROOT CAUSE of why bulbs ended up in a bad state in the first place (both the retracted 17-bulb measurement and the real historical 3/17-under-a-real-burst incident this module's docstring already documented) is still unexplained; what's fixed is that a bad state, whatever causes it, is now DETECTED honestly and actually REPAIRED, not that the cause is understood. Do not read "BUILT, room-proven" as "the mystery is solved." |
| §62 | STAR/Singles/power — accent param (spark colour) forced black, not LedFX's white default | `BUILT` (full), **proven live 2026-08-16** | A device's "accent" param (`sparks_color` on `power`, etc.) is force-written black by `scene_compiler._entry_config` on every compile unless the scene entry itself authored a value (`fx/device_model.accent_param_for`, ported from spot-effects' own `services/trigger_engine.py` accent-defaults-to-black rule) — see `AGENTS.md`'s SPECTRA app section for the mechanism. PR #84 (2026-08-15) closed the un-ported gap this repo's own `services/scene_v2_compiler.py` docstring had already flagged (accent not yet carried by the fixed colour vocabulary): SPECTRA's descendant compiler had silently inherited that same gap, with nothing downstream to catch it — STAR/Singles/power was rendering LedFX's white spark default instead of his authored black. PR #84 itself shipped **room-unproven** ("STAR was not the active scene during my investigation window... this fix is unproven in his room"). **PR #90 (2026-08-16) is the live-room proof that closes it**: his own corrected report named "radial," not "radio" — the `Strips` category's `radial-dummy` virtual, whose effect is promoted `melt`→`power` by `effect_steps` at intensity ≥0.7 (the original regression check only exercised intensity 0.5, below that threshold, so it never touched the reported path — the actual bug in the check, not the fix). Live-fired STAR at intensity 0.8, `dry_run=False`, against his real room: the `fx_seam` write for `radial-dummy` carried `sparks_color=#000000`, confirmed by reading the write back, not inferred from the fire call succeeding. See §56 for the sibling STAR fix from the same investigation. **PREMISE CORRECTION, code-verified 2026-08-16 (PR #102)**: PR #90's live proof targeted the wrong fixture. `radial-dummy` is a `type: "dummy"` device with no IP and no physical light behind it (confirmed against his live `storage/spectra/fx-live/config.json`) — he cannot have seen white sparks there; a proof at that virtual can never be the proof he asked for. The `Strips` category (`storage/device_categories.json`) has exactly two virtuals, read fresh rather than assumed: `radial-dummy` and `tv-mapper`. The task brief that opened this pass had *also* guessed wrong, naming `tv-backlight`, `crystal`, `porch-rail`, and the kitchen sconces as separate category members — `crystal` and `porch-rail` are NOT in `Strips` (`crystal` is `Matrix`/`Particles`, reached via `crystal-mapper`; `porch-rail` belongs to no category at all). The one real member is `tv-mapper` — a single virtual whose `segments` (read from live `fx-live/config.json`) span three genuine WLED devices: `tv-backlight` (pixels 0–559), `sconce-kitchen-right` (two segments), `sconce-kitchen-left` (two segments) — those are the fixtures he was actually looking at. **New evidence, no synthetic intensity this time**: his own `storage/spectra/show_log.json` already held 30 genuine automatic STAR fires on 2026-08-16 that crossed the `effect_steps` promotion threshold (intensity ≥0.7) during his real, unprompted listening — most recently 20:28:03Z and 20:28:14Z (intensities 0.7325, 0.75), ~24 minutes before this check ran. Side-effect-free replay (`POST /api/scenes/{id}/fire`, `dry_run=true` — the compiler's own docstring guarantees dry and live share identical resolution up to the seam for this deterministic, non-dice entry) at those exact real intensities shows `tv-mapper` compiling identically to `radial-dummy`: `effect_type=power`, `sparks_color=#000000`. At a real observed sub-threshold intensity (0.6435, also lifted from the same show log), `tv-mapper` stays on `melt` — confirming the check must genuinely cross 0.7 to exercise the reported path, exactly as PR #90's own note warned. **The honest limit, unchanged and worth restating**: no independent physical-fixture read-back exists for WLED devices (no camera, no bulb-state GET, no equivalent) — this proves what was WRITTEN to `tv-mapper`, not what his eyes saw at the strip or the sconces; that confirmation is his alone. Room untouched throughout: only GETs and side-effect-free dry-run fires were used — `room-controls`, the active scene (`Orbits V2`, never disturbed), and the ambient hold (17/17 lit) all read byte-identical before and after. |
| §63 | Ambient — second colour for dark mode (day-one composition fix) | `BUILT` (full) — **room-proven 2026-08-16**, PR #91 merged (`aa7ecde`) | Resolves `data/backlog.md` `spectra-dark-light-vs-ambient-ruling` (filed 2026-08-15, immediately after §9 landed): dark/light acts on his STREAMED devices only (`crystal-mapper`/`radial-dummy`/`tv-mapper`, via `dark_lock`), while `ambient_mode="always"` holds every Hue device unconditionally over direct bridge REST, bypassing LedFX entirely — so switching dark mode on left his main Hue lights bright, which reads as broken for something he called GLOBAL dark/light even though both features were individually doing exactly what he'd asked. Three readings were put to him (dark overrides the hold / ambient-always wins outright / the two compose); **his ruling, verbatim**: "dark mode during ambient should choose a different color that I pick so we have one color for regular ambient light mode or hybrid mode and then one color for dark mode for now make them the same but let me be able to pick it with the color picker." **Dark never bypasses Ambient and Ambient never blocks dark — dark mode changes WHAT Ambient holds.** Built: `RoomControlState.ambient_color_dark` (`spectra/services/room_controls.py`), the same shipped LedFX colour picker (§46) as `ambient_color`, no new control — deliberately `Optional[str] = None` rather than a value copied at a migration instant, so the two stay identical **by construction** ("for now make them the same") until he diverges them, with no one-time snapshot to drift. `room_controls.effective_ambient_color(state)` is the one resolver every ambient write/verify/change-detection path now goes through instead of reading `ambient_color` directly — `ambient_color_dark` wins only while `dark_mode_enabled` is True AND it's been authored; otherwise unchanged from before this field existed. Three call sites route through it: `ambient_music_gate.reconcile()`'s write, `verify_now()`'s independent read-back (§58's status-honesty verifier — routing through the resolver matters here specifically, or a genuinely-correct dark hold would misreport as `partial` forever against the wrong target colour), and `reconcile_ambient_if_changed()`'s change-detection gate (fires on a dark-colour edit, on `dark_mode_enabled` itself toggling while the two colours differ, and correctly not on an edit to a colour that isn't currently in effect). Transition — decided, not asked, his brief left it open: a colour change while Ambient already holds re-invokes the SAME `_apply()` "colour changed while holding" branch a human editing the picker by hand already exercises, riding Hue's own bridge-side 1.5s `dynamics.duration` ramp (`AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS`) — deliberately NOT the ~16s two-phase release/catch-up ramp, since that mechanism hands a frozen bulb back to a *live rendering effect* it needs to catch up to, and this is a colour swap within an already-held room where both endpoints are static. No new transition code at all: `effective_ambient_color()` resolves to a different value and the pre-existing re-apply branch does the rest — which is also why toggling before he's picked a distinct dark colour is a correct no-op. Also composes with §5/§6 (`spectra/services/ambient.py`'s own docstring: **Ambient covers Hue only, by design, matching legacy's scope exactly** — "the Entertainment-API brightness cap this sidesteps is a Hue-only problem," non-Hue devices keep running their normal show; his own §53 language, "my Hue Lights are lit and bright but the other lights are still running the show," confirms this is his own mental model too, not an implementation accident; also see `data/decision-ambient-hue-only.md`, his own words, 2026-08-15: "I only want ambient to manage Hue Lights for now so that is correct" — a decided design choice, not a gap). Tests: `tests/test_room_controls.py` (7 new — resolver defer/override/revert, hex validation, an old-file-without-the-field load with no migration code needed, `reconcile_ambient_if_changed` firing/not-firing correctly) + `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py` (4 new, end to end against the mocked Hue bridge — holds the dark colour when dark mode is on, a toggle re-applies live at the resolved colour with the eased glide, a toggle is a no-op when the colours match, `verify_now()` confirms against the dark colour rather than misreporting `partial`). Deliberately NOT extended: Sonic's settings-console `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` (§45) — a new `RoomControlState` field is invisible to the agent until added there on purpose, and this wasn't asked for. **A real live collision, found and fixed at rebase time, before room-proving**: PR #93 (§61) landed independently while this branch was queued and added `ambient_music_gate.verify_now()`'s straggler-repair write (`ambient.repair_stragglers()`), calling it with the bare `ambient_color` rather than the resolved colour — exactly the composition bug this row exists to prevent (a straggler repaired while a distinct dark colour is active would have been rewritten back to the *normal* colour, re-drifting it the very next tick). Fixed during the rebase: `verify_now()` now resolves `target_color = effective_ambient_color(controls)` once and reuses it for both the `verify_held()` read and the `repair_stragglers()` write; `tests/test_ambient_music_gate.py::test_verify_now_repairs_a_straggler_to_the_dark_colour_not_the_normal_one` proven to fail against the pre-fix call shape before the fix was restored. **Glide extended to 3000ms, 2026-08-16 — HIS STATED PREFERENCE, NOT AN OBSERVED RESULT, and that distinction matters here specifically**: `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS` (this row's own `dynamics.duration` ramp, above) raised from legacy's 1500ms per his ask — "The transition appears to be about 1.5 seconds like you said. go ahead and extend it to 3 seconds though." That sentence was given BEFORE he had actually watched a live toggle; his own words immediately after, correcting the earlier brief that had asked this row to record an eyewitness result: "Sorry I thought that you had already started running these transitions let me test it directly like you said." His agreement was courtesy about our own description of the mechanism, not an observation of it running — **THE EASED TRANSITION REMAINS UNPROVEN**, same honest-gap posture this row's own "Transition — eased, not snapped" finding above already models for the OFF-path CLIP-polling limit, and the 3000ms value must not be read as settled until he has actually watched it. **Investigated rather than assumed, per the brief's own instruction to verify a claimed defect before fixing it**: a claimed retry/read-back collision (`_write_and_confirm`'s bounded retries reading a Hue bulb back while it's still mid-glide, manufacturing false stragglers as the ramp lengthens) does NOT exist as described — retries already write via `snap_body`, which drops `dynamics` entirely ("a stubborn light should snap, not take another [ramp] to maybe land"), so a retry write is never a glide and its settle wait was already independent of `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS`. The REAL timing defect this pass found and fixed instead: `repair_stragglers()` calls `_write_and_confirm` with a body carrying NO ramp at all (`_light_payload(color_hex)`, no `ramp_ms` — a stubborn light should snap on ITS first write too, not just its retries), yet `settle_ms` keyed off `attempt == 0` rather than off whether that attempt's write actually had a ramp — so repair's first attempt was needlessly waiting out a full `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS` before reading back an instant write, a wasted wait that would have silently doubled with this change. Fixed by keying `settle_ms` off `"dynamics" in write_body` instead of the attempt index: the ON-hold's real glide (attempt 0, ramped) is unaffected and still self-scales; repair's un-ramped write now settles in `AMBIENT_CONFIRM_SETTLE_MS` alone, at any `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS`. `verify_now()` cannot race a live glide either — it skips its tick entirely while `_apply_lock` is held (module docstring), and a hold's own write-then-confirm sequence holds that lock through its full settle, ramp included; a scene/trigger fire arriving mid-transition is genuinely fine too, since Ambient freezes each Hue device's own stream while held (`set_frozen(True)`) — a scene fire's writes to a frozen device are dropped at the render layer regardless of glide length, and non-Hue devices were never touched by this constant. The OFF path (`AMBIENT_OFF_FADE_PCT`/`AMBIENT_CATCHUP_MS`) re-checked and unaffected in shape: phase 1's dim fade still self-scales its own wait to `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS` (no read-back on that path, so no misread risk), phase 2's catch-up ramp is untouched (its own separate `AMBIENT_CATCHUP_MS`) — his praised fade-and-release shape is preserved, just phase 1 now takes 3s instead of 1.5s, matching the same constant governing the ON-side hold. `spectra/services/release_fade.py`'s own `RELEASE_FADE_MS` — the SEPARATE panic-release fade behind `services/release.py`'s red button, not this feature — is deliberately left at 1500ms, out of scope (a one-shot power-off he isn't judging by eye the same way); its comment claiming numeric parity with `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS` is corrected to note the divergence rather than left stale. Also fixed alongside: `scripts/check_spectra.py`'s ambient section restored `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS` to a hardcoded `1500` in its `finally` block after mutating it for the spec run — the one constant of five NOT captured via `_orig_*` the way its siblings are — now captured/restored the same way, since the hardcode would otherwise have silently left the module at the wrong default for the rest of that process's life.
