New test: `tests/test_ambient.py::test_repair_stragglers_settle_does_not_wait_for_a_ramp_it_never_sent` proves the fix directly (asserts the settle sleep is `AMBIENT_CONFIRM_SETTLE_MS` alone, not `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS+AMBIENT_CONFIRM_SETTLE_MS`, against a genuine repaired straggler). Full suite: 541 collected, 536 passed, 2 skipped (pre-existing live-model smoke tests, no API key in this environment), 3 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated to this change (`test_settings_agent_cli.py` ×2 — missing `mcp` package in this worktree's venv; `test_spectra_engine.py::test_mid_group_scenes_animate_on_the_harness` — needs seeded live storage this disposable worktree doesn't carry — all three confirmed identical/absent on pristine `master`, same class as §60/§61/§64's own notes); `scripts/check_spectra.py`'s Ambient section: all checks pass. **One question answered, not built, per his own brief**: exposing this glide through the settings console alongside `global_transition_ms` would be cheap — one more `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` entry reading `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS`'s own bounds, the same shape the other four agent-tellable room-control fields already use — but he hasn't asked for it, so it stays a plain constant. **DEPLOY HELD, by design**: this change must not go live before he has actually watched the CURRENTLY-DEPLOYED 1.5s glide and reported what he sees — deploying 3000ms under him now would mean he judges 3 seconds while believing he is judging 1.5, poisoning the one measurement that can settle this.

**Room-proven 2026-08-16**, his room free but HE WAS AWAY — every check below is instrumented (bridge CLIP v2 read-backs, server logs, the deployed commit's own source), never eyeballed; **he has not personally seen this yet**. Read his live state first (`GET /spectra/api/room-controls`): `ambient_mode="always"`, `ambient_color="#f5da8c"`, `ambient_color_dark=null`, `dark_mode_enabled=false`, `scene_change_mode="full"`, 17/17 lit — matched exactly what firstmate reported pre-session. Deployed commit confirmed genuinely running first (`aa7ecde`, `grep`-verified against the live checkout, not assumed from the deploy message alone) before anything was touched. Four points proven, each read directly at both bridges via `scripts/verify_ambient_dark_colour_fixtures.py` (new, this PR — read-only for its own GETs; reuses `ambient._resolve_lights_named`/`_color_matches` directly so it proves against the exact set and tolerance Ambient itself uses, not a re-derived guess), with `dark_mode_enabled`/`ambient_color_dark` driven via real `PUT /spectra/api/room-controls` calls:
  1. **`ambient_color_dark=null` defers to the normal colour** ("for now make them the same," his own words) — baseline read 17/17 on at `#f5da8c`'s exact xy, both bridges.
  2. **Authoring a dark colour alone, with `dark_mode_enabled` still false, is a live no-op** — `PUT ambient_color_dark="#1a2a6c"` came back with no `ambient_result` key at all (the `reconcile_ambient_if_changed` gate correctly found nothing to do); re-read confirmed 17/17 unchanged at `#f5da8c`.
  3. **Toggling `dark_mode_enabled=true` swaps the hold to the dark colour** — `PUT` returned `ambient_result: {"status":"on","lights_set":17,"lights_total":17}` (one straggler needed the existing retry pass, log-confirmed: `"1 light(s) not yet confirmed... retrying: ['Under Spiral']"` then `"held at #1a2a6c, 17 light(s) confirmed"` — the retry mechanism working as designed, not a drop); direct bridge read-back confirmed 17/17 at the dark colour's exact xy on both bridges.
  4. **Toggling back returns to the normal colour** — `PUT dark_mode_enabled=false` logged `"held at #f5da8c, 17 light(s) confirmed"`; 17/17 confirmed at the bridges.

  **Transition — eased, not snapped, proven at two levels, honestly bounded at a third**: (a) code-level, confirmed against the ACTUAL deployed source (not assumed from the PR diff): the write path unconditionally sends `dynamics: {"duration": 1500}` on the Hue PUT for every hold engage/re-apply (`ambient.py`'s `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS=1500`, `_light_payload`) — the identical mechanism his own already-praised engage-hold and ease-back already use, not a new one; (b) structurally, server logs show the swap as a single continuous hold — `"Ambient ON: ...held at #1a2a6c"` then later `"Ambient ON: ...held at #f5da8c"` with no intervening `"Ambient OFF: ...released"` between them, ruling out a hard release-then-reacquire snap; (c) **honestly could not be visually confirmed**: rapid-polling (150ms) the CLIP v2 light resource during the OFF transition showed a strictly binary jump (exactly the dark xy for ~5.2s, then exactly the normal xy from the next sample on) with no intermediate value ever observed — the bridge's REST resource reports commanded/target state, not a live-animating frame, so polling cannot see the physical fade a person standing in the room would; only an eyewitness can close that gap, and none was available this session.
  5. **PR #93 collision fix re-confirmed live, not just at rest**: with the dark colour held, one bulb ("Standing Lamp Side") was deliberately drifted out-of-band (direct bridge PUT, bypassing SPECTRA, to an xy matching neither colour) to force a genuine straggler. The periodic verifier caught and repaired it within one tick — log-confirmed (`"Ambient repair: re-asserted the ambient colour on 1 straggler(s): ['Standing Lamp Side']"`) — and the bridge read-back confirmed it landed on the DARK colour's exact xy, never the normal one. Full 17/17 re-confirmed DARK afterward.

  **His room restored exactly as found** and independently re-verified three ways after: `GET /room-controls` matches the pre-session state field-for-field, `GET /engine/status`'s `ambient` block reads `mode: "holding", held: true, 17/17 lit, unlit: []`, and the direct bridge read-back shows 17/17 at `#f5da8c`'s exact xy on both bridges. |
| §64 | Audit: every SPECTRA site that treats a Hue bridge 2xx as proof of application | **AUDITED**, one gap **found and fixed**, code-proven; **room-unproven — he was away** | Dispatched (spectra-audit-2xx-proof, 2026-08-16) on D6's own premise (a 2xx proves acceptance, never application) to check that discipline is actually applied everywhere, not just at ambient.py where it was found and fixed first. **Retraction noted, not re-derived**: D6's own burst-rate-cliff claim was already retracted before this audit started (48 self-checked trials, zero drops at any pace) — this row does not reopen that question or imply a drop rate; the only established fact carried forward is "a 2xx proves nothing," independent of pacing. **Sites inspected, found already correct/exempt (no change)**: `fx/devices/hue.py`'s `flush()` — the actual per-pixel colour stream is raw UDP at ~30fps, re-asserted every frame, so a single dropped frame self-heals on the next one — the task brief's own stated exemption criterion; its `action:"start"`/`action:"stop"` session PUTs are session-lifecycle calls proven downstream by whether frames actually flow (DTLS handshake success, `live_host.py`'s frame-freshness checks), not a bulb-colour claim. `spectra/services/fx_seam.py` (`apply_writes`/`get_virtuals`/`set_virtual_config`, the S1/S3 owner-Fire scene-fire seam) and `spectra/services/fx_executor.py` (the S2 engine's glide/jump seam) — both write to the LOCAL render engine (in-process facade or a same-host LedFX HTTP call), not the flaky Hue-bridge/zigbee hop itself; both raise loudly on failure (no swallowed exception, no confident report over a failure) and, once landed, the actual bulb colour is driven continuously by the same re-asserted-every-frame mechanism as `flush()` above — correctly exempt, not merely convenient. `spectra/services/ambient.py` — the gold standard this audit measures everything else against (`_hold_and_confirm`/`repair_stragglers`/`verify_held`, §51/§58/§61); one stale claim in its own docstring (asserting the release path "already reads real state back," written before checking) is corrected as part of this row, see below. `spectra/services/ambient_music_gate.py` — pure orchestration over `ambient.py`'s already-confirmed primitives, correctly never re-derives its own weaker notion of "held" (`_apply`/`verify_now` read). `spectra/services/ledfx_release.py` + `release.py`'s `_verify_released()` — the external-LedFX-virtual half of release cleanup: the PUT's own "success" body is NOT trusted alone, a genuinely independent GET re-read of `/api/virtuals` after cleanup is what `_verify_released()` actually checks — correctly built, unlike the Hue half (below). `spectra/services/handover.py` — never writes an individual Hue light; its own verification (`live_host.py`'s `activation_gaps()`/`device_gaps()`) is about virtual/stream activation, which is what its own claims are actually about — not a bulb-colour claim, so nothing here needed a bulb-level read-back. `fx/devices/wled.py`'s `WLED.release_realtime()` — the WLED equivalent of the Hue off-write, POSTs `{"live": false}` and trusts the response; deliberately NOT extended by this audit — WLED is wifi/JSON, not the zigbee-mesh-behind-a-bridge path D6's established fact is specifically about, and its self-reverting on-device-show behaviour was already independently live-verified 2026-08-14 (see the S3 section of `AGENTS.md`). **The gap found and fixed**: `spectra/services/release_fade.py`'s final Hue OFF write (the one write in the whole release panic-handle that determines whether Home Assistant inherits a dark room or a bulb still lit at SPECTRA's last streamed colour) only ever checked `raise_for_status()` — a 2xx — exactly the pattern D6 says proves nothing. `release.py`'s own `_verify_released()`, despite its docstring's claim, never independently checked individual Hue bulb state either — only process/ownership-level state (`live.active`, external LedFX virtuals). Fixed: `release_fade.fade_and_release_hue()` now reads each light back after the off write (`RELEASE_OFF_SETTLE_MS` wait, GET, one paced retry if still on), returns any still-on light's bridge name in a new `still_on` field instead of folding it into a bare "faded" claim; `release_room()` folds a non-empty `still_on` into `ReleaseResult.problems` and forces `verified=False` — the same "released-unverified" (HTTP 207) surface the LedFX-virtuals gap already used, so a caller sees the specific bulb named rather than a clean "released". `ambient.py`'s own docstring corrected to stop citing the (never true) claim that the release path already had this. Tests: `tests/test_release_fade.py` (+4 — happy-path read-back, a bridge built to 2xx-accept-but-not-apply the off write [`stuck_bridge`, the literal D6 shape] proving the retry fires and the light is named in `still_on`, the settle/retry pacing itself, the no-Hue-devices shape unaffected) + `tests/test_release.py` (+1 — `release_room()` reports `verified=False` with the light named in `problems` when the fade's read-back finds a straggler); full suite 535 collected, 531 passed, 2 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated (`test_settings_agent_cli.py` — missing `mcp` package in this worktree's venv, confirmed identical on pristine `master` before this work started, same class as §60/§61's note), 2 pre-existing skipped (live-model smoke tests, no API key in this environment). **What this row does NOT establish**: the fix is code-proven only — `scripts/verify_release_fixtures.py` (pre-existing, read-only, GET-only) is the live-fixture instrument that would prove it against his real bridges after an actual release press, but HE WAS AWAY for this pass and a release press is exactly the kind of action this task's own hard limits say to leave for a room window, not manufacture one to test. **A related but separate finding, written up, not fixed — see [OQ-9](#open-questions)**: the legacy (pre-SPECTRA, still-live) `services/ambient_mode.py::_apply_hue` carries the SAME 2xx-only pattern this row fixed in `release_fade.py` (`if resp.status_code < 400: count += 1`, no read-back at all) — and its own `_bridge_client` writes go direct to the Hue bridge over REST, bypassing `api.ledfx_client`'s S3 ownership gate entirely, the same shape `spectra/services/ambient.py`'s own bridge client uses by design. Not fixed here: it is the "old world" D4 already holds retirement-gated rather than scheduled, and fixing the ownership-bypass half is a materially different, larger problem (a second writer that isn't gated by the ownership record at all) than the read-back gap this row otherwise closes. |
| §65 | Curve selector behaviour — collapsed-on-open, scrollable, editing is structurally a one-off | `BUILT` (full), UI eye-checked at real phone width, not room-proven | His ask, verbatim: "I like the previews and how they look. however I don't want them to be expanded when I open the window I want them to expand when I click to select a curve... it should be scrollable... if I edit it it should immediately change it to a one-off, not default to saving the curve. There should also be a button for me to save it as a named curve and I should be able to overwrite with a warning dialogue." Closes the exact hole that bit him earlier this week: dragging a shared curve used to be one click ("Save curve") away from silently retuning every other scene using it. `spectra/web/src/scenes/tabs/SequencingTab.tsx` rebuilt around this: the tile grid (unchanged `CurveThumbnail` previews, still §54's — not redesigned) now sits in a `maxHeight:260/overflow-y:auto` scroll container and stays COLLAPSED (no editor) on open; clicking a named-profile or "Inline one-off…" tile both selects/attaches it (unchanged prior behaviour) AND opens a modal "curve window" (`position:fixed` overlay, `maxWidth:92vw`/`maxHeight:86vh`/`overflow-y:auto`, the same phone-safe shape `ImportDialog.tsx`/`SpectraTriggerDialog.tsx` already use) holding the full, still-unredesigned `CurveEditor`. Flat/not-sequenced select without opening a window (neither has a shape of its own to edit — unchanged from the prior gating). **THE safety property, structural not conventional**: `CurveEditor`'s `onChange` writes ONLY to local React state (`draft`) — no code path in the file reaches `saveCurvesMut`/`curves[existingId]` from a drag. Two explicit actions exist and only two: "Apply edited one-off to this scene" / "Detach — edit just this scene" calls `attachMut` with `kind:"inline"` targeting THIS scene's own `entries[sceneId].inline_points` — it has no argument and no branch that can address the shared `curves` store, so it is incapable of retuning another scene, not merely unlikely to. "Save as named curve…" is the ONLY function in the file that can write `curves[id]`; its own `SaveCurveDialog` checks the typed name against every existing profile name and, on a match, switches to a second stage naming the curve, how many OTHER scenes use it, and requires an explicit "Overwrite" click — Cancel/Back returns to editing with the stored curve untouched (verified live: read `storage/spectra/sequencer.json`'s `curve_profiles` before and after reaching the warning stage — byte-identical until Overwrite is actually clicked, changed correctly after). A scene switch (`useEffect` on `scene.id`) resets the open window/draft/dialog so an in-progress edit can never leak onto a different scene\'s save. **Groups/tabs — deliberately NOT built** (his own words, "in the future", "perhaps different tabs"): the tile list stays a flat array keyed by curve id, generated by one `Object.values(curves).map(...)` — grouping or tabs later is a `groupBy` over that same list feeding separate tile arrays, not a new data model or a rewrite of the grid/window split. **Phone-first verification, the actual bar this task set (the device-preview strip's own regression was the cautionary tale)**: live-eye-checked on an isolated `python -m spectra` instance on a spare port (`SPECTRA_PORT=18010`, disposable fixture `SPECTRA_STORAGE_DIR` seeded with 12 distinct curve profiles across 2 scenes — never his live `storage/spectra/`, never port 8010) with a bogus `SPOTFX_PORT` so the bridge never reached his real spot-effects process, driven via chrome-devtools-axi at a REAL 390×844 phone viewport (`resize`, not a narrowed desktop window). Confirmed: the collapsed grid scrolls (12 tiles, scrollbar engaged) with no window open; clicking a profile tile opens the window with the curve visible and no horizontal overflow introduced by any of this change (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth` measured before/after — the only overflow present on the page is pre-existing and unrelated, from `device-preview-strip`/the tab bar, confirmed by listing every off-screen element and finding none belong to this file); dragging a point live (synthetic `PointerEvent`s at real screen coordinates) flips the status line to "Editing — one-off, not yet applied" instantly; "Save as named curve…" pre-filled with the open profile's name and submitted unchanged correctly surfaced the overwrite warning naming the exact other scene using it, and the on-disk curve was provably unchanged until "Overwrite" was clicked, then correctly updated; "Apply edited one-off" was independently confirmed to rewrite only the acting scene's `inline_points` while the OTHER scene's `curve_ref` and the shared profile's own points were untouched, read directly from the fixture's `sequencer.json`. **Not room-proven** — this is UI-only, touches no device write path, and his live curve library was never opened; this row is his eye's to confirm next. Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts` topic `tab-sequencing` rewritten to match (collapsed-grid/scroll/window/one-off/overwrite-warning language, replacing the old always-expanded/Promote-button description). **Superseded 2026-08-19, card reopened (PR fm/spectra-curve-selector-rework)**: he re-clarified both halves of the SAME original ask, ruling out this row's own "collapsed grid sits in the window by default" reading and its "status flips immediately, the underlying write still waits for a click" reading — his words: "I do not want them to be expanded when I open the window... [not] a collapsed accordion he has to expand... there is a button, and pressing it pulls the selector up" (the trigger is a live `CurveThumbnail` preview button, reusing the existing thumbnail rather than a second rendering that could disagree with it — his ask, "the button should show the current preview"); and "the status change IS the apply... no apply button, no pending state" — an edit (drag/add/remove point) now writes straight to the entry's own one-off `inline_points` on the very first touch, coalesced (one request in flight, same shape `ColorSetsPage`'s live-drag preview already uses), with NO intermediate "Editing — one-off, not yet applied" text and NO separate "Apply" click. The safety property is unchanged in kind, only in timing: the auto-commit path is the exact same `attachMut(kind:"inline")` call `applyOneOff` always used, still incapable of reaching `curves[id]` — verified live (not just code-read) against an isolated `python -m spectra` + a stub `/api/color-sets` server on spare ports: editing an attached shared profile from BOTH the Sequencing tab and a colour Group's Likelihood section immediately flipped that entry's `curve_ref`→`null`/`inline_points`→the edited points while `GET /sequencer/curves` proved the shared profile's own stored points never changed; a new "↺ Revert to original" restores exactly what was attached before the edit session (including an edited already-inline curve's own pre-edit points, not just "back to inline"), also proven live. Carried to every surface the component appears on (Sequencing tab, a colour Set's card, a colour Group's card — all three eye-checked at a real 390×844 viewport). Help: `tab-sequencing` and `colorsets-likelihood-curves` both rewritten to match. |
| §66 | Fix: Ambient discarded the brightness of the colour he picked | **FIXED**, code-proven (offline, mocked bridge); **room-unproven — pending the room window, he was at his lights** | Reported live 2026-08-16 (spectra-ambient-brightness-lost): he set his dark-mode Ambient colour to a darker cream (`#8b7e53`) and the lights did not dim — "putting it in dark mode did not dim the lights so I'm wondering if that's working at all?" **Root cause**: `spectra/services/ambient.py`'s `_light_payload` built brightness from a hard-coded `AMBIENT_BRIGHTNESS_PCT = 100` constant, never from the hex colour; the bridge's xy chromaticity (`_hex_to_xy`) also discards luminance, so a darker shade of the same hue survives only as a ~0.01 xy shift — invisible on a real bulb. The read-back couldn't catch it either: `_hold_and_confirm`/`_state_matches` confirmed against the SAME hard-coded constant that had just been written, so it could never disagree with itself — the identical shape D6/§64 already named for a 2xx write, one layer up (a confirmation that can't fail is not a confirmation). **Why §52/§53's original room proof missed this**: that proof (and every Ambient proof since) exercised colour changes only as large HUE swaps — his cream to a deep blue — never two shades of the SAME hue. A hue swap moves brightness for free under a broken always-100 constant (nothing to hide the bug from, since the constant never varied to begin with) — the constant's own invariance was indistinguishable from "brightness isn't the thing being tested." A same-hue-different-lightness case is the one pairing that isolates brightness from hue and was never run. **Legacy checked first, per standing rule — and it does NOT settle this**: `services/ambient_mode.py::_light_payload` never derived brightness from colour either; `settings.ambient_brightness` (config.py, default 100) was always a wholly separate, independently-authored slider with its own UI control (`SettingsPage.tsx`'s "Brightness (%)" range input), completely orthogonal to `ambient_color`'s hex — legacy has no derivation measure to defer to, because it never derived at all. Recorded rather than discarded per the Admiral's own instruction: a standalone independent ambient-brightness slider is a real legacy control SPECTRA's ambient surface dropped entirely on the port, separate from the derivation question this row answers, and is very plausibly a control he has not yet noticed is missing — flagged for a later, separate pass, not built here. **His ruling, which does settle it** (2026-08-16, same day, his own words): "I want the brightness of the color that I choose for both ambient modes to be applied to the lights" — derive from BOTH `ambient_color` and `ambient_color_dark`, not one fixed constant, and not a new independent slider. **Which measure**: HSV Value (max RGB channel) over relative luminance or CIE L* — his own arithmetic, verified rather than adopted blind: on his two colours, HSV Value gives 96.1%/54.5%, relative luminance 71.4%/21.0%, CIE L* 87.7%/53.0%. Relative luminance was rejected because `_hex_to_xy` already hands the bridge a hue's full chromaticity, so a brightness measure that ALSO discounts for a hue's intrinsic dimness double-counts it — a saturated `#0000ff` computes to ~7% under luminance, so authoring a vivid blue would render an almost-off bulb, the exact "fights the picker" failure this fix exists to prevent. HSV Value keeps a fully-saturated colour and pure white at 100% (a colour picked at full intensity reads as authored at full intensity) and drops only as a colour is genuinely lightened/darkened — his everyday cream lands at 96% (an imperceptible ~4% dimmer than the old always-100 constant) rather than luminance's ~71% (a ~29% drop to his resting ambient he never asked for and would notice). **Fix**: `spectra/services/ambient.py` gained `_hsv_value_pct(hex)` (same `max(r,g,b)/255` formula the pre-existing release catch-up ramp's `_live_look` already used for its own brightness proxy off the live frame — one measure, not two independent reimplementations); `_light_payload`'s `brightness_pct` now defaults to `_hsv_value_pct(color_hex)` instead of the removed constant; `reconcile`'s ON path and `repair_stragglers` both derive and pass the SAME value into `_hold_and_confirm`/`_write_and_confirm`, so `_state_matches`'s read-back now confirms against the actually-intended value, not a constant that agreed with itself by construction. `AMBIENT_BRIGHTNESS_PCT` removed entirely — no RoomControlState field was added; nothing to migrate, no new UI control (both colour pickers already exist). `services/ambient_mode.py` (legacy) and the OFF/fade path (`AMBIENT_OFF_FADE_PCT`/`_fade_dim_payload`, legacy's `ambient_fade_brightness`) are untouched — the fade carries no colour target, so there is nothing for the derivation to apply to. **Tests** (`tests/test_ambient.py`): `_hsv_value_pct` unit coverage (his two colours' exact values, a saturated colour staying at 100% rather than luminance's ~7%, invalid-hex fallback), `_light_payload`'s default-derives / explicit-override behaviour, the OFF-fade path proven unaffected by the derivation, and the regression the original proof was missing — `test_reconcile_on_same_hue_different_lightness_confirms_different_brightness`: his cream and darker cream held in sequence through the real `reconcile()` → `_hold_and_confirm` → `_write_and_confirm` path against a mocked bridge, then an INDEPENDENT read-back (bypassing `_color_matches`, which deliberately ignores brightness) confirming the physical (mocked) bulb landed and was CONFIRMED at 55%, not the old 100 — a regression back to the hard-coded constant fails this test via the confirm-retry loop, not merely a stale assertion. Full suite: 544 passed, 2 pre-existing environmental failures (`test_settings_agent_cli.py`, missing `mcp` package, confirmed identical on pristine `master` before this work), 2 pre-existing skipped. **What this row does NOT establish**: brightness read back at HIS real bulbs, two different confirmed values for two colours of the same hue — the live-instrument equivalent of the offline regression test above — is deliberately not run here; he was standing at his lights mid-fix, this task's own hard limits reserve any visible live change for firstmate's room window, and the Admiral asked to be told before anything visibly changes so he can take it himself. |
| §67 | Trigger-level scene pools — a weighted subset override on the sequencer's free choice | `BUILT` (data model + kernel + engine wiring), **no UI — this was a data-and-test task by his own instruction; §67's Part 2 is a proposal awaiting his word, see OQ-11** | His own ask, verbatim: "hand them to the room sequencer, but triggers should be able to carry some meta data that can say choose from only these scenes and includes weights. Suggest best methods for displaying and containing that data. Develop a test using one of the songs with the triggers you mentioned." Read his real data before designing, per standing instruction, every number reverified fresh (`scripts/check_trigger_scene_pools.py` Part A, not assumed from the brief that named them): `storage/spectra/triggers.json` has 832 songs, 20,958 `fire_scene` / 7,163 `fire_response` / 236 `fire_scene_update` triggers, and **every one of the 20,958 `fire_scene` triggers has `scene_id: null`** — the migration already hands the choice to the sequencer for all of them, and **his hand-built scene pools are not in this file and cannot be reconstructed from it.** Legacy `storage/events.json` confirms the lead: 898 `"weight"` occurrences, all inside `scene_group_members: [{event_id, weight}]` on 9 `scene_group` events, 100% `scene_group_mode="weighted"` — a PORT of a proven legacy shape (`services/trigger_engine.py::_peek_scene_group_member`'s weighted branch: `random.choices` over raw member weights, `exclude_current` support, no curve/genre involvement), not an invention, the same weighted-draw shape §10's Colour Set Groups already ported for colour sets. **Part 1 — where the data lives**: `spectra/models/trigger.py`'s `FireSceneAction` gained one optional field, `scene_pool: Optional[list[ScenePoolMember]] = None` (`ScenePoolMember = {scene_id, weight}`), consulted ONLY when `scene_id is None`. **Absent is unconstrained by construction, not a special case**: none of his 20,958 existing triggers carry the key, so every one parses with `scene_pool=None` and reaches the exact same `_default_select_scene` kernel draw as today — proven by parsing a real trigger from his corpus unchanged (spec check B1), not merely asserted. Rejected: a shared/named "Scene Group" entity mirroring §10's `ColorSetCard(kind="group")` — that's SPECTRA_SPEC.md §2, still a `GAP`, and would need its own authoring surface/storage/resolution registry; his own words describe inline per-trigger metadata, not a new shared/reusable entity, and inline is the smaller, sufficient, no-settings-form-sprawl build. **The draw deliberately does NOT compose with the kernel's curve/genre/affinity** (`selection_kernel.select_from_scene_pool`, a pure weighted draw reusing `_draw`'s zero-veto/positive-finite-score discipline) — mirrors legacy's own weighted scene-group mode and `color_set_groups._pick_member`'s weighted branch, both pure weight draws with no curve involved; composing pool weight with curve/genre would be a genuinely new mechanism with no precedent in either codebase to port from. A pool member naming a since-deleted scene is filtered out before drawing; an empty-after-filtering or all-zero-weight pool draws nothing rather than silently falling back to the unconstrained kernel — "only these scenes" stays "only these scenes, if any remain valid," never a surprise expansion. Wired into `trigger_engine.TriggerEngine._fire`: a `scene_pool`-carrying trigger takes precedence over the kernel draw, proven at the store level (spec check B9) and at the real render pipeline (`tests/test_trigger_engine.py::test_scene_pool_narrows_and_biases_the_kernel_draw` — a scene pinned to a hard curve-veto in the sequencer config still fires every time when named in the trigger's own pool, at the trigger's own intensity, landed on the real dummy device). `spectra/api/triggers.py`'s existing scene-id-reference validation now also 422s an unknown `scene_pool` member id, matching the existing `scene_id`/`color_set_id` checks — authoring-mistake validation on the existing CRUD surface, not new UI. **Part 2 — how it should display (PROPOSAL, not built, per the task's own instruction)**: two shapes considered for a phone screen, where he's already rejected one design today for stretching off the side (§9's curve-selector precedent). **(A) an inline compact row inside the trigger's existing editor card** — a horizontal chip list, one chip per pooled scene (name + a small weight badge, e.g. "STAR ×3"), tap a chip to adjust its weight via a stepper (no free-text entry, no sideways scroll), a trailing "+" opens a scene-picker sheet reusing `FavoritesPicker.tsx`'s proven phone-safe modal shape (the same one §5's Ambient area picker and §43's device favourites already use) — bounded height, wraps rather than stretches. **(B) a separate named/shared "pool" object** mirroring §10's Colour Set Groups authoring page (`/colorsets`-style), a trigger merely referencing it by id, edited on its own full page with a list+weight-slider layout. **Recommendation: (A)** — his own words describe per-trigger metadata, not a new shared entity to author on its own page (that's the (B)/§2 Scene Groups shape, a materially bigger build nothing has asked for yet); a trigger typically needs 2–4 candidate scenes, which fits a chip row without scrolling; and it reuses an already-proven phone-safe picker pattern instead of a new full-page CRUD surface. (B) stays available later without a data-model change if he asks for pools reused across many triggers — the same shared-vs-local split `SelectorEntry.curve_ref`/`inline_points` already uses for curves. **Part 3 — the demonstration**: `scripts/check_trigger_scene_pools.py` Part C runs against "Lift Me Up" by Yes (`spotify:track:0KpvMmpUFIMVpY14ypSdBB`, his heaviest scene-change song, 125 `fire_scene` triggers, count reverified fresh) — or either of the other two heaviest songs the brief named via `--song-uri` (Starship Trooper, `3PQWdLxl8mzu5Ukt7rxHuv`, 105; Xpander, `76mh0rcSJpNhoCCqazptWm`, 104), titles confirmed against `storage/profiles/*.json`'s own `spotify_uri` field, not guessed from filenames — using his REAL trigger timestamps/intensities and his REAL 9-scene sequencer config (read-only copies; `/home/javi/SpotFX` is never written to). Three things side by side: (1) today, all 125 triggers store `scene_id: null`; (2) under the sequencer with no metadata, the real kernel draw spans 6 of his 9 real scenes across the song (Black Hole V2/STAR/Orbits V2/Dancers V2/Squiggles V2/Fireworks V2) — the baseline variety; (3) with an illustrative example weighted subset (Black Hole V2 ×3, Orbits V2 ×2, STAR ×1 — explicitly NOT a recovered version of any lost hand-built pool, labelled as such in the script's own output), every pick lands inside the named 3 scenes and still spans all 3, biased toward the higher weights (58/46/21 across 125 draws, deterministic under a fixed seed) — his variety survives, narrowed and biased exactly as asked. 24 spec checks, 0 failures, deterministic (seeded RNG) and reproducible against his real live storage on every re-run. Full suite after this change: 573 (569 passed, 2 pre-existing environmental failures — `test_settings_agent_cli.py`, missing `mcp` package, same as §66's note, unrelated to this change — 2 skipped); `tests/test_trigger_engine.py` alone went 7→8 (the new frame-level pool-precedence proof). No migration touched his 20,958 existing triggers — none needed one. PR: `fm/spectra-trigger-scene-pools`. |
| §68 | Per-item mode availability — scenes, colour sets, colour groups each get a Hybrid/Light/Dark toggle | `BUILT` (full), 22 new unit tests, phone-eye-checked (isolated instance); room-unproven | His ask 2026-08-17, verbatim logic: item="light" is available in light+hybrid, skipped in dark; item="dark" available in dark+hybrid, skipped in light; item="default"/hybrid always available — "make it a 3 flavor toggle button that stays the same size as it cycles through." New field `display_availability: "default"\|"dark"\|"light"` on `SceneV2` (`spectra/models/scene.py`) and on `ColorSetCard` — BOTH the root `models/color_set.py` model (spot-effects' own, authored via `/api/color-sets`) and its SPECTRA-side read-only projection (`spectra/services/color_sets.py`, `extra="ignore"` — needed its own copy of the field or every read silently dropped it). Deliberately a DIFFERENT field from `ColorSetCard.display_mode` (the retired dark/light "mode lane" variant-swap field, §36) — same three-value vocabulary, unrelated semantics; reusing the old field would have silently conflated a variant-selection concept with an availability-gate concept. Pure rule: `spectra/services/mode_availability.py::available_in_room_mode`. Enforcement is AUTOMATIC-selection only (an explicit human action always bypasses it, the same convention Force Scene already established): `scene_sequencer._roll` filters its scene-candidate pool before the kernel draw (never wastes a pick on an unavailable scene); `scene_sequencer._default_eligible_sets` filters the colour-set roll's candidate pool the same way; `scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id` — the ONE scene-fire choke point shared by the sequencer's own rolls AND `trigger_engine`'s `fire_scene` action — gates both the scene and the resolved colour set/group member at the top, `Force Scene`-pinned fires exempted (same "an explicit pin keeps its declared life" rule pause/dinner_party/ambient already follow); `color_set_groups._pick_member` filters a Group's member pool by each member's own availability before cycling/weighting (`resolve_for_fire_mode_gated` additionally gates the GROUP CARD ITSELF before member substitution, for the trigger-authored `select_color_set` action and `fire_scene_by_id`'s colour-set resolution); `trigger_engine._default_select_scene`/`_default_select_color_set` mirror the sequencer's own filtering for generated-trigger picks. `resolve_ref`/`POST /room-color/apply` (the manual apply surface) and manual test-fires are UNCHANGED — never mode-gated, matching every other "explicit action" exemption in this codebase. Frontend: `spectra/web/src/components/ModeAvailabilityToggle.tsx`, a fixed `width: 82px` cycling button (Hybrid → Light → Dark → Hybrid) — verified in-browser that the width never changes across all three states. Replaces the Scenes editor's "▶ Test Fire (dry)" button (his own instruction — the dry-run capability itself survives unremoved: `POST /api/scenes/{id}/fire` with `dry_run=true` is what agents already use directly, nothing lost from the system); also placed on the Colour Sets/Groups editor toolbar next to Preview (§69). Tests: `tests/test_mode_availability.py` (22, pure rule + every enforcement point above, including a `FakeScene`-duck-typing regression caught by `tests/test_fire_history.py`'s own fixture — fixed with `getattr(scene, "display_availability", "default")`, matching `accepts_color_set`'s existing defensive-access convention). Full suite: 601 collected, 598 passed (3 pre-existing/environmental — 2 `test_settings_agent_cli.py` missing `mcp` package, 1 `test_spectra_engine.py` mid-group-scenes needing seeded live storage — all three confirmed identical on pristine branch tip before this work via `git stash`), 2 pre-existing skipped. |
| §69 | Colour Set/Group editor: "Apply to room" → Preview — tap 5s/hold 60s, always reverts, live-drag updates | `BUILT` (full), 7 new frame-level tests against a real headless dummy host, browser-proven end-to-end (isolated instance + fake LedFX stub); room-unproven | His ask 2026-08-17, verbatim: tap pauses SPECTRA's automatic changes 5s while the colour applies, then reverts; hold ½s pauses up to 60s and stays until a second press, the timer, or navigating away; dragging colours during a preview updates live. **Acceptance criterion 1 (revert fidelity)**: `spectra/services/room_preview.py` snapshots via `fx_seam.get_virtuals()` and the FIRST apply write share ONE read (no window for something else to land between "read what's live" and "write over it") — mirrors `dark_light.py`'s own pre-dark snapshot/restore, the closest existing precedent for "read live, hold a still frame, replay it verbatim." Colour application reuses `scene_compiler._set_entry_by_virtual`/`_apply_set_colors` (the same pure functions `drift_conductor.apply_color_set` uses) but writes directly through `fx_seam`, NOT through the engine's executor or `drift_conductor.apply_set_directly` — a preview never touches the room's persisted `active_set_id`/wheel position/fire_history, since it must always fully revert, unlike a real "Apply." Frame-level proof: `tests/test_room_preview.py` against a REAL headless dummy host (`fx.headless`+`fx.facade`, ownership=spectra, same rig `test_dark_light.py` uses) — tap auto-reverts to the exact pre-preview config, a hold outlasts the tap window, starting a new preview reverts the old one first (proves no snapshot-stacking), a live-drag update changes the room but a later revert restores the ORIGINAL (not the dragged) colour. **Acceptance criterion 2 (the pause, all three releases)**: `spectra/services/preview_pause.py` — a module-level deadline OUTRANKING every existing deferral reason (pause/dinner_party/ambient/force_scene) at `bridge.conductor_deferral`/`sequencer_deferral`, `engine.fire_response_event`/`fire_scene_update_event`; timer release is the server-side `asyncio.create_task` auto-revert (proven); explicit release is `POST /api/room-preview/release` (second press); navigate-away is `ColorSetsPage.tsx`'s unmount effect (in-app route change, `releasePreview()`) PLUS a `beforeunload` handler using `navigator.sendBeacon` (`releasePreviewBeacon`, tab close — `fetch` isn't reliably delivered from that event) — both browser-proven: an in-app navigation to Scenes mid-hold-preview reverted the fake LedFX target within 600ms. The server-side timer is the backstop if BOTH client paths fail to land (a killed process, not just a closed tab). **Acceptance criterion 3 (write rate)**: `ColorGradientPicker`'s own `onChange` is already debounced 200ms (react-gcolor-picker); `ColorSetsPage.tsx` adds a second layer — at most ONE `/room-preview/update` in flight, a drag tick landing mid-request is COALESCED into a single follow-up call with the latest card (dropped, never queued) — proven live: editing a hex field while previewing landed exactly one changed colour on the fake LedFX target, and the pause timer/snapshot were untouched by the edit. API: `spectra/api/room_preview.py` (`POST /start {card,hold}`, `POST /update {card}`, `POST /release {}`, `GET /status`) — `card` is the editor's current DRAFT (unsaved), round-tripped through `spectra.services.color_sets.ColorSetCard`, so dragging colours never needs a Save between ticks; this also means Preview no longer force-saves first the way the old "Apply to room" did. Frontend: `spectra/web/src/queries.ts` (`startPreview`/`updatePreview`/`releasePreview`/`releasePreviewBeacon`), the existing `spectra/web/src/lib/useLongPress.ts` gesture factory (already in the codebase, used unmodified) drives tap-vs-hold on the same button. **Known pre-existing gap, NOT fixed here (out of scope for this task)**: `ColorSetsPage.tsx`'s outer layout is a fixed `260px 1fr` two-column grid with no phone-responsive redesign (unlike `ScenesPage.tsx`'s own `useIsPhone()` single-pane treatment) — at a real 390×844 viewport the editor column is squeezed and its toolbar buttons (including the new Preview/toggle) are visually cramped, though fully functional once reached. Flagged for a future pass, not expanded into here. Tests: `tests/test_room_preview.py` (7) + `tests/test_mode_availability.py`'s `test_bridge_deferral_reports_preview_first`. Full suite counts: see §68's row (same run). |
| §70 | SPA `index.html` cache-control defect — verifying what the server serves is not verifying what a given browser fetches | `FIXED` (full), PR #111 merged (`ddd2d55`), verified through the real reverse proxy against isolated instances; **not independently re-confirmed against his live `:8000`/`:8010`** | Found 2026-08-17, root-caused after his phone kept showing old behaviour on a page a task had reported finished, while `curl`ing the served bundle proved the new code was correctly built and deployed — **the check itself was aimed one step short of what mattered**: it proved what the server serves, never what a given browser will actually fetch. Root cause: both SPA mounts — `main.py`'s `/app` and `spectra/app.py`'s `/spectra`, each its own `SPAStaticFiles(StaticFiles)` subclass, not shared code — served `index.html` with `last-modified`/`etag` but **no `Cache-Control` header at all**. With no `Cache-Control`, browsers fall back to heuristic caching and can reuse a stale `index.html` for a while without ever revalidating — and a stale `index.html` names the OLD content-hashed bundle filename, so a client sitting on a cached copy never even asks whether anything changed. **Fix**, both `SPAStaticFiles.get_response` overrides: `index.html` (and the SPA-fallback response served for any unknown client route) → `Cache-Control: no-cache` (revalidate every time via etag — not "never store," so a 304 still short-circuits the body); hashed files under `/assets/` → `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (content-hashed, so a new build always produces a new filename — safe to cache forever, no revalidation needed). `spectra/services/spectra_proxy.py` passes all non-hop-by-hop response headers through unchanged, so a header set on the SPECTRA process (`:8010`) reaches clients through the spot-effects reverse proxy (`:8000`) verbatim — confirmed by reading the proxy's `_pass_headers`, not assumed. **The general lesson, now recorded in `AGENTS.md` for future deploy-verification work**: confirming a frontend deploy landed for the person looking at it needs the response's `Cache-Control` header checked, not just the bundle's content — a byte-identical `curl` of the bundle proves the server-side deploy succeeded and proves nothing about what a given already-open tab will do next. Regression coverage: `tests/test_spa_cache_headers.py`, parametrized over both mounts, proven red against the pre-fix code and green after — including a 304 revalidation round trip through the real reverse proxy against isolated instances on spare ports, never his live `:8000`/`:8010`. This is the same class of failure `docs/SPECTRA_SPEC.md`'s own maintenance history keeps re-learning: a write's/build's own success proof (a 2xx, a matching `curl`) is a snapshot of the SERVER side only — see D6 (Hue bridge 2xx ≠ applied) and AGENTS.md's "write-time confirmation is a snapshot, not a standing guarantee" entry for the same shape found in two other subsystems. |
| §71 | Room bar regrouped into three press-and-hold buttons (Mode / Ambient / Scenes) | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR #116 (`fm/spectra-topbar-grouped-buttons`), not yet merged, verified only against an isolated instance on a spare port | His ask, verbatim: "on the spectra top bar, group items that are related, like the light/dark mode and the color picker and slider. Make it just a single button that is pressed and held to expand vertically down with the additional options... Pressing 'Mode' and not holding should just cycle between the three options. Color the button based on it's mode. Don't apply change for 1s to avoid spam. Ambient should be a light bulb icon that cycles on and of on short press. Bundle transitions into scenes. Pressing scenes opens the options because it don't want it to cycle anything." `RoomControlsBar.tsx` rebuilt around three `TopBarGroupButton.tsx` instances (new, generic, reused three times) plus the standalone Brightness dimmer, which he didn't name as belonging to any group and was left ungrouped. **Mode**: short tap cycles Hybrid → Dark → Light → … instantly on screen; the button is coloured by the current mode (Light dynamically shows the actual configured `display_light_bg_color`, not a fixed swatch — literal reading of "colour the button based on its mode"); holding (~500ms, `useLongPress`, the same tap-vs-hold primitive ColorSetsPage's own Preview button already uses) opens a panel with the mode select, the Light colour picker, and the brightness slider. **Ambient**: a 💡 button; short tap toggles straight on/off (remembers the last non-off of the three settings so toggling back "on" restores it, not one fixed choice, applied immediately with no delay — the task's 1s rule was stated for Mode's cycling specifically, not this binary toggle); holding opens a panel with the three-setting select, both authored colours, `AmbientGroupsPicker` embedded directly (no separate modal — the task's own "including the Hue area selection"), and the live status badge. **Scenes**: bundles scene-change tier + Force Scene + the global transition pace (his own "bundle transitions into scenes"); every tap just opens the panel, never cycles — his own stated reason, honoured by giving Scenes `holdToExpand=false` in `TopBarGroupButton` rather than a short-press handler at all. **THE 1s DELAY, separated from the button's own responsiveness, on purpose**: `useDebouncedApply.ts` (new, generic) splits DISPLAY (synchronous `setLocal` on every tap — the button always feels instant) from APPLY (the actual `PUT /room-controls`, trailing-debounced 1000ms, re-armed on every further tap) — debouncing the button itself was rejected explicitly as the "sluggish/unresponsive" failure mode the task called out; instead only the deferred callback is delayed, so a burst of cycling sends exactly one request, for whichever mode was landed on last, after taps stop for a full second. Verified live against the isolated instance (below): two rapid taps (Dark→Light→Hybrid) produced zero PUTs during the burst and exactly one PUT 1.3s later carrying `display_mode: "default"` (Hybrid, the landed-on value) — proving both "instant display, delayed apply" and "only the landed-on mode is ever applied." The existing local-vs-server reconcile effect (`if (data && !save.isPending) setLocal(data)`) is guarded with a third condition, `!modeApply.isPending()`, so a background query refetch mid-cycle can't silently snap the button back to the old mode before the debounce fires — a gap the naive port of the pre-existing pattern would have left open. **Phone constraints, both verified as real touch, not mouse-down**: `TopBarGroupButton`'s panel is `position: fixed`, portalled to `document.body`, placed via the same viewport-clamping math `ColorGradientPicker.tsx`'s popover already uses (measure the trigger's rect, clamp `left` to stay on-screen, `top` always `rect.bottom` — never flipped upward) — proven at a real 390×844 viewport (chrome-devtools-axi, Playwright Chromium, `emulate --viewport "390x844x3,mobile,touch"`): all three panels' bounding rects stayed fully inside `[0, 390]` including the rightmost Scenes button, and `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` throughout (no page-level horizontal scroll) — the same instrument and pass/fail bar the device-preview phone-matrix fix (§43) established. The hold gesture itself was exercised with a real `PointerEvent` dispatched `pointerType: 'touch'` (not a mouse click) and correctly opened the panel. Touch-callout/selection/context-menu suppression (`.top-bar-group-btn`: `touch-action: manipulation`, `-webkit-touch-callout: none`, `-webkit-user-select: none`/`user-select: none`, plus an `onContextMenu` guard) confirmed via computed style (`touchAction: "manipulation"`, `userSelect: "none"`) and a dispatched `contextmenu` event returning `defaultPrevented: true`; `-webkit-touch-callout` itself doesn't surface through Chromium's `getComputedStyle` (Chromium doesn't implement the property at all — it's WebKit/Safari-only, which is the actual iOS target this guards) so its presence was confirmed by reading the stylesheet rule, not by a runtime read that Chromium can't give. **Room-unproven**: every check above ran against an isolated `python -m spectra` on a spare port (`SPECTRA_PORT=8110`, this worktree's own `storage/spectra`, real bridge WS read-connected to his live spot-effects `:8000` for song-state only, executor `recording` — dark, never writes to real hardware) — his actual `:8010` browser session was never touched, per this task's own hard limits. `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean; no backend change, so no pytest surface was touched by this PR. Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts`'s `room-controls-bar`/`dark-light-mode`/`ambient-hue-groups` entries rewritten to describe the grouped-button layout in place of the old flat strip. |
| §72 | An authored black `bg_color` is LOAD-BEARING in Hybrid mode — do not remove it as "redundant" | `RETIRED` (his own proposed fix, investigated then not built — superseded by a per-scene colour-set preference design instead; no data or code changed) | **Read this before touching any colour set's `bg_color`/`bg_mode`/`background_brightness`**: `storage/color_sets.json` has 30 entries across 22 colour sets (8 Black Hole cards, 7 Orbit cards + `Orbits`, 6 `Line - *` cards — full list in `scripts/check_redundant_black_backgrounds.py`) that author a black background. These look redundant — black text on a black background reads as "does nothing" — and his own first instinct, verbatim, was exactly that: *"If this can be fixed by changing color sets to not have a redundant black background and 0% brightness on the background do that instead of writing complicated code."* **It is not redundant. Removing it trades one visible bug for another.** His report was that Black Hole ignores Light mode's forced background — true, and confirmed against the real vendored render pipeline (`scripts/check_black_bg_light_mode_interaction.py`, `fx.headless`, no live storage/network): `dark_light.py`'s Light mode forces a non-black `background_color` onto a virtual's live effect config once; `scene_compiler._apply_set_colors`/`_entry_config` then sets `config["background_color"]` whenever `entry.bg_color` is truthy (additive or overwrite — the mode doesn't matter, only whether a value is authored at all), and `fx/effects/__init__.py::Effect._apply_config` partial-MERGES each write (`self._config = {**self._config, **config}`) — so any entry authoring `bg_color` re-asserts it on every fire and stomps Light's write straight back to black. **But that same stomp is also the only thing clearing a virtual's background to black on every fire of these 22 sets.** Proven in the harness (Part 2): if some earlier fire left a real, non-black colour on a virtual (another colour set's own authored background, e.g. `Calm - Purple`'s `#ff9940`, or a leftover Light-mode write not yet repainted away), stripping the black `bg_color` from Black Hole/Orbit/`Line - *` means that leftover colour now bleeds through their next fire instead of being reset — **colour bleed between scenes, in ordinary Hybrid/Default operation, not just a Light-mode edge case.** Nobody would predict this from reading the data alone — a black `bg_color` looks like dead weight sitting next to a black effect colour, and only running the actual write-merge-then-render path against a prior non-black state surfaces that it's doing real, protective work. **Dark mode is genuinely unaffected** — `dark_lock`'s clamp forces black on every write path regardless of what's authored, so this warning is Hybrid/Default-specific. **What actually happened**: this was investigated as a candidate one-line data cleanup and came within one step of being dispatched as exactly that — "sounds obviously safe" was the working assumption going in. It was not safe; the Admiral has since replaced the underlying idea with a different, per-scene colour-set preference design instead, so nothing was lost by not shipping the data edit — but the reason the data edit itself was unsafe is the durable part, and it will apply again to any future data cleanup that looks at these 30 entries and calls them redundant. His authored `storage/color_sets.json` was never written — verified by md5 against a snapshot taken before this investigation began. Both scripts are kept, read-only, re-runnable rather than a claim in a document: `scripts/check_redundant_black_backgrounds.py` (names the 30 entries) and `scripts/check_black_bg_light_mode_interaction.py` (reproduces the equivalence proof — Dark identical, Hybrid not — against the real pipeline on demand). |
| §73 | Sonic — parameter discovery + a deterministic "did it work" line (two defects from his first real flare) | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-sonic-param-discovery, not yet merged, verified only offline + isolated instances | His two reports from the same first attempt (creating "Reverse Direction" on "Fireworks V2," which silently succeeded, `flare_kind_created`, backed up): **(1)** "Sonic needs visibility into parameters and such and what they do, we need to maybe use the help file and give sonic access to it"; **(2), the one he actually complained about**: "after I gave it the parameter name it worked for a moment and then spit out what looks like a long JSON file level of text into the console. I don't know if it actually completed the task or if it just gave me the output but I didn't need to see that output." **Defect 1 — parameter blindness.** `set_flare_kind`'s `params` field had no discovery surface at all; he had to already know the exact name `reverse` before Sonic could act. Built the catalogue his own architecture ruling requires ("the enumerated operation set that ENFORCES the boundary and the catalogue that lets Sonic DISCOVER what to change must be THE SAME DECLARATION") but for PARAMETERS this time, not operations: `fx/device_model.py` gains `param_descriptions(effect_type)` — reads the vendored effect's OWN `CONFIG_SCHEMA` live (`cls.schema()`, the same MRO-merging technique `scripts/backfill_param_defaults.py` already uses to read baked defaults) and returns each param's voluptuous `description=` text verbatim — and `param_catalogue(effect_type)`, which merges that with the existing shared registry's type/min/max/options/default (`config/effect_params.json` via `effect_params()`, the data every write path already validates against). **Neither half is hand-typed**: type/range come from the registry every other consumer already trusts; the "what it DOES in his language" text is read fresh off the real effect class on every call (module-level cache, cleared by `refresh()`) — a future vendor edit to a param's description changes the catalogue for free, with nothing else to update, closing exactly the "confidently describe a parameter that no longer behaves that way" risk his ask named. Verified against the concrete case: `param_descriptions("fireworks")["reverse"]` reads *"Implode: particles converge onto the burst point instead of exploding away"* — the real meaning of the `reverse=1.0` he set, sourced from `fx/effects/fireworks.py`'s own schema, never previously surfaced anywhere. Two new `SonicOperation`s in `spectra/services/scene_console.py` (its own domain, no cross-import): `list_scene_params(scene_id)` — cheap, names only, grouped by the distinct effect types the scene's devices actually run (base + any ⚡ effect-step variants, since a flare kind's params are name-broadcast to whatever effect carries the name, not scoped by device target) — and `get_param_info(effect_type, name)` — full detail for exactly ONE named parameter, per his own token-efficiency requirement ("query it NARROWLY... must not load the manual every turn"): the system prompt tells Sonic to call the index once, then the detail call once per param it's about to set, never a bulk dump. Both hand-added to `spectra/services/settings_mcp_server.py` (that file is deliberately hand-maintained, one wrapper per `ALL_OPERATIONS` entry, not generated — confirmed by the existing regression test that lists the live MCP tool manifest and asserts it matches `ALL_OPERATIONS` exactly; the CLI backend's synthetic transcript fixtures' recorded `tools` manifests were updated to include both new names for the same reason). **Defect 2 — the ambiguous JSON dump, root-caused to two independent places, both fixed.** First, `SYSTEM_PROMPT` itself told the model to "relay the tool result's own `preview` field back to him... quote it rather than re-describing it from memory" — `preview` is `_diff_scenes()`'s structured `{field: {before, after}}` dict, and a scene that already has flare kinds diffs the WHOLE `flare_kinds` list wholesale on any kind edit (`_diff_scenes`'s own documented "nested structure changed → show the whole field" simplicity trade-off) — "quote it" was read literally, producing exactly the JSON-blob-in-chat he saw. Rewritten: the model is now told every reply ends in ONE short plain-language sentence — the tool result's own new `summary` field (applied) or `reason` field (rejected) — and is explicitly forbidden from pasting/quoting a tool result's raw JSON/dict/`preview` shape. Second, independently, `spectra/web/src/lib/sonicPreview.ts`'s `fmtValue()` — the frontend's OWN dedicated preview renderer, used by both `SonicChatPopover.tsx` and `SettingsConsolePage.tsx` — did `JSON.stringify(v)` for any object/array value, so even a disciplined model's reply would have sat next to a raw dump the instant the preview message rendered; fixed to summarize (item count, or names when entries carry one) instead of stringifying, proven by a new backend test asserting a third flare-kind create on an already-two-kind scene keeps its `summary` under 80 characters with no `{`/`[` in it while the underlying `preview.flare_kinds.after` still genuinely holds all 3 (the real data is still there for the dedicated diff view — only the raw dump is gone). **The deterministic summary line itself**: every `apply_*` in `scene_console.py` and `settings_console.py`'s `apply_change` now stamps a `summary` string built from the same structured fields already in the entry (`Created scene "X".` / `Set Entry blend to 1500 on "X".` / `Created flare kind "Reverse Direction" on "X".` / etc.) — never re-derived by the model. `settings_agent.run_turn()` (and the CLI backend's `_parse_transcript()`, which shares the same tool surface) now also collects rejected write attempts into a new `rejected` list alongside `changes` — a refusal's own `reason` is already plain language (`SceneOpError`/`SettingChangeError.payload()`), so "if it failed, that says so just as plainly" needed only surfacing it structurally, not a second field. Both frontends render a new, visually distinct `.settings-console-msg-status` chat message — ✓/✗ prefixed, built from `summary`/`reason`, always shown regardless of what the model's own reply text says — immediately after every turn with any write attempt, so the "did it work" answer is never something he has to infer from prose or a diff. **Constraints honoured**: read-only against his live `:8010` throughout (no scene fired, no restart, no deploy); Sonic was never run against his real scenes/storage — every test uses `tmp_path`-isolated storage, matching the existing `test_scene_console.py`/`test_settings_console.py` fixture convention; his 9 real authored scenes were never targeted. Tests: 13 new (`tests/test_scene_console.py`, sections 10–11) — parameter discovery (index, detail, both error paths, a live-schema-edit test proving the description genuinely cannot go stale independent of any cache), and the summary/rejection line (every write op's summary text, the JSON-dump regression specifically, and `run_turn`'s new `rejected` list) — plus 7 existing CLI-backend fixture files updated to include the 2 new tool names in their recorded manifests (additive only, no test logic changed). Full suite 620 passed, 2 skipped (pre-existing live-model smoke tests), 8 pre-existing environmental failures unrelated to this change (`test_crystal_activation_verify.py` ×3 — needs seeded live storage; `test_spa_cache_headers.py` ×5 — needs a frontend build; both confirmed failing identically on pristine `master` before this change). `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean. **Room-unproven**: no live Sonic call was made against either backend (no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in this build environment, and the task's own constraint was read-only against his live instance) — the fix is proven at the mechanism layer (the real `_dispatch()`/`run_turn()` code paths, real `CONFIG_SCHEMA` reads, real frontend formatter logic) and via `tsc`/`vite build`, not by watching Sonic actually answer "what does reverse do" or confirm a flare kind in his own chat window. |
| §74 | Per-scene PREFERRED COLOUR SET MODE | `BUILT` (mechanism + four scenes wired), inert until colour sets are marked — room-unproven, verified only against an isolated instance/local storage snapshot | His ask, verbatim: "Scenes can have a mode and they can also have a preferred color set mode. black hole would prefer dark mode color sets. This means you don't have to change any color sets. We just set black hole, fireworks, and Dancer to having a preference for dark mode color sets. So they don't run light mode color sets unless the system is set to light mode." This is the per-scene design §72 says superseded his own proposed "remove the redundant black backgrounds" fix — that data edit was investigated, found unsafe (colour bleed between scenes), and retired in favour of this build instead; his authored colour-set data was never touched by either investigation. A follow-up confirmed a SECOND scene, "Black Hole V2 UI", gets the same preference — four scenes, not three. **A second, deliberately separate axis from §67's per-item mode AVAILABILITY** (`SceneV2.display_availability`, whether an item plays at all): `SceneV2.preferred_color_set_mode` (`"default"\|"dark"\|"light"`, `spectra/models/scene.py`) decides WHICH colour sets a scene draws from once it does play — not the same control, per his own instruction to keep the two apart. Resolution: `spectra/services/mode_availability.color_set_preferred(card_availability, scene_preference, room_mode)` — no preference matches everything; a declared preference matches every set marked the same way PLUS every unmarked ("default") set (his own ask was additive, "you don't have to change any color sets"); it excludes only a set marked the OPPOSITE mode. **Hybrid vs explicit, decided and justified**: he stated only one direction (system Light overrides a Dark preference) — generalised symmetrically here (explicit Dark also overrides a Light preference) so neither direction can strand a scene with zero eligible sets; preference is therefore consulted ONLY while the room is Hybrid, and an explicit Dark or Light room mode uses the AVAILABILITY-filtered pool as-is, preference or not. Wired at the one existing choke point that already applies availability to a colour-set pool, `scene_sequencer._default_eligible_sets` (the sequencer's own automatic colour-set roll) — deliberately not extended to `drift_conductor`'s destination pool or `scene_response.py`'s flare colour-jump pool, neither of which apply mode AVAILABILITY there either today (checked, not assumed); a manual apply/preview/test-fire bypasses preference the same way it already bypasses availability. **Verified himself, not just measured once**: his real `color_sets.json` has 0 of 50 kind="set" cards carrying a non-default `display_availability` (0 of 58 including groups) and 0 carrying a `dark_variant`/`light_variant` — so on the day this ships, every one of the four scenes' preference matches nothing marked and THE FALLBACK IS FULL, UNFILTERED SELECTION, proven (not merely asserted) by `tests/test_color_set_preference.py::test_preference_never_produces_an_empty_pool_when_nothing_is_marked` running all four scenes' actual `dark` preference against a 50-set unmarked library, in every room mode, and asserting the pool stays the full 50 — an empty pool here would have gone four of his scenes dark the moment this deployed. **No second marking system built**: the existing Mode availability toggle on a Colour Set (`ColorSetsPage.tsx`, §67) is the one honest marking surface preference reads — his answer to his own original Black Hole complaint is therefore "not yet, until some colour sets are marked Light or Dark there" — the mechanism alone does not change what he sees; `ColorSetsTab.tsx` now also shows each set's own marking read-only (☾/☀) so marking is visible from the scene side while deciding a preference. **"Black Hole V2 UI" — asked, not assumed, per the task's own instruction**: it is structurally thinner than "Black Hole V2" (targets a Particles device entry vs Matrix, noticeably fewer flare kinds once loaded, and no other storage file references its scene id) — flagged to the Admiral before touching it; he confirmed it gets the preference too. `scripts/set_scene_colorset_preference.py` (dry-run default, `--apply`) sets the four scenes' preference — DELIBERATELY a raw-JSON-dict patch of exactly the one key, not a `scene_store.save()` round-trip: an earlier draft that went through `SceneV2.model_dump_json()` was caught (before merge) silently rewriting each scene's legacy flare-band shorthand into canonical `flare_kinds` storage — a far bigger change than "set one field" — reverted in favour of the surgical patch, backed up to `storage/spectra/backups/scenes-preference-<stamp>.json` before writing; `tests/test_set_scene_colorset_preference.py` locks the byte-identical-except-one-key property down so it can't regress silently again. Not run against his live `:8010` storage by this build — per this task's hard limits, that is an operator/deploy step, same as `seed_star_strips.py`'s own convention. Spec: `tests/test_color_set_preference.py` (22 tests) + `tests/test_set_scene_colorset_preference.py` (6 tests). Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts`'s new `scene-colorset-preference` entry, cross-linked from `mode-availability` and `colorsets-groups-page`. |
| §75 | STAR's `star` jerks on every ordinary flare — measured, not assumed; fix eases dice re-rolls the registry already marks smooth | `FIXED` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-star-jerky-parameter, not yet merged, verified offline + against his real live scene data read-only | His report, verbatim: *"i'm watching the effect star play and it is very jerky when it switches the amount of star the parameter. It should be smooth I think some flares need to be looked [at]."* His own guess (flares) was investigated rather than assumed — it is directionally correct but not because a flare is broken; a flare doing exactly what it's built to do is the cause. **Mechanism, confirmed against his real, live STAR scene** (read via `GET /spectra/api/scenes/{id}`, not the stale local worktree copy of `storage/spectra/scenes.json` — see the caveat below): `star` is a `signal="random", mode="steps"` binding, `sticky=false` (unlike `edges` on the same Matrix device, fixed sticky=true per §56/OQ-6 — confirmed still correctly deployed live, not the regression a first pass over a stale local snapshot suggested). STAR's `responses.flare` block has **`reroll_dice: true` spanning the full `[0,1]` intensity range with no gap**, so `models.scene._migrate_flare_kinds` auto-attaches "Dice Re-roll" to *every* flare band — meaning **every ordinary flare, at any intensity, re-rolls `star`.** Live-measured flare cadence during an actively playing track (`GET /spectra/api/engine/status`'s `responses.recent_surges`, 10 consecutive real surges): **roughly 0.6–1.2s apart** — not inferred, timestamped. Every re-roll landed via `scene_response.ResponseEngine._reroll` → `executor.jump()`, a 1ms tween the engine lands on the next render frame — an instant snap, unconditionally, regardless of whether the target param could be interpolated. That combination (re-rolls ~once a second, each an instant snap between `{0.3, -0.3, 0.0}`) is the jerk, reproduced from live telemetry, not assumed from his description. **The three-cause question, answered with evidence**: (1) TRUE — the write path is a hard jump where a glide was available and simply unused; (2) TRUE — a flare (the "Dice Re-roll" kind specifically) does fire repeatedly and re-set it, at a measured ~1Hz; (3) FALSE for `star` — `fx/effects/radial.py`'s `star` param is a genuine continuous float (`vol.Coerce(float)`, `[-1,1]`) driving a continuous ripple formula (`ripple = 1 + star * cos(edges * angle)`), read fresh every frame with zero internal smoothing of its own — nothing stops it being glided from the outside, and the codebase's tween engine (`fx/effects/__init__.py::start_param_transitions`, already reached by every colour/gain/phase/brightness glide in this module) is already fully capable. `edges`, by contrast, genuinely is steppy — `vol.Coerce(int)`, `[0,8]`, used as an actual polygon vertex count (`np.pi*2/edges`) — smoothing it would mean crossfading two renders, not interpolating a number; moot for this bug regardless, since it's already sticky and not being re-rolled. **A pre-existing, unused signal for exactly this split was already sitting in the data**: `config/effect_params.json` already tags `star: {"type": "numeric", "smooth": true}` and `edges: {"type": "integer", "smooth": false}` — someone had already encoded "star should ease, edges shouldn't" in the param registry; nothing in `spectra/`/`fx/` Python ever read the `smooth` key before this fix (grep-confirmed). **Fix**: `scene_response.py::_reroll` now splits its re-rolled targets by this exact flag — a param the registry marks `smooth` (and whose binding-resolver kind is numeric, never toggle/string/integer) lands via a new `glides` dict and `executor.glide(..., DICE_REROLL_GLIDE_MS=220)` instead of `jumps`/`executor.jump()`; everything else is unchanged. brightness/background_brightness (always genuine floats) glide unconditionally, matching how every other brightness write in this module already lands. **Precedence preserved**: an explicit param-patch kind on the same event (e.g. STAR's own "Flare patch 0.7–1", which pins `star` to `0.0` at high intensity) still wins over a same-event dice re-roll and still lands as an instant jump — any dice-only glide target a later patch also claims is dropped before either write is issued, so the pre-existing "reroll → patch" precedence (patch always wins) is bit-for-bit unchanged, only re-derived cleanly instead of via incidental same-dict overwrite ordering. No scene data was touched — this is a pure engine-mechanism fix; `star`'s stored binding (values, dice letter, fallback) is untouched, it still re-rolls on every flare, it just eases there now. 220ms was chosen to sit comfortably inside the measured ~0.6–1.2s gap between ordinary flares (still reads as a flare accent, not a slow fade) — not intensity-scaled like the colour-jump ramp, since a dice roll carries no magnitude to key a ramp off of (module docstring, unchanged). **Scene-wide audit** (his explicit ask — "check the other stepped parameters too"), run against his real 9 live scenes via the same read API, not the stale local snapshot: exactly **2** non-sticky `signal=random, mode=steps` bindings exist anywhere — STAR's `star` (this fix) and Orbits V2's Matrix `reverse` (a `toggle`, not numeric — a direction flip is inherently binary, "smooth" isn't a coherent ask for it, and it's untouched by this fix; flagged for the record, not fixed, since it wasn't reported and a binary flip has no continuous state to ease through). Verified offline against the real vendored render pipeline and his real scene shape (no live write, no restart): `scripts/check_spectra.py` — new/updated checks for the star/edges authored-pair split (star eases, edges still jumps, in the same fire), the `blur`/`reactivity` stepped-effect re-roll case (also registry `smooth=true`, also now eases), and an extended `_no_flare_moves_star_edges` proof firing flare@0.1/0.5/0.97 + drop@0.97 against his real, live STAR scene data (copied read-only from the live app dir's `storage/spectra/scenes.json` into this worktree, refreshing the same gitignored, untracked path this script's live-storage sections always read — see the caveat below) confirming: low/mid-band re-rolls of `star` glide (never jump), the 0.7–1 band's explicit patch still jumps (unchanged precedence), and `edges` still never appears in any executor write. Full suite: **623 passed, 5 failed, 2 skipped** (630 collected) — all 5 failures confirmed pre-existing and identical on unmodified `master` in this same environment (`git stash` + rerun): 3× `test_crystal_activation_verify.py` (needs seeded live device topology) + 2× `test_settings_agent_cli.py` (missing optional `mcp` package in this venv). `check_spectra.py`'s own "raw seeder output" section also fails identically on unmodified `master` with the same fresh live storage (needs the legacy `storage/scenes_v2.json` S1-seeder input, out of scope, matches the documented environmental-failures pattern) — every check before and after that point, including every new one this fix adds, passes; the checks after it were verified via an isolated standalone reproduction of the same proof body rather than the full script, since a straight-through script exits on first failure. **A caveat worth keeping**: this worktree's own `storage/spectra/scenes.json` (gitignored, untracked) was an Aug-13 snapshot that predated the §56 sticky fix — a first pass reading it looked like `edges`' fix had regressed live. It hadn't; the live process at `:8010`, read fresh over the network per this task's "a worktree isolates the filesystem, not the network," had the correct, already-fixed `edges` binding. Local `storage/spectra/*.json` in a worktree is not proof of current live scene state — read the live API (or copy fresh from the live app dir, read-only, the way this fix's own verification did) before trusting a local snapshot for anything dated. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired and nothing was written to live storage or hardware — his STAR scene was not playing when this was investigated (the room was mid-show on a different scene), and per this task's own instruction his eyes were on the room, so no surprise change was driven; the fix is proven at the mechanism layer (the real `_reroll`/`executor` code paths, against his real scene's real binding/flare-kind shapes) and via the executable specs, not by watching the fixture play smooth in his room. |
| §76 | Black Hole spawns blobs invisibly on the crystal (round 1) then too far inside the visible edge (round 2) — infall-mode spawn location moved from the rim's near-zero-density corner band to a hit-rate-maximizing annulus to a direction-dependent boundary that follows the hex silhouette itself | `FIXED` (full), room-unproven — round 1 PR fm/spectra-blackhole-hex-spawn merged as #123 (`d7a0d79`); round 2 PR fm/spectra-blackhole-spawn-at-edge, not yet merged, both verified offline against the real vendored render pipeline and the real device profile | His report, verbatim: *"in the black hole effect the diameter for where the blobs spawn is too large, shrink it so that they are visible almost immediately after they spawn on the hex-shaped grid."* Established first, per his own instruction, whether the effect knows anything about the gap cells at all: it does not, anywhere — `fx/effects/twod.py`'s `r_width`/`r_height` are always the FULL addressable rectangle (`72x37` for `crystal-mapper`), and nothing under `fx/` reads `storage/device_profiles/` (grep-confirmed; only the offline `tools/gifsmith/`/`tools/dancesmith/` asset tools do) — Blackhole renders purely in normalized `(r, theta)` space, blind to which cells are real light vs the dummy `gap-crystal-mapper` device. Measured his real crystal-mapper geometry (`storage/device_profiles/crystal-mapper.json`, `scripts/check_blackhole_hex_spawn.py`): real-pixel density is a flat **50%** for `r<=0.85` (the hex-lattice checkerboard interior) and collapses to 44% at `r=0.90`, 19% at `r=1.0`, 0% past `r≈1.2` — a cliff at the hexagon's own boundary, not a gradient. His live "Black Hole V2" scene (Matrix/crystal-mapper, `storage/spectra/scenes.json`) uses `reverse=false` (infall — the classic black-hole pull), whose spawn annulus was a hardcoded `(0.90, 1.05)` — almost entirely inside that near-zero-density band: only 26% of spawns landed on a real pixel, so a fresh blob was typically invisible until it had fallen most of the way to the horizon. **Which fix, and why, per his own instruction not to just shrink a number**: `radius_scale` is Blackhole's existing user-facing knob and is literally documented `"Spawn radius as a fraction of the panel edge"` — but it actually scales `sx`/`sy`, driving spawn, travel, kill radius, the rendered disc and corner-exit uniformly; turning it down would have shrunk the whole effect and abandoned most of the crystal's real reach (a uniform shrink, explicitly rejected). The actual fix pulls the previously-unexposed, hardcoded spawn annulus itself inward to new `SPAWN_ANNULUS_MIN/MAX = (0.70, 0.85)` constants (`fx/effects/blackhole.py`, `fx/VENDOR.md` deviation #12) — inside the flat 50% interior (a Monte-Carlo real-hit-rate check against the live device profile: 50.0%, up from 26.0%), while leaving `radius_scale`/`sx`/`sy` untouched, so the fall from spawn down to the horizon/center still spans nearly the whole panel exactly as before.
