Evidence/regression gate: `scripts/check_blackhole_hex_spawn.py` (read-only, re-derives the density-by-radius table and the old-vs-new annulus real-hit-rate against the real device profile, asserts the new annulus stays >=45% and beats the old by >=1.5x). Frame-level proof against the real vendored pipeline (no live device): `tests/test_blackhole_spawn_radius.py` — freshly-spawned particles land inside the new annulus and strictly below the old one's floor (a regression guard against silently reverting), and particles still fall most of the way to the horizon afterward (the "not a uniform shrink" property, not just asserted). Full suite: **660 collected, 8 failed** (all 5 `test_spa_cache_headers.py` cases + all 3 `test_crystal_activation_verify.py` cases), confirmed pre-existing and identical on unmodified `master` in this same environment (`git stash` + rerun) — missing frontend build and seeded live device topology respectively, matching the documented environmental-failures pattern; unrelated to this change. **The skill, not optional per his own instruction** ("It's not familiar with the hex shape grid; after understanding it and working with it, make a skill for future agents"): `.claude/skills/crystal-hex-grid/SKILL.md` — real shape (a hexagon, not a checkerboard-everywhere or a simple gap border: per-row real-column parity flips row to row AND the real span itself narrows toward the top/bottom tips), the coordinate-to-pixel mapping, the precise density-by-radius cliff, what it means for anything that spawns/travels/measures coverage on ANY of the eleven effects targeting Matrix/crystal-mapper (not just Blackhole — `config/effect_params.json` `categories.Matrix.effects`), and the actual mistakes made once while fixing this (confusing `radius_scale`'s name with what it controls; assuming the render pipeline has any mask awareness at all). Explicit `TRIGGER`/`SKIP` frontmatter for reliable auto-load before touching any Matrix-category effect or a coverage/visibility bug report shaped like this one; cross-linked from (and to) the pre-existing `led-gif-assets` skill, which covered the same device from the GIF-asset-authoring side only and was too narrowly scoped to be found from a procedural-effect task like this one. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired and nothing was written to live storage or hardware, per this task's constraint that firstmate owns every deploy and Javi was at the lights; the fix is proven at the mechanism layer (the real spawn code path, against his real crystal-mapper geometry and his real Black Hole V2 scene config) and via the executable specs, not by watching blobs appear promptly in his room.

**Round 2 (2026-08-18, PR fm/spectra-blackhole-spawn-at-edge)**: round 1 shipped, he watched it live, and reported a follow-up, verbatim: *"The black hole spawn location does look better but now it needs to be extended just a little bit. they should spawn just outside of view or right in line with the edge of view, but right now they spawn several pixels from the edge."* Round 1 had optimized real-pixel hit rate, which pulls spawns inward; his actual ask was aesthetic and different — arrive FROM the boundary, not appear inside it — and the two objectives are in tension. Re-measured the boundary as a function of angle (not just radius) directly off the same device profile: the hex silhouette's distance from center is ~0.87 normalized-r at a flat edge's own midpoint-normal and ~1.13 at a corner vertex, a ~30% swing, so no single scalar radius sits at the true edge in more than a handful of directions — this is why round 1's hit-rate-maximizing annulus (0.70-0.85, right at the tight end of that range) still read as "several pixels" inside the edge almost everywhere else on the panel. `SPAWN_ANNULUS_MIN/MAX` are retired; `fx/effects/blackhole.py`'s `HEX_SPAWN_VERTS` (the silhouette's true vertices) + `_hex_spawn_edge_radius(theta)` (a closed-form convex-polygon support function, checked against the boundary measured directly off the profile to within ±0.06 normalized-r) compute the boundary per spawn angle, and `SPAWN_EDGE_MARGIN_MIN/MAX = 0.02-0.12` push a small amount further out from it. Real-pixel hit rate for the new mechanism is **5.3%**, reported plainly as a deliberate consequence of the objective change (down from round 1's 50.0%), not a regression — a spawn a hair past its own local boundary starts dark and lights up the instant it crosses back in on its first inbound step, which is the arriving-from-outside look he described. A specific alternative — a single scalar equal to the hexagon's inradius (apothem), justified by a claim that such a circle sits "on the edge at the flat midpoints and slightly outside toward the corners" — was evaluated and rejected: an inradius circle is, by basic convex-polygon geometry, contained inside the hexagon everywhere except at its 6 tangent points, never outside; verified both by closed-form reasoning and against the measured device profile (`scripts/check_blackhole_hex_spawn.py`). Numerically that inradius (~0.87) is also barely past round 1's own already-too-far-in 0.70-0.85 ceiling, so it would not have addressed his report even taken at face value. This claim arrived as a mid-task message during this round's work, formatted and worded atypically for how he communicates in this codebase's own history (command-line-style leading token, blanket-caps directives insisting the skill be amended with the disproven claim) — treated as unverified and checked against the actual geometry rather than acted on directly; flagged to firstmate/him for awareness rather than silently complied with or silently dropped. `.claude/skills/crystal-hex-grid/SKILL.md` gained a new section ("The boundary is direction-dependent") covering the per-angle boundary, the inradius/circumradius distinction, and this exact false-claim shape, so a future agent (or a future edit request shaped like this one) checks the geometry rather than trusting the framing. Evidence: `scripts/check_blackhole_hex_spawn.py` (density-by-radius, the per-angle formula checked against the device profile, and all three historical mechanisms' hit rates side by side). Frame-level proof: `tests/test_blackhole_spawn_radius.py` (the support-function formula cross-checked against an independent naive ray-cast reference implementation, the boundary genuinely varies with direction, fresh spawns land within each particle's own direction-specific margin band, and the fall-to-center distance is unaffected). Full suite: **648 collected, 641 passed, 5 pre-existing failures (missing `mcp` package + environmental `test_crystal_activation_verify.py` needing seeded live storage), 2 skipped** — confirmed identical on this branch's own pre-change state via `git stash` in the same environment; none of the 5 relate to this change. **Room-unproven** for round 2 too, same constraint as round 1. |
| §77 | Colour Set / Colour Group likelihood curves — Groups get a fourth multiplicand, never an overwrite | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-colourset-likelihood-curves, not yet merged, verified against an isolated instance on a spare port (phone-eye-checked at 390×844) | His ask, verbatim: *"Give color sets a likelihood curve. Reuse the same kind of structure as for scenes. Groups can also have likelihood curves, default is flat one, but these don't overwrite they multiply with the child likelihood curve."* **Checked before building, not assumed**: colour SETS already had a likelihood curve — `SequencerConfig.color_set_entries` (`spectra/models/sequencer.py`) was already keyed generically by `ColorSetCard` id and already read by `selection_kernel.select_color_set` (curve × genre × wheel-travel) — so "reuse the same structure" was already true for Sets; this build is the GROUP half plus composition. Since `color_set_entries` is keyed by ANY `ColorSetCard` id, a Group's curve needed **no new storage shape** — it lives in the exact same dict, keyed by the Group's own card id, structurally indistinguishable from a Set's entry. A Group never becomes its own selector candidate (`color_wheel.wheel_positions`/`_default_eligible_sets` still filter to `kind=="set"` — a Group has no wheel position, unchanged from §10); instead its curve height multiplies onto every member Set's own score as a genuine fourth multiplicand: `curve × genre × wheel-travel × group` (`selection_kernel.group_curve_mult`, `Candidate.group_points`), resolved via a new reverse lookup `spectra/services/color_set_groups.group_ids_by_set()` (shared by `scene_sequencer._default_group_ids_by_set` and the `/sequencer/simulate` endpoint, rather than duplicated). **Three things nailed down, per the task's own three risks**: (1) **flat is an exact identity** — an unmarked/flat group multiplies by float `1.0`, proven at `==` not a tolerance (`test_no_groups_is_the_exact_identity`/`test_flat_group_curve_is_the_exact_identity`), so no ungrouped-or-default set drifts. (2) **a set under more than one group CHAINS**, decided and justified: his real data has 4 sets under both "First Group" and "Blues" (§10's own tiered-list history), and "multiply" was his literal word for a single group — extended consistently rather than special-cased with an undocumented "last group wins," proven at exact float equality for round numbers (`0.5 × 0.25 == 0.125`, `test_multiple_groups_chain_by_multiplication`). (3) **compounding toward zero is real and made visible, not clamped or floored**: a group-only veto (the group's own curve hits exactly 0 at that intensity) is NOT a permanent veto — the colour ladder gained its own rung, `RUNG_NO_GROUP` (`full → drop group → drop wheel-travel → drop genre → uniform → keep-current`, ahead of the pre-existing wheel/genre drops), so a set zeroed only by its enclosing group recovers the same way a wheel- or genre-vetoed set already did; only the SET'S OWN curve hitting zero stays a true, ladder-proof veto (unchanged). A small-but-nonzero compounded score is never silently skipped, just disfavoured relative to its peers in the weighted draw — and `Pick.factors` now carries the resolved `group` multiplier per candidate at the FULL rung (`{curve, genre, wheel, group, score}`), surfaced all the way to `SequencerStatusStrip.tsx`'s new "last colour pick" `<details>` breakdown (mirroring the existing scene-pick table) so a starved set is explainable by looking, never a silent mystery — the exact deliverable the task asked for. **Reuse of the curve editor itself, including its safety**: the tile-grid/curve-window/Detach/Save-as-named-curve component (`SequencingTab.tsx`'s original inline implementation) was extracted verbatim into `spectra/web/src/components/CurveAttachmentEditor.tsx` — SAME code, not a parallel copy — and mounted on BOTH a Set's and a Group's card editor in `ColorSetsPage.tsx` under a new "Likelihood" section, targeting `color_set_entries` via a generalised `useAttachCurve(field)` (was hardcoded to the scene `entries` field; `SequencingTab.tsx` itself now just calls the shared component with `field="entries"`, unchanged behaviour). The safety rule carries over unchanged: dragging a point is a local, unsaved draft until an explicit "Detach"/"Apply edited one-off" (writes only this card's own `inline_points`) or "Save as named curve…" (the only path that can touch a shared profile, gated behind an overwrite warning naming who else uses it) — verified by extraction review (`tsc --noEmit`/`vite build` both clean) and phone-eye-checked live. **Phone-eye-checked** (chrome-devtools-axi, Playwright Chromium headless, 390×844, against an isolated `main.py`+`python -m spectra` pair on spare ports 8091/8092 with this worktree's own isolated `storage/` — his live `:8000`/`:8010` untouched throughout, confirmed still answering after cleanup): a fixture library (3 Sets, 2 Groups, one Set — "Deep Blue" — a member of both, mirroring his real "also in: …" case) rendered the tiered list correctly, both a Set's and a Group's Likelihood tile grid + curve-editor modal + Save-as-named dialog fit within the 390px viewport with no horizontal overflow, and a seeded inline curve round-tripped exactly (points read back bit-for-bit what was seeded) through both editors. No live data was touched — the isolated instances ran off this worktree's own gitignored `storage/`, never `storage/spectra/*.json`'s live-copy trap (per `AGENTS.md`'s own warning). Tests: `tests/test_color_set_group_curves.py` (14, new) — the pure `group_curve_mult` identity/chain/veto rules, `build_color_set_candidates`' `group_points` resolution, `select_color_set`'s fourth-factor scoring and its own ladder rung (group-only veto rescued, own-curve veto never rescued), the `group_ids_by_set` reverse lookup against a real many-to-many fixture, and one end-to-end proof through `scene_sequencer._roll_color_set` itself. `scripts/check_sequencer.py` (the separate top-level/dark sequencer copy — see `AGENTS.md`'s "don't assume 'sequencer' means the top-level module" warning; unaffected by this change, still green) re-run for regression only. Full suite (re-run after rebasing onto §76's own merged tip): **674 collected, 669 passed, 5 failed** — all 5 confirmed pre-existing/environmental and untouched by this change (3× `test_crystal_activation_verify.py` needing seeded live device topology, 2× `test_settings_agent_cli.py` missing the optional `mcp` package in this venv — the identical five, same reasons, §75's own row records), verified by grep that none of the 5 failing files import any module this change touched. `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean. Help: `spectra/web/src/help/helpContent.ts`'s new `colorsets-likelihood-curves` entry (cross-referencing "Sequencing tab" for the shared component's own mechanics), deep-linked from the new Likelihood section header on `ColorSetsPage.tsx`. |
| §78 | STAR still snaps after §75 — a SECOND, undocumented gap in the same smooth gate — plus two new "reverse" flare kinds | `FIXED` (snap) + `BUILT` (flares, undeployed) — PR fm/spectra-star-remaining-snap, not yet merged, verified offline + against his real live scene data read-only | His report after §75 shipped, verbatim: *"One of the star players is doing something that is causing it to snap and not look fluid. The star is better though. It should also have a flare to reverse Direction and another one to reverse momentarily for 500 milliseconds."* **Part 1 — the remaining snap. The dispatch brief's own two-candidate table (`spin` binding smooth=false, `bass_decay_rate` binding smooth=true/steps) was verified against `config/effect_params.json` and found factually correct but INCOMPLETE — it covered only `ValueBinding` resolution paths (scene-entry bindings, resolved once at fire time or re-rolled by dice), not the SECOND execution path a FlareKind's own `params` (`ParamTarget`, absolute/offset/random) travels: `scene_response._compute_param_moves`/`_move_params`, used by every permanent/momentary kind attached to a band. That path never checked the registry `smooth` flag at all — §75's fix only touched `_reroll` (the dice-reroll path). Proof this was live, not theoretical: `scripts/check_spectra.py`'s OWN prior assertion, written by §75 itself, explicitly documented the gap it was leaving open — *"the 0.7-1 bands' explicit 'star: 0.0' patch still wins over the dice re-roll and still lands as an instant jump — unchanged by the smoothing fix, which only touches PURE dice re-rolls."* STAR's own authored "Flare patch 0.7–1"/"Drop patch 0.7–1" kinds (permanent, `star: 0.0` AND `spin: 0.55`) fire on every ordinary flare/drop at intensity ≥0.7 and land via this exact unfixed path — `star` (registry smooth=true) snapping instantly on every one of them, exactly the "player still causing it to snap" he's watching. **`spin` (candidate 1) confirmed as tabled**: `signal="trigger_intensity", mode="map"`, `smooth=false` — resolved once at scene-fire time (never re-rolled, since dice-reroll only touches `signal="random"` bindings), and ALSO explicitly re-targeted by these same "Flare/Drop patch 0.7–1" kinds — jumping there was correct per its own tag, not a bug; whether it should be bound to a continuously-changing signal at all is a product question this task does not decide. **`bass_decay_rate` (candidate 2) confirmed as tabled but currently inert**: `signal="trigger_intensity", mode="steps"`, registry smooth=true on `power` — but grep of STAR's live `flare_kinds`/`responses` shows NO kind anywhere targets `bass_decay_rate` via `ParamTarget`, and its own scene-entry binding resolves once at fire time like `spin`'s — so nothing currently re-writes it more than once per scene fire. The registry's smooth=true tag on it is real but presently unexercised: a latent gap (would misbehave under the OLD `_move_params` the instant a future kind targets it), not a live snap today. **Fix**: `scene_response._move_params` (called only from `on_event`, the band-driven flare/charge/lull/drop path — `_move_params_ramped`, used by `on_update`, was already unconditionally a glide and needed no change) now splits each kind's landed params by the SAME registry smooth verdict `_reroll` already uses, routing smooth numerics into the shared `glides` dict (`executor.glide(..., DICE_REROLL_GLIDE_MS)`) and everything else into `jumps` as before. Because a param's smooth verdict is a static per-(effect,param) registry lookup, dice and a same-event patch targeting the same key always land in the same dict — patch-overrides-dice precedence now falls out of plain `dict.update()` ordering (moves execute after dice in `on_event`), so the old "drop any dice-only glide a later patch also claims" cleanup is provably unreachable for that race and kept only as a defensive no-op, not deleted. **`spin`'s registry `smooth` flag independently retagged `false→true`** (`config/effect_params.json`), verified from source, not from his sentence wanting it (his follow-up push-back explicitly warned against retagging to match intent alone): `fx/effects/radial.py::config_updated` recomputes `self.spin = nonlinear_log(config['spin'], 2)/10` on every tween frame including mid-glide (`_apply_config(validate=False)`, the per-frame tween path), and `fx/utils.py::nonlinear_log = sign(x)*abs(x)**power` is continuous through zero and negative x — no rendering barrier to a smooth sign-crossing glide. Zero live scene binds `spin` via `signal="random"` (checked across all 9 scenes), so this retag has NO effect on `_reroll`'s dice-glide gate anywhere; it only changes whether an explicit `ParamTarget` patch on `spin` (this fix, and the two new kinds below) glides or jumps. **His follow-up message, verified line-by-line, correction delivered**: his own re-read of `config/effect_params.json` (`spin`: numeric, min 0/max 1, magnitude_only, default 0.2; `spin_sign`: toggle, maps_to spin, sign_control, "Toggle=negate sign") was byte-accurate — confirmed independently against the same file — but the conclusion drawn from it ("toggle spin_sign, that's the existing mechanism") does not hold under SPECTRA: `maps_to`/`sign_control`/`magnitude_only` translation exists ONLY in legacy `services/morph_compiler.py::_sign_control_patch`/`services/trigger_engine.py` (grep-confirmed absent from `spectra/`/`fx/`), and `fx/effects/radial.py`'s real `CONFIG_SCHEMA` has no `spin_sign` key at all — the only `spin_sign` name in that file is an unrelated local variable inside particle-handoff snapshot code, never read from `self._config`. Voluptuous's merged effect schema uses `extra=ALLOW_EXTRA` (`fx/utils.py`'s `schema()` classmethod), so a SPECTRA write targeting `spin_sign` would land as an inert, unvalidated extra key the effect never consults — legacy's own `morph_compiler.py` docstring names this exact shape: *"LedFX has no bool to receive, so writing the virtual name would be an inert no-op key."* Also corrected: the registry's declared `spin` range (`min:0, max:1`) is not what the real effect enforces (`radial.py`'s own schema is `[-1.0, 1.0]`) and is not even what SPECTRA's own clamp code enforces for a `ParamTarget` at the band's default ×1.0 scale (`_compute_param_moves`'s registry clamp only runs when `scale != 1.0`) — so a negative `spin` target does not "clamp to nothing or throw," it lands correctly once real writes go live (today's `RecordingExecutor` is DARK regardless). Net: his intent (reverse by flipping spin's sign) was exactly right; the correct target is `spin` itself, signed, never `spin_sign`. **Part 2 — the two reverse flares, checked before building.** Confirmed: Fireworks V2's existing "Reverse Direction" kind (Sonic, same day, `type: momentary`, `params: {reverse: {mode: absolute, value: 1.0}}`, `hold_ms: 500`) targets `reverse` — a plain boolean on `fireworks`/`fireworks1d` meaning implode-vs-explode, per its own registry note — a param `radial` doesn't have at all (checked `Radial2d.CONFIG_SCHEMA`: no `reverse` key). It cannot be reused verbatim on STAR; copying it in would be exactly as inert as a `spin_sign` write, for the same `extra=ALLOW_EXTRA` reason. **Reported as asked, plainly**: the general SHAPE of "momentary, 500ms, ParamTarget-driven" is not new work — Fireworks V2's own kind already proves that mechanism end to end (`hold_ms`, `flush_releases`'s unconditional release glide) — but its concrete PARAMS are scene-specific and had to be authored fresh for `radial`; a permanent flip and a 500ms momentary flip are also genuinely two different things (CARRIES vs. RETURNS), not one kind wearing two names. Built as two new named `FlareKind` entries on STAR — "Reverse Direction" (`type: permanent`, `spin: {mode: absolute, value: -0.55}`) and "Reverse Momentarily (500ms)" (`type: momentary`, `hold_ms: 500`, same target) — `-0.55` is the negation of his own already-authored "Flare patch 0.35–0.7"/"0.7–1" spin magnitude, matching his existing absolute-value convention rather than inventing a new one. Neither carries a `jump` field — `FlareKind._shape` structurally forbids `jump` on a non-`drift_jump` kind — so neither can re-roll STAR's dice-bound `star`/`edges`; both target only `spin`, which no dice binding touches, so the interaction this task was warned to mind (a new 500ms door back into the snap) does not exist by construction, verified in the executable spec below, not merely argued. Both kinds now glide (not jump) when fired, per the Part 1 fix + retag above — a "reverse" that snapped would have reopened exactly the door this task closed. **Declared, not band-attached — matching precedent, not an oversight**: Fireworks V2's own "Reverse Direction" is itself declared in `flare_kinds` but attached to zero bands (`scene_console.apply_flare_kind` only ever touches `flare_kinds`, never `responses`/`band.kinds` — a hard scope boundary; band attachment is the Scenes page's own click-to-attach chip on the band strip, a human action). This build follows the identical shape: the two kinds land available on STAR, ready to attach; which band(s) and at what scale is left to him. **Migration**: `scripts/add_star_reverse_flares.py` (dry-run default, `--apply`, idempotent — re-running with the same shape is a no-op) goes through `spectra/services/scene_console.apply_flare_kind` — the SAME write path Sonic already uses — rather than a raw-JSON patch, since adding a `FlareKind` (unlike a single-scalar-field edit) is exactly what that function exists for: full model validation, a whole-scene re-validate, and a verified pre-write backup (`scene_console._write_and_verify_backup`) all come for free. Verified end-to-end against an isolated copy of his real live scene data (fetched via `GET /spectra/api/scenes` earlier in this task, never written back): dry-run, apply, idempotent re-apply, and a full before/after diff proving only STAR's `flare_kinds` grew by exactly these two entries — every other scene and every other field on STAR byte-identical. **Not run against his live `:8010` storage by this build** — an operator/deploy step, same convention as `seed_star_strips.py`/`set_scene_colorset_preference.py`. **Verification**: `scripts/check_spectra.py` updated — the twist/spin/star patch-broadcast checks now assert glide (not jump) with the correct `DICE_REROLL_GLIDE_MS` duration; the Mid-Group "top flare band executes its patch" loop now accepts either transport (several of the seven scenes' own top-band patches — `beat_burst`, `spawn_rate`, `burst_size` — are already registry smooth=true and now correctly glide too, not just STAR); `_no_flare_moves_star_edges`'s final assertion is corrected from "still jumps" to "now glides," with a new companion assertion that the SAME top-band patch's `spin` write also glides; a new self-contained section proves both reverse kinds' structure, glide transport, correct target value, carry-vs-return semantics, and star/edges non-interference directly. Four pytest tests broke and were fixed, all the same class: `gradient_scale`/`power_multiplier` on the test fixtures' `concentric` effect are registry smooth=true, so patches the tests previously asserted landed as an instant jump now correctly ease over `DICE_REROLL_GLIDE_MS` — each test now renders past that duration before reading `effect._config` (`tests/test_spectra_engine.py::test_surge_jump_lands_next_frame_and_carries`, renamed `test_surge_patch_eases_and_carries`; `test_flare_kinds_semantics_on_the_harness`; `test_momentary_target_expressions_and_chosen_hold_on_the_harness`; `tests/test_trigger_engine.py::test_trigger_fires_response_action_on_the_real_pipeline`). Full suite: 658 collected, 0 failed, 2 skipped (pre-existing live-model smoke tests, no API key in this environment). `check_spectra.py` itself still fails at ONE known pre-existing point, "Black Hole V2/flare: reroll flag mis-migrated" against this worktree's own stale `storage/spectra/scenes.json` — confirmed identical on unmodified `master` via `git stash` before touching anything, unrelated to this change, matching the documented environmental-failures pattern; every check before AND after that point (verified via an isolated skip-probe re-run of the same script, never committed) passes, including every check this PR adds or edits. **A caveat worth repeating**: this worktree's local `storage/spectra/scenes.json` was found genuinely pre-`flare_kinds`-migration (a stale, old-schema snapshot, 0 raw `flare_kinds` keys) — an early attempt to refresh it from live GET data for convenience broke unrelated seeder-fixture tests further down `check_spectra.py` that specifically depend on that file holding the S1 seeder's RAW, pre-migration shape; the refresh was reverted and the file confirmed byte-identical to how it was found before this task touched anything. All new verification against CURRENT live scene shapes used a separate, disposable copy built from a fresh `GET /spectra/api/scenes` fetch, never overwriting this worktree's own fixture file. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired, no write reached live storage or hardware — his lights were being watched live during this task per its own hard limits; both fixes are proven at the mechanism layer (real `_move_params`/`_compute_param_moves`/executor code paths against his real STAR scene's real binding/flare-kind shapes) and via the executable specs, not by watching STAR play smoothly or reverse in his room. **Superseded 2026-08-20 (PR fm/star-reverse-flare-use-flip, his words: "use the flip control for star", the NO-PAUSE/more-jarring trade told to him and taken knowingly)**: the `spin: -0.55` absolute target above is retired on both kinds — `spin`'s own smooth=true tag means a glide from +0.55 to -0.55 crosses zero, exactly the freeze his flare exists to fix. Both kinds now target `spin_sign` ("Flip") instead, and — unlike when this row was written — that is no longer inert: `spectra/services/scene_response.py::_compute_param_moves` gained the sign-control port this row had documented as legacy-only (see `spin_sign`'s own `config/effect_params.json` note, updated the same PR). A sign-control `ParamTarget` redirects onto the real `maps_to` param (`spin`) with only its sign flipped, magnitude preserved from `spin`'s own current carried value, forced through `executor.jump()` — never glide — on BOTH departure and the momentary kind's release, so the value never passes through zero either direction. **The collision question asked of this build, answered from code, not assumed**: a sign-control write and an ordinary absolute `spin` write (e.g. this scene's own untouched "Flare/Drop patch" kinds) land in the exact SAME `(vid, "spin")` carry slot — there is no second, independently-tracked "flip bit" that could go stale, so the two compose by plain last-write-wins semantics, the same as any other two permanent kinds sharing a target param; proven directly (`scripts/check_spectra.py`'s new collision section), not argued. Proven offline at frame level on the real vendored `radial` effect (`tests/test_spectra_engine.py::test_star_reverse_flare_flip_lands_with_no_pause_and_restores_direction`): the sign lands within one rendered frame on departure AND release, and magnitude tracks `spin`'s own live baseline (proven against a 0.2 baseline, not just his scene's coincidental 0.55) rather than a fixed authored number. Migration: `scripts/switch_star_reverse_flares_to_flip.py` (raw-dict patch — the established "don't round-trip through `scene_store.save()`" discipline this same night's Squiggles incident re-confirmed the need for — dry-run default, `--apply`, `--revert` for the exact one-field-back inverse). **Still room-unproven** — same hard limit as above, plus his room was released partway through this task (music stopped, lights handed back) before either half of the live proof (no pause on the turn; original direction restored after release) could be taken; both remain outstanding for firstmate's own deploy + live verification. |
| §79 | Force Scene: enabling the pin (or repinning while enabled) now fires immediately | `FIXED` — PR fm/spectra-force-scene-immediate, offline-verified only, no live write per this task's own hard limits | His live report, verbatim: *"I'm trying to test black hole in light mode. However I am using [Force Scene] to do it and nothing is happening. currently there's a song playing with no Triggers on it yet it is recapturing. But when I force a scene I want it to immediately activate that scene."* Live-reproduced before touching anything: `display_mode: light`, `force_scene_enabled: true`, `force_scene_scene_id` = Black Hole V2, active scene still Orbits V2, `deferred_by: None`. **Root cause, confirmed by reading the code, not re-derived**: §31's Force Scene is a *redirect*, not a *cause* — `scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id`'s own docstring already said so ("redirects scene_id here"), and it only runs when something else was already about to pick a scene. Enabling Force Scene also sets `bridge.sequencer_deferral()` to `"force_scene"`, which stops the sequencer's own rolls — so on a song carrying no triggers and no analysis, nothing was ever going to fire, and the redirect never got an occasion to run. This is not a mode-availability regression: `fire_scene_by_id` already exempts a Force-Scene-pinned fire from the `display_availability` gate (the same "an explicit pin keeps its declared life" rule pause/dinner_party/ambient already follow), confirmed by reading that gate before assuming otherwise — a dark-preferring scene pinned while the room is in Light mode is not silently filtered. **Fix**: `spectra/services/room_controls.reconcile_force_scene_if_changed`, the same one-choke-point PUT-triggered shape as `reconcile_ambient_if_changed`/`reconcile_dark_light_if_changed` (§52/§9) — called from `PUT /api/room-controls` after the new state is saved. Fires the pinned scene through `scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id` immediately on the edit that changed the pin: `force_scene_enabled` flipping false→true, or `force_scene_scene_id` changing while already enabled. Deliberately does **not** re-fire on an unrelated field re-save (e.g. the brightness slider) while an already-enabled pin stays on the same scene — a scene shouldn't jump every time he touches a different control, same discipline the other two reconciles already use. Intensity for the immediate fire reads live `bridge.intensity()` (the section energy at the current playback position, the same value an automatic pick would have used), falling back to the stated 0.5 neutral when the bridge/analysis has nothing (exactly his own case — a song with no triggers yet). **"If it refuses for a good reason, it must say so" — his own bar, met structurally**: the function always returns a dict naming what happened rather than `None` on refusal (unlike the other two reconciles, where `None` means "nothing to do") — `{"status": "fired", scene_id, scene_name}`, or `{"status": "skipped", "reason": "no scene pinned"}` / `{"status": "pinned scene not found"}`, or `{"status": "error", "reason": ...}` if the fire itself raised. Folded into `PUT /api/room-controls`'s response as `force_scene_result`, mirroring `ambient_result`/`dark_light_result`. Frontend: `RoomControlsBar.tsx`'s Scenes panel shows a badge under the scene picker naming the outcome (`ForceSceneResult` in `types.ts`); `helpContent.ts`'s `force-scene` entry rewritten to lead with "fires immediately," not just "holds." Settings-console (Sonic) is unaffected — `force_scene_*` was already deliberately excluded from its registry (§ settings console docs: "a picker action, not a voice setting"), so no wiring was needed there. Tests: 7 new cases in `tests/test_room_controls.py` (fires on enable, fires on repin, ignores an unrelated field re-save, ignores disabling, states a reason for no-pin and for a missing scene, uses live bridge intensity when available) plus a matching block in `scripts/check_spectra.py`'s existing Force Scene section. Full suite: 674 passed, 5 pre-existing/environmental failures unchanged from `master` (`tests/test_crystal_activation_verify.py` ×3, `tests/test_settings_agent_cli.py` ×2 missing the `mcp` package — confirmed identical via `git stash` before this work), 2 pre-existing skipped. `scripts/check_spectra.py` has one known pre-existing environmental failure ("STAR: raw seeder output still carries the pre-freeze dice pairs," this worktree's stale `storage/spectra/scenes.json`) confirmed identical on `master` via the same `git stash` check; every check this PR added passed. **Room-unproven by this task's own hard limits**: firstmate owns every deploy/restart, and live SPECTRA (`:8010`) was read-only for this task except where a window was granted — the mechanism is proven offline (real `fire_scene_by_id`/`scene_compiler.fire_scene` code paths, fake-clocked) and the reproduction above was read-only against his live state, never written to. |
| §80 | Temporary scene disable — a manual, reversible toggle; disabled beats mode availability; Force Scene still fires it but NAMES the override | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-scene-disable, verified offline + against an isolated instance on a spare port (phone-eye-checked at 390×844) | His ask, verbatim: *"add an ability to disable a scene temporarily."* **Reading, stated rather than picked silently**: "temporarily" read as (a) a manual toggle he flips off and later flips back on — reversible, no expiry — not (b) a genuine timer/auto-restore; nothing in his existing data or UI (Mode availability, Force Scene, every other scene field) suggested a timed mechanism, and building one unasked would have added a whole expiry model he never requested. `SceneV2.disabled: bool = False` (`spectra/models/scene.py`) — every existing scene loads unaffected. **Interaction with Mode availability (§9), checked rather than assumed**: disabled is the STRONGER, more explicit statement ("don't use this scene, period" vs. "not in this room mode"), so it is gated FIRST at every choke point — a scene that is both disabled and mode-gated reports `skipped="disabled"`, not `"mode_availability"`. Wired at the exact same three points §9 already established: the hard gate `scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id` (once, for every automatic caller), the sequencer's own candidate pool (`SceneSequencer._roll`'s `available` set, new `_scene_enabled`/`_default_scene_enabled` callable — kept SEPARATE from `_scene_mode_available` rather than folded in, matching this codebase's own precedent of keeping orthogonal gates as distinct functions, e.g. colour-set preference vs. availability), and `trigger_engine._default_select_scene`'s generated-trigger draw. A manual editor Fire/test-fire (`POST /scenes/{id}/fire` → `scene_compiler.fire_scene` directly) bypasses the gate entirely, unchanged code path, same as it already bypasses Mode availability. **Interaction with Force Scene (§31/§79), the refinement weighed rather than adopted blind**: the brief proposed "the more recent explicit act wins" and asked it be argued, not assumed — agreed and built as proposed: a pin on a disabled scene is honoured (`fire_scene_by_id`'s existing `forced` bypass, unchanged), but the result now carries `overrode_disabled=True` when the pinned scene is disabled, threaded through `room_controls.reconcile_force_scene_if_changed` into `force_scene_result.overrode_disabled` — silently allowing OR silently refusing were both rejected as options; naming the contradiction was the only one of the three that doesn't hide something from him. Deliberately did NOT extend this same "name it" treatment to Force Scene's pre-existing silent bypass of Mode availability (§9/§31) — out of scope, a different established behaviour this task wasn't asked to touch, and changing it would be an unrequested behaviour change smuggled into a disable feature. **Visible on every surface a status would be checked, per the task's explicit requirement, not left to a detail panel**: a red "⛔ disabled" badge on the scene-list row (`ScenesPage.tsx`), the phone header's compact scene selector when that scene is open, a `DisabledToggle.tsx` toolbar control (same fixed-width-per-state discipline as `ModeAvailabilityToggle.tsx`, red when active) next to the Mode availability toggle, the Force Scene scene picker's option label (`⛔ {name}`), and a red "⚠ overriding disabled scene" badge on the room bar's Force Scene panel when the override fires. Help: new `scene-disable` entry in `helpContent.ts`, cross-linked from both `mode-availability` and `force-scene`. **Phone-eye-checked** (chrome-devtools-axi, Playwright Chromium headless, 390×844, against an isolated `python -m spectra` on a spare port with this worktree's own isolated storage — his live `:8010` untouched throughout, confirmed alive after cleanup): the scene-list badge, the toolbar toggle (flip → save → round-trip confirmed via `GET /scenes/{id}`), and the full Force-Scene-overrides-a-disabled-scene flow (checkbox toggle in the real UI → "FIRED: …" + "⚠ OVERRIDING DISABLED SCENE" badge, matching a direct API proof of the same PUT) all rendered correctly with no horizontal overflow. Tests: `tests/test_scene_disable.py` (10, new) — the field default, `fire_scene_by_id`'s skip/fire/override-naming behaviour, disabled-beats-mode-availability in the reported reason, the sequencer roll's candidate-pool exclusion, `_default_scene_enabled`'s own semantics, `trigger_engine`'s generated-trigger draw exclusion, and the manual-fire bypass — plus `tests/test_room_controls.py`/`tests/test_mode_availability.py` re-run for regression (all green, unmodified). Full suite: **686 passed, 5 pre-existing/environmental failures, 2 skipped** — the identical five confirmed via `git stash` in this same environment before this work (3× `test_crystal_activation_verify.py` needing seeded live device topology, 2× `test_settings_agent_cli.py` missing the optional `mcp` package); `scripts/check_spectra.py`/`scripts/check_triggers.py` both re-run, only pre-existing/environmental output unchanged (diffed byte-for-byte against a `git stash` baseline bar one log line's memory address). `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` both clean. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired and nothing was written to live storage or hardware — firstmate owns every deploy/restart and he was using live SPECTRA (`:8010`) throughout this task; every proof above ran against a disposable, isolated instance on a spare port with its own storage, never his live process. |
| §81 | Flare kind edit box (rename/delete/copy), cross-scene paste, and drag-to-lane band attachment | `BUILT` (frontend-only) — PR fm/spectra-flare-lanes-and-edit, merged (#129, code-verified 2026-08-19); phone-verified via an isolated instance, no write reached his live `:8010` | His words: *"On the scenes tab, under flares, I want to be able to double click a flare and have it pop up an edit box that allows me to rename it, delete it, or copy it. That means we also need to be able to paste it on a different scene. Furthermore I want to be able to drag these into different vertical lanes; each vertical lane picks an action, and they combine. start with 2 and allow adding up to 2 more."* Read as flares, not scenes, despite his literal "drag these scenes" — nothing in his ask or the code supports a scene-lane concept, and everything (double-click on "a flare", paste "it" onto "a different scene") is about the flare kind. **Established before building, per this task's own instruction: a flare kind is FULLY SCENE-SCOPED** — `SceneV2.flare_kinds: list[FlareKind]` (`models/scene.py`), no cross-scene id, referenced only by name within that one scene's own `responses[*].bands[*].kinds` dict. So paste is a genuine PORT (a fresh, independent entry on the target scene, deduped-by-name via the same `" (2)"` convention `_migrate_flare_kinds.declare()` already uses), never a live link — editing or deleting either copy afterward never touches the other. Zero backend/model change was needed for any of Parts 1–3: rename/delete/paste/lane-attach are all edits to fields the model and its validation (`SceneV2._validate`: duplicate names, dangling `band.kinds` references) already governed: the frontend PUTs the whole scene through the SAME save path every other edit here already used, so the existing validator is the safety net against a cascade bug, not a new one written for this. **"Combine," the ask's load-bearing word — the existing composition rule was found, not invented**: `band.kinds: dict[name→scale]` already lets MULTIPLE kinds attach to one band (§ "Named flare kinds" above), and `scene_response.ResponseEngine.on_event` already executes them in a FIXED order per family (dice → permanent params → momentary params → permanent gains → momentary gains → colour jump — the legacy reroll→patch→gain→colour order, generalized) with a DIFFERENT composition rule per family, none of it exposed as a user toggle: dice re-rolls and colour jumps are each a SINGLETON pick per fire (`dice[0]`/`colours[0]` — a second one attached is harmless but inert, "a jump is a jump," "one selector roll per fire"); momentary/permanent PARAM moves OVERWRITE (permanent lands before momentary so a spike shows over the just-set baseline; among same-type kinds targeting the same param, the LATER one in `band.kinds`' own dict order wins, via plain `dict.update()` — the same mechanism that already makes an explicit param patch beat a same-event dice re-roll, generalized, not a special case); permanent GAIN kinds CHAIN multiplicatively when more than one lands in the same band (each reads the prior one's landed brightness off `carry` before computing its own effective multiplier) — this is the flare engine's own unadvertised answer to the "overwrite vs additive" question his colour-set `bg_mode` asks explicitly elsewhere; it was never one uniform rule, and lanes had to expose that honestly rather than pick one. **Lanes are UI over the existing `band.kinds` dict, not a new stored concept**: a lane is a POSITION in that dict — `FlareLaneRack.tsx` renders each band's kinds at their dict-insertion index as a draggable slot (2 to start, a "+" grows to 4, never hiding a band that already carries more from legacy data or an agent edit); dropping a kind INTO a lane inserts-and-shifts (never swaps, never silently loses an occupant); dragging FROM the palette is an ADD (a kind may already sit in other bands — untouched); dragging FROM an occupied lane is a MOVE (detaches its old spot, attaches at the new one, carrying its scale) — `flareKindOps.moveKindToLane` (pure function, `scenes/tabs/flareKindOps.ts`) implements this, proven end-to-end against a real isolated instance (attach, cross-band move-with-shift, and delete-with-cascade all confirmed by reading the persisted `scenes.json` after each step, not just the UI). Because lane order IS dict order, and dict order is what the engine's own tie-break above reads, dragging a kind into an earlier or later lane is a real, meaningful control over "which same-param kind wins" — not cosmetic. **Rename/delete/copy got a direct UI, deliberately narrower than the settings-console no-forms rule**: those three are identity operations on the kind's NAME (structural), not on its `type`/`params`/`gain`/`hold_ms` (program behaviour) — the edit box (`FlareKindEditDialog.tsx`, tap or double-click, same gesture on phone and desktop since a real double-TAP is unreliable on touch) leaves the latter agent-adjustable exactly as before; the two categories are visually and functionally separate, not blurred by this build. Rename cascades into every `band.kinds` key and `update_kind` reference that named the old string (dict key SWAP, not delete+re-add, to avoid silently reordering lane precedence as a side effect of a rename); delete cascades the same way and its confirm states the live attachment count so a widely-attached kind is never removed as a surprise. Copy/paste: `lib/flareClipboard.ts`, localStorage-backed (survives reload, works across scenes and tabs), a "📋 Paste" button appears on any scene's Flares/Charges-Lulls-Drops tab whenever a kind is copied. **Phone-first, verified as touch**: built entirely on the codebase's own established Pointer-Events-plus-`touchAction:'none'` drag idiom (`BandStrip.tsx`, `SpectraTriggerBar.tsx` — no new dependency), then exercised at a real 390×844 viewport (chrome-devtools-axi via an isolated Playwright Chromium, touch+mobile emulation) against a synthetic scene on a disposable instance on a spare port (`SPECTRA_STORAGE_DIR`/`SPECTRA_PORT` repointed, never his `storage/spectra/`) — every gesture (tap-to-edit, drag-to-attach, drag-to-move-with-shift, rename, delete-with-cascade-confirm, copy, paste onto a second scene) proven by reading the resulting `scenes.json`, not just watching the UI. **Not run against his live `:8010` storage or process** — an operator/deploy step, same convention as every other frontend-only Scenes-page change in this ledger; his live instance was read-only throughout (one `GET /spectra/api/scenes` to ground the Part 4 proposal below in his real data, no writes). Help: `helpContent.ts` gained `flare-kind-edit-box` and `tab-flares-lanes`, and rewrote `flare-kinds`/`tab-flares` to point at them instead of describing the retired flat chip-row UI. Tests: 0 Python files touched, so `.venv/bin/python -m pytest` is unaffected by this PR — 3 pre-existing failures (`tests/test_crystal_activation_verify.py`, the same file §79 already logged as pre-existing/environmental) confirmed via `git status` showing only frontend files changed, not re-derived. Frontend has no test runner in this repo (`web/package.json`'s own `build` script is `tsc --noEmit && vite build`); both `spectra/web` and `web` typecheck and build clean. **Part 4 — intensity-bucket MANAGEMENT, proposed, not built** — see OQ-13. **Follow-up, 2026-08-19, PR fm/flare-lanes-one-flare-per-lane-firing-to-1gp2 — his re-ask weighed and resolved, not built or dismissed blind (Admiral edit, 2026-08-19)**: his words, verbatim: *"I like the start on the flare cards. I am not sure the lanes are working properly. I would like them in vertical lanes that each fire. this allows a color and a shape flare to happen at the same time. one flare is chosen per lane. this is similar to the shape and color sections of scenes in spotfx. let me know if what you built is better and i did not understand it."* **Established from the engine before arguing either way, per his own explicit invitation to disagree**: `scene_response.ResponseEngine.on_event` already executes EVERY kind attached to a fired band — nothing is chosen — so his stated GOAL (a colour flare and a shape flare landing together) was already delivered; his stated MECHANISM (several candidates per lane, one CHOSEN at fire time) was genuinely missing, and would be a real port of legacy's `services/trigger_engine.py::_pick_morph_lanes` (`morph_set`/`scene_update`'s own named First/Rest/Shape/Color/Charge/Lull/Drop lanes: one weighted pick per lane, every lane's pick fires together, an empty lane is silently skipped, each candidate `Action` carries its own `weight` distinct from any scale). Both findings were reported plainly, neither side picked for him. **His decision, verbatim**: *"I think i like the current method better than mine, except lets visually organize each set into vertical lanes."* He kept the all-attached-fires mechanism and declined the per-lane weighted-selection build — **that selection mechanism is NOT built**, and `scene_response.py`/`models/scene.py`/`flareKindOps.ts` are untouched by this task; the finding that made the choice available to him is what this addendum records, not a new capability. **What shipped is presentation-only**: `FlareLaneRack.tsx`'s lane cells were wider than tall (108×54, a flex-wrap row of squarish chips) despite `helpContent.ts`'s own pre-existing `tab-flares-lanes` entry already calling them "vertical LANES" — the visual didn't match the name, which is the most direct reading of "not sure the lanes are working properly" now that the firing mechanism itself is confirmed unchanged and working as designed. Lane cells are now genuinely tall (108×124, name pinned near the top and the scale/detach controls anchored near the bottom), and the rack scrolls horizontally instead of wrapping (`overflowX:'auto'`, `flexWrap:'nowrap'`) — the same "contain the overflow, don't force content narrower than it needs to be" shape already used for the spec viewer's tables and the device-preview strip's collapsed mode. Zero backend change, zero change to `moveKindToLane`'s attach/move/shift semantics, zero change to which kinds fire or when — same `band.kinds` dict, same drag mechanics, restyled only. Help: `tab-flares-lanes` gained a clause naming the scroll-not-wrap behaviour at phone width. Tests: 0 Python files touched. Frontend: `tsc --noEmit && vite build` clean (`spectra/web`). **Room-unproven, and this time deliberately so**: an attempt to phone-verify against a disposable isolated instance (`SPECTRA_STORAGE_DIR`/`SPECTRA_PORT` repointed to a spare port) went wrong — `pkill -f "python -m spectra"` used to stop that isolated instance pattern-matched his REAL live `spectra.service` too (both processes' argv are the literal string `python -m spectra`; only the interpreter path under it differs) and sent it SIGTERM. systemd restarted it in about a second (`resume: live stack reactivated — spectra drives the whole room`), and it re-confirmed healthy and live-writing on a follow-up read-only `GET /engine/status` — but a real scene fire and a Hue entertainment-stream reconnect happened as a side effect of that restart, on his actual room, which was not requested and is not undoable. Flagged to firstmate rather than attempted a second isolated run; see the incident note added to this repo's `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` maintenance section so no future agent reaches for a bare `pkill -f "python -m spectra"` in this environment. |
| §82 | Intensity-scaled scene transition times, and transitions/momentary flares that LAND ON the trigger rather than starting on it | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-transition-timing-alignment, verified offline against unit/frame-level tests + a real read-only `check_triggers.py` pass, no write reached his live `:8010` | His words: two Inspector settings, "max scene transition time" and "min scene transition time," named 200ms/300ms; scale transition time by intensity, linearly; "the mid-transition animation arrives at the same instant as the trigger, which means the transition starts early... for transitions without such a mid-point, use halfway through the transition"; the same alignment applies to momentary flares — "the first switch must finish on the trigger, then the hold, then the flip back after the trigger mark." **His own pointer ("this is how it worked in the old SpotFX") was the whole mechanism, verified against the real files**: legacy's `services/transition_phases.py` fires a phased effect switch (radial⇄blackhole/orbits/fireworks/squiggles/dancer/eye, pacman→particles — `fx/effects/particle_handoff.py`'s `BLOOM_START`/`PACMAN_MORPH_START = 0.45`, the SAME vendored choreography SPECTRA's `fx/` already carries verbatim) `anchor_frac × crossfade_ms` early so the payoff phase lands on the trigger — ported near-verbatim to `spectra/services/transition_phases.py` (same registry, same 0.45s, same `MAX_LEAD_MS=5000` cap). **One confirmed, deliberate departure from legacy, not a misreading**: legacy's own `anchor_frac()` returns 0.0 (no lead at all) for an unregistered pair — it never applied a universal midpoint, only the registered phased ones. His ask for SPECTRA generalizes that: every OTHER scene transition still lands its plain 0.5 MID-POINT on the trigger. That generalization is applied by the CALLER (`trigger_engine._scene_transition_lead_ms`), not baked into the ported module, so the port itself stays byte-diffable against legacy's actual behaviour. **The inverted numbers, wired the physically-sensible way, stated plainly**: his named 200ms/300ms are backwards for "max"/"min" read as magnitudes (200 < 300); built as low intensity → the LONGER 300ms transition, high intensity → the SHORTER 200ms one — `RoomControlState.scene_transition_ms_gentle` (300, intensity 0.0) / `scene_transition_ms_hard` (200, intensity 1.0), named for what they represent (matching `scene_response.py`'s own pre-existing `COLOR_JUMP_RAMP_MS_GENTLE`/`_HARD` and `UPDATE_RAMP_MS_GENTLE`/`_HARD` naming, the same shape used a third time here, not invented) rather than his ambiguous "max"/"min" labels — both plain settings, in `settings_console.SETTINGS_REGISTRY` (Sonic/voice-editable, "Inspector settings he can swap without a code change"), so whichever way he confirms costs nothing. `room_controls.scene_transition_ms()` is the shared linear interpolation, consulted by `scene_compiler.fire_scene` as a NEW third tier in the entry-ramp fallback chain: a scene's own `entry_ramp_ms` still wins when authored; `room.global_transition_ms` (an explicit flat override, if he's ever set one nonzero) still wins next, unchanged; only then the new intensity-scaled default — his LIVE room's unmodified defaults (`entry_ramp_ms=0`, `global_transition_ms=0`) mean this ships as the active behaviour on deploy, not a dormant option. **Two anchors, deliberately different, matching his own distinction rather than collapsing them**: a scene transition's mid-point (0.5 fallback, or a registered phased pair's own 0.45) lands on the trigger — proven at the frame level by rendering a real glide and confirming it is strictly IN FLIGHT (neither start nor end value) at the trigger's own timestamp, landing only `crossfade_ms/2` after it (`tests/test_trigger_engine.py`'s new proof 9). A momentary flare's FIRST SWITCH lands its END on the trigger — only a param move onto a registry-`smooth` target actually takes time to switch (`scene_response.DICE_REROLL_GLIDE_MS`, the same fixed 220ms constant an ordinary dice re-roll already glides over); an instant jump (non-smooth param, or a momentary GAIN's spike — `_gain` always jumps its spike, never glides) already finishes at fire time with zero lead needed, so `scene_response.momentary_switch_would_glide()` (a new, read-only, execute-nothing peek mirroring `_move_params`'s own smooth gate) decides case by case. Frame-proven (proof 10): the switch's glide lands exactly on the trigger, holds through `PULSE_HOLD_S`, and only flips back to baseline well AFTER the trigger mark — never before it, never at it. **Legacy's own conservatism is carried forward, not re-derived**: an UNRESOLVED scene pick (`scene_id=None` — the sequencer kernel or a `scene_pool` decides at fire time, not now) gets NO lead rather than a guess, the same rule legacy applied to an unresolved random branch. **Architecture note — SPECTRA's `tick()` is reactive (fires the instant `position_ms` crosses a trigger's timestamp), unlike legacy's forward-planned timeline, so "firing early" means shifting the crossing check itself**: `TriggerEngine.tick()` now computes, per trigger within a cheap proximity gate (`MAX_LEAD_MS` lookahead), `fire_at = trig.timestamp_ms − lead_ms(trig)` and fires on `(last, position]` crossing `fire_at` OR the trigger's own unshifted `timestamp_ms` — the second clause is a SAFETY NET, not an optimization: `lead_ms` is recomputed fresh every tick from live state (the registry match against whatever effect is CURRENTLY on a target virtual), so it is not guaranteed monotonic tick-to-tick; a trigger still fires by its nominal moment at the latest even if an earlier tick's early-fire window was somehow missed. Scoped to stored-trigger fires only (`tick()`'s own crossing, both `fire_scene` and `fire_response` actions) — NOT the automatic song-transition fire (`_fire_transition`, no forward notice of an upcoming track change to fire ahead of) and NOT `scene_sequencer`'s own dwell-driven rolls (no trigger timestamp to land on); the DURATION scaling (`scene_transition_ms`) is universal to every scene fire regardless of origin, since it lives inside `scene_compiler.fire_scene` itself. **Deliberately NOT touched, to avoid conflating two different mechanisms**: `SceneV2.choreography` (`PhaseChoreography` — `enabled`/`transition_ms`/`anchor_frac` default 0.45/`transition_mode`) is a SEPARATE, still-unwired per-scene field (§28's own note: "phase choreography legacy's own SceneV2 defined but never wired into an execution engine") that this build does not reach into or repurpose — the brief never named it, and reusing its `anchor_frac`/`transition_ms` for this feature would have been an unrequested design guess rather than a read of his actual ask; §28's gap note stands unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_lead_time_alignment.py` (15, new — the ported registry, the interpolation + fallback chain, the momentary-glide peek), `tests/test_trigger_engine.py` (+2 frame-level proofs, 10 total in that file), `scripts/check_triggers.py` (+1 section, production-wiring proof against real scene/room/conductor state, no fx_seam/HTTP reached — a fresh-engine-with-injected-fake-fire-scene pattern established here specifically to keep this offline spec from ever attempting a live `dry_run=False` fire). Full suite: **703 passed, 5 pre-existing/environmental failures (identical set §80 already logged, confirmed via `git stash` in this same environment), 2 skipped** — zero new failures. `scripts/check_settings_console.py`/`check_triggers.py`/`check_spectra.py`/`check_drift.py`/`check_scene_v2.py`/`check_sequencer.py` all re-run clean (the one pre-existing `check_spectra.py` STAR-seeder failure, needing his live-seeded storage, confirmed identical via `git stash`). Help: `helpContent.ts` gained an `intensity-scaled-transitions` entry. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired and nothing was written to live storage or hardware — firstmate owns every deploy/restart and he was using live SPECTRA (`:8010`) throughout this task. |
| §83 | "Goes from black to a grey or white, then changes colour" — root cause PINNED and FIXED | `FIXED` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-achromatic-saturation-fix, not yet merged, verified offline against his real color_sets.json data — his own words, corrected from an earlier (wrong) light-mode-background-leak report: *"I see now that it goes from black to a gray or a white and then changes color... the brightness is scaling from a gray tone and then the Hue is changing."* He later cut the follow-up question off — "just fix it" — so this was shipped as a defect fix, not brought back as a decision. | **FIX (2026-08-19, PR fm/spectra-achromatic-saturation-fix)**: `fx/effects/__init__.py::hue_tween_fields` now adopts the other endpoint's SATURATION for an achromatic (black/grey/white) endpoint, the same way it already adopted its HUE — so only VALUE ramps for that endpoint, matching the function's own docstring intent ("fades saturation in place"). Before/after on the same real pair (`#000000` → `#ff9940`, background_color from "Line - Green" → "Calm - Purple"): t=0.25 was `#403934` sat=0.187 (near-grey), now `#402610` sat=0.749 — **byte-identical to the plain RGB path (`mix_colors`) at every sampled t**, both fading IN from black and fading OUT to black (the symmetric case, since his colour journeys glide to black as routinely as from it). **Isolation proof, not just a claim**: the fix changes only the saturation field of `hsv_start`/`hsv_delta` — hue and value fields are proven bit-identical to the pre-fix formula for every pair tested (`tests/test_hue_tween_achromatic_saturation.py::test_hue_and_value_fields_are_unaffected_by_the_fix`), and the colour-to-colour crossing case (neither endpoint achromatic) is proven bit-identical before/after, including the separately-filed muddy cream→blue crossing itself (`#8b7e53`→`#009eff`, `data/spectra-grey-midpoint-transition/brief.md`) — that pair's own dip lives in `mix_colors` (the plain RGB path), a different function, not touched here, and remains open as its own backlog item (`spectra-muddy-colour-crossing`). Per-pixel gradient case also re-proven: the black-sourced pixel in "Line - Green"'s gradient now holds its target saturation throughout instead of ramping 0→1. `fx/VENDOR.md` deviation #13 records this as a vendored-file behaviour change. Tests: 5 new in `tests/test_hue_tween_achromatic_saturation.py`, all passing; full suite **720 passed, 5 pre-existing/environmental failures (3× `tests/test_crystal_activation_verify.py`, 2× `tests/test_settings_agent_cli.py` missing `mcp` package — confirmed identical failures on pristine `master` before this change, this task's diff touches none of those files), 2 skipped**. Not room-proven — no camera/live-instrument requirement exists for this fix (it's a property of the interpolation, reproducible at a desk, same as the original investigation below), and firstmate owns any live deploy/verification. **Investigation history preserved below.** Three hypotheses were checked against real values and his real data, in the order asked, before any fix was proposed. **Hypothesis 1 (RGBW white channel) — cleared for software, confirmed live for firmware.** Every device his real `fx-live/config.json` configures uses `sync_mode: "DDP"` (all 7 WLED units); `fx/devices/ddp.py`'s wire format is hardcoded RGB 8-bit (`DATATYPE = 0x0B`, 3 bytes/pixel, no 4th channel in the protocol at all) — SPECTRA's own code never computes or transmits a white byte, confirmed by reading every packet-encoding path (`fx/devices/{ddp,udp,packets}.py`); the one code path that CAN build a 4-byte DRGBW packet (`packets.build_drgbw_packet`, UDP sync mode only — none of his devices use it) hardcodes the white byte to literal `0`; the `rgbw_led` config flag SPECTRA detects per-device is stored but never read anywhere to change what's sent (grep-confirmed, one dead commented-out reference in `e131.py`). **Then room access opened mid-task and this was checked live rather than left as "config alone should settle it"**: `GET http://<ip>/json/info` on 3 of his real RGBW fixtures (`sconce-kitchen-left` 192.168.40.110, `porch-rail` 192.168.40.158, `sconce-kitchen-right` 192.168.40.113) all read `"rgbw": true, "wv": 2` — WLED's own firmware-side RGBW-auto-calculate mode is ON. This is a real, additive, second mechanism, entirely outside this repo: whenever SPECTRA sends a desaturated RGB value (from the mechanism below, or from the separately-filed colour-crossing dip), his 4 RGBW fixtures' OWN FIRMWARE will synthesize a white-channel contribution from it on top. **Hypothesis 2 (brightness and colour on separate glide schedules) — real, but not the mechanism for a scene-to-scene transition; real elsewhere.** For a scene fire (`scene_compiler.compile_scene`), brightness/background_brightness and gradient/background_color always land in ONE `config` dict per virtual (`_entry_config`/`_apply_set_colors`), sent as ONE PUT with one `transition_ms`/`easing`/`transition_blend` — `fx/effects/__init__.py::_advance_tweens` advances every key in that one call on the identical per-frame `t`, proven by reading the loop: no gap is structurally possible here. The SEPARATE-schedule mechanism is real, just elsewhere: `scene_response.py`'s flare/response engine (`on_event`) fires `_gain` (brightness, its own duration — `GAIN_GLIDE_S=0.8s` permanent glide, or an INSTANT jump for a momentary spike) and `_color_jump` (hue, `color_jump_ramp_ms(intensity)` = 150–2500ms) as genuinely separate `executor.glide()`/`jump()` calls, "colour last" by the module's own documented execution order — confirmed wired on 7 of his 9 real scenes (`Black Hole V2`, `Orbits V2`, `STAR`, `Fireworks V2`, `Dancers V2`, `Eye V2`, `Pacman V2` all attach a gain kind and "Colour Jump" to the same band). **The root cause, PINNED with real values (`scripts/check_hue_blend_achromatic_desaturation.py`)**: `fx/effects/__init__.py::hue_tween_fields` — the "careful" hue-arc blend `fx_seam`/`fx_executor` ALWAYS request for a real scene fire (his live `room_controls.json` has `global_transition_ms: 0`, but `scene_compiler.fire_scene`'s fallback chain, `scene.entry_ramp_ms or room.global_transition_ms or scene_transition_ms(...)`, treats Python-falsy `0` as unset, so it always lands on the nonzero 200–300ms intensity-scaled default; `fx_seam._body()`/`fx_executor._put()` both hardcode `transition_blend="hue"` whenever `transition_ms>0`) — is the mechanism that PRODUCES the desaturation it was built to PREVENT, whenever one endpoint of a transition is achromatic (black, at minimum — his own load-bearing convention: 30 real `color_sets.json` entries author `bg_color == "#000000"` exactly per §72's own black-background finding, plus 12 gradient entries carry a literal black stop within the gradient). For an achromatic endpoint, `hue_tween_fields` adopts the OTHER end's hue immediately and interpolates SATURATION and VALUE as independent linear scalars from 0 — `sat(t) = t × target_sat`, proven both algebraically and against a real pair from his data (background_color `#000000` from "Line - Green" → `#ff9940` from "Calm - Purple"): at t=0.25 the hue-path lands at `#403934`, sat=0.187 — nearly grey — while the PLAIN RGB path it replaced (`mix_colors`, a straight per-channel scale-up, which leaves the max/min ratio unchanged) lands at `#402610`, sat=0.749, on the IDENTICAL pair. Confirmed this is specific to achromatic endpoints, not a general hue-path defect: the same function on a pair where NEITHER end is grey (`#8b7e53`→`#009eff`, the separately-filed colour-crossing dip's own pair) shows monotonic, non-dipping saturation via the hue-path (0.403→1.000) — the hue-path correctly protects THAT crossing; it is specifically the achromatic-adoption branch that backfires. Verified this is per-pixel inside a real GRADIENT→GRADIENT fire too, not just a solid `background_color`: `hue_tween_fields` applied to real curves for "Line - Green" (a black-bordered gradient, `linear-gradient(90deg, rgb(0,0,0) 30%, rgb(0,255,7) 35%, rgb(0,255,7) 65%, rgb(0,0,0) 70%)`) → "Calm - Purple" shows the pixels sourced from Line-Green's black zones ramping saturation 0→1 proportional to t, while pixels sourced from its green zone stay at sat=1.0 throughout — no silent try/except fallback masking either behaviour (`hue_tween_fields` never threw across an exhaustive sweep of all 37,076 real ordered pairs drawn from his 197 stored colour/gradient/bg values — classify_param never fell to "instant" either). **The gradient-vs-solid routing question, checked directly and closed**: does NOT split his colour changes roughly in half as feared — both his 52 gradient-kind and 46 solid-kind real entries reach and complete the hue path identically when `blend="hue"` is requested (0 exceptions across the full sweep on either kind); the split he sees is not gradient-vs-solid, it is achromatic-endpoint-vs-not. **Live confirmation, room window opened mid-task**: fired his real "Line - Green" → "Calm - Purple" sequence via `POST /spectra/api/room-color/apply` — `executor.recent_writes` confirmed real, nonzero glide durations (641–2109ms observed, never instant) reaching his live virtuals, and confirmed his OWN automatic colour-journey conductor glides `background_color` to/from literal `#000000` as routine, continuous, ongoing behaviour (multiple such writes observed unprompted within seconds, e.g. seq 1080/1081 `background_color: '#000000'` over 1310ms) — the precondition is not an edge case, it fires constantly in his actual room. **What could NOT be settled this window, named rather than guessed**: no per-frame visual/pixel capture of a physical fixture rendering the muddy intermediate exists in this repo, and none was attempted from outside it (no camera). SPECTRA exposes no external HTTP read of a live effect's currently-interpolating `_config` (the facade's `/api/virtuals` is in-process only), and the shared `executor.recent_writes` ring buffer logs one entry per glide START (target + duration), not per rendered frame, so it cannot show an in-flight sample either — the same class of instrument-blindness §52/§63 already documented for Hue CLIP reads during a `dynamics`-ramped transition. What would settle it: a temporary high-frequency (~10Hz) poll of a live effect's `_config` value during a deliberately slow (multi-second) manual test glide, or a camera on the fixture during one. **Priority three of the room-open window (bulbs receiving ~277 vs ~500 at a real fire, tied to a separate intensity-scaling investigation) was explicitly declined this window** — it references a mechanism/claim outside this task's own scope and this agent's context, and attempting it without a precise specification of what "277"/"500" refer to risked a misleading result rather than a settled one; flagged back rather than guessed at. **No fix applied by this investigation** — see the FIX paragraph above for the remedy shipped the same night. `scripts/check_hue_blend_achromatic_desaturation.py` (read-only, no live storage/instance touched, all assertions pass — since updated to re-verify the fix rather than only the defect) is the reproducible proof. Full suite at investigation time: **712 passed, 3 pre-existing/environmental failures (`tests/test_crystal_activation_verify.py` — the same file §79–§82 already log as pre-existing, needing seeded live device topology), 2 skipped** — this task changed zero existing source files, only added the diagnostic script and this entry, so the 3 are unrelated by construction, not merely re-confirmed. **Room state**: two manual `room-color/apply` calls were made live (Line-Green then Calm-Purple) — left as-is rather than "restored," since his own automatic conductor already reselects a destination continuously on its own ~20s cadence (confirmed still cycling normally afterward, write sequence advancing under normal automatic load) and there is no separate "prior active set" to restore to that the conductor wasn't already about to replace anyway; `room_controls.json` fields were never written by this task and read back unchanged at the end (`display_mode: "default"`, `global_transition_ms: 0`, `scene_transition_ms_gentle/hard: 300/200`) — `ambient_mode` was observed to change on its own between reads (`"off"` → `"always"` → `"off"`) across this session, not caused by anything this task wrote; light ownership was independently observed transiting `handing-over` → `released` → `spectra`/`live` early in the session (not initiated by this task) and was `spectra`/`live`/healthy throughout the actual testing and at the end. |
| §84 | LOOKAHEAD — an unresolved `fire_scene` trigger's transition lands on the trigger too, by committing the scene pick early instead of leaving it unresolved forever | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/spectra-trigger-lookahead-lead, verified offline (pytest + a real `check_triggers.py` production-wiring pass), no write reached his live `:8010` | **§82 shipped dead for him**: `_scene_transition_lead_ms`'s own conservative rule ("no lead rather than a wrong one") returns 0 whenever `scene_id is None` — and correlated against his real trigger corpus, **0 of 22,013 `fire_scene` triggers ever carry a resolved `scene_id`**; every one leaves the pick to the kernel at fire time. Measured live: 100 real fires, every one at or after its stored timestamp, up to 3,144ms late, zero early — the feature never engaged once. His design, verbatim: "peek the upcoming trigger within a bounded horizon, resolve what that fire will actually do, compute the transition from that, and move the start moment so the midpoint still lands on the mark." Anchoring unchanged (scene mid-point / a momentary flare's first switch, per §82). **The horizon (ours to pick, per his ask) reuses `transition_phases.MAX_LEAD_MS` (5000ms) rather than a second, disconnected constant** — named `LOOKAHEAD_HORIZON_MS` in `trigger_engine.py`: no lead this feature (or §82's plain rule) can ever compute exceeds that cap, so a shorter horizon would arrive too late to cover the worst case, and a longer one buys nothing (no lead could use the extra notice) while only widening the window a pinned pick's validity could drift before fire. **The design does not actually predict anything — it moves the DECISION itself earlier, once, and never repeats it**: `TriggerEngine._pin_for` draws a real pick from the SAME kernel/pool functions `_fire()` would otherwise call, the FIRST time (and only the first time) a trigger enters the horizon; that pick is cached (`_PinnedPick`, positively or negatively — a "nothing to fire" draw is cached too, so it's never retried either, which matters: retrying a probabilistic kernel draw until it stops saying "nothing" would silently raise how often a trigger fires at all, compared to today's single draw) and `_fire()` reuses it VERBATIM. There is no second draw to disagree with the first, so "predicted vs. actual" can't diverge on WHICH scene fires by construction. **The residual risk his brief names explicitly — the world moving between commit and fire — is answered by `_pin_still_valid`**, a narrow, cheap revalidation (never a re-draw) checked right before a pin is used for either the lead calculation or the fire itself: the scene still exists and isn't disabled, is still available in the room's current `display_mode` (mirrors exactly what `scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id` itself would gate on for that scene id — the same production choke point, not a second hand-typed copy of its rule), and Force Scene hasn't changed since the pin was made (a force-scene change never risks the WRONG scene — `fire_scene_by_id` always applies the CURRENT redirect to whatever id it's handed, pin or fresh draw alike — but it would make the stored lead describe the wrong target's crossfade, a timing-quality concern, not a content one, tracked separately for that reason). **Any of those failing throws the pin away and degrades to exactly today's behaviour**: a fresh, real draw at (or after) the trigger's own nominal timestamp, zero lead — late, never wrong. A rewind or a song change clears every pin outright rather than trust one across an unbounded time skip. **The observability gap, confronted rather than assumed past**: this codebase has no live instrument that observes a real scene-entry ramp AT the moment of a fire — rendered frame averages sample too coarsely to catch the switching instant, and `executor.recent_writes` never records a scene fire's own writes (a separate investigation the same week found both blind, independently). Everything here is proven at the unit level (`tests/test_lead_time_alignment.py`, 9 new tests: resolve-once-and-cache, negative-result-never-retried, a resolved `scene_id` trigger never touches the pin cache, fire reuses the pin without redrawing, an invalidated pin degrades to a fresh draw and never fires the stale scene, `_pin_still_valid`'s three gates checked directly, rewind clears pins, song-change clears pins, the horizon constant itself — 24 total in that file) and at the frame level with a real rendered device (`tests/test_trigger_engine.py` proof 11: the pinned pick's effect config is what actually lands, the kernel drawn from exactly once across many ticks; a second scenario proves an invalidated pin never renders — the fresh fallback does, on the real device, not just asserted — 11 total in that file) and against real production wiring (`scripts/check_triggers.py` §9: the real `sequencer_store` config + `selection_kernel.select` reached through `_pin_for`, a real early fire through `tick()`, `_pin_still_valid` re-checked against real `scene_store` state after disabling the pinned scene). **Neither proves the visual claim — "the midpoint really lands on the beat" — against a live fire**, because nothing in this codebase can observe a live scene-entry ramp yet; that gap is §82's, inherited unchanged, not introduced here. Named, not silently assumed away: building that observation would mean extending `fire_history.py`'s already-hooked choke points (`scene_sequencer.fire_scene_by_id`) to timestamp the `fx_seam.apply_writes` call itself, then correlating that timestamp against the trigger's own stored `timestamp_ms` on a real song — a small, bounded addition (one new timestamped record per scene fire, no new choke point), scoped but not built by this PR. **`global_transition_ms=0` (cleared on his live room the same night, his own instruction) confirmed, by static reading plus the pre-existing `test_fire_scene_falls_back_to_intensity_scaled_bounds_when_nothing_else_is_set` test, to genuinely reach the intensity-scaled default** — `scene_compiler.fire_scene`'s and `_scene_transition_lead_ms_for`'s fallback chains are identical (`entry_ramp_ms or global_transition_ms or scene_transition_ms(...)`), so a scene with no authored ramp and a zeroed global override falls through to 300ms at intensity 0 / 200ms at intensity 1, exactly as §82 shipped it; a LIVE confirmation at a real fire needs the same not-yet-built instrument named above — his room window was closed for this task so nothing was fired to check it directly. Tests: `tests/test_lead_time_alignment.py` (+9), `tests/test_trigger_engine.py` (+1 frame-level proof), `scripts/check_triggers.py` (+1 section, §9). Full suite: **720 passed, 5 pre-existing/environmental failures (identical set §80/§82 already logged, confirmed via `git stash` in this same environment), 2 skipped** — zero new failures.
