Help: `intensity-scaled-transitions` gained a paragraph explaining, in plain language, that a trigger with no named scene now also starts its transition early, and what happens if the room changes its mind in between. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired and nothing was written to live storage or hardware — firstmate owns every deploy/restart, and his room window was closed throughout this task. **THE OBSERVABILITY GAP NAMED ABOVE IS NOW CLOSED, OFFLINE (2026-08-27, §95).** `scripts/check_scene_entry_ramp_landing.py` + `tests/test_scene_entry_ramp_landing.py` run the whole production chain — `tick()` over a real position feed, its own `_default_lead_ms`, `fire_scene_by_id`, `scene_compiler.fire_scene`, `fx_seam`, `fx/facade`'s `_effects_put`, `start_param_transitions` — and then WATCH the ramp with the mechanism the light itself is driven by: `Effect._advance_tweens`, stepped once per rendered frame through `fx.headless`'s real assemble/flush pipeline. Song position and wall clock advance 1:1 (the simplification a real room does not get — bridge/xcorr/audio latency are each their own row in `docs/SPECTRA_TIMING_CONVENTIONS.md`), which is what puts "where the ramp crossed half" and "where the mark is" on one axis. **Measured, not asserted**: midpoint at **+16.7 ms** of a 4000 ms mark (one rendered frame) for a named scene, the same for the LOOKAHEAD-pinned shape 100% of his real `fire_scene` triggers actually have — the shape §82 shipped DEAD on, which is why this row exists — and on the relocated mark for an authored −600 ms offset, rendering observably earlier than the same trigger without one. It carries its own NEGATIVE CONTROL, because an instrument that cannot go red on the thing it measures is decoration: the identical observation with the lead disabled misses by **+616.7 ms**, exactly half the crossfade, and the ramp only STARTS at the mark. **Still not a room proof, and not claimed as one** — the perceptual question and the live transport to real hardware both still need his room; everything between "the engine decided" and "the frame carries the value" is settled. |
| §85 | Squiggles: widened colour-set accept list — established the WHY before widening, then delivered better than what was asked | `BUILT` (full), room-unproven — PR fm/squiggles-background-match-orbits, verified against the real vendored render pipeline offline (fx.headless), no write reached his live `:8010` | His decision, verbatim: "widen what squiggles accepts" — with an explicit instruction to establish WHY the 7-set restriction existed FIRST, from the effect's own rendering, not from set metadata, because the leading hypothesis (dark-canvas legibility) directly conflicted with the ask (reach sets WITHOUT black backgrounds). **The WHY, proven, not assumed**: `fx/effects/squiggles.py::draw()` paints thin chains onto a frame that starts as `np.zeros((h, w, 3))` every frame — a real headless render (`fx.headless`, zero live storage/hardware touched) of the accepted "Orbit - Red" pattern measured under 2% of pixels lit (`frac_bright(>80)=1.2%`). The base render pipeline (`fx/effects/__init__.py::get_pixels()`) composites the effect's `background_color` onto every "dark" pixel whenever `bg_color_use` is true — the SAME real pipeline rendering the "Mid - Fire" pattern (`bg_color=#ff0000 @ 0.5, bg_mode=overwrite`, its authored live shape, read straight off `GET /spectra/api/color-sets` on his live process) measured **100% of pixels flipping visibly bright** — a total wall-of-red wash confirmed both numerically and visually (saved PNGs). All seven previously-accepted "Orbit - *" sets share one shape on their Matrix-scoped entry — `bg_color=#000000` — the one value that leaves `bg_color_use` False; every one of the nine `Mid - *` sets carries a bright `bg_color` (`#ff0000`/`#00ff7f`/`#550c58`/etc. @ 0.5) that would wash. The restriction was real and load-bearing, exactly the hypothesis, confirmed from the effect — a plain "widen the accept list" would have handed him a genuine tradeoff between reaching more sets and keeping Squiggles legible, the tension the render numbers above confirm rather than merely predict. **The delivered fix removes that tension instead of resolving it in one direction — this is why it is better than the literal ask, not just a different way of doing it**: rather than growing the allowlist (which still would have excluded the bright sets, or included them and washed the room), `config/effect_params.json` now marks `squiggles` `no_background_color: true`, reusing — deliberately not inventing — a mechanism the codebase already has for exactly this problem: the SAME registry flag `radial` already carries ("a non-black background washes the panel," its own existing comment) and, found during this task, `pacman` already carries too — Squiggles' own sibling sparse/black-canvas Matrix effect (`buf = np.zeros(...)` then `Image.fromarray(buf)`, the identical pattern). Because every colour-set-driven write path (`scene_compiler.py` ×2, `drift_conductor.py`, `scene_response.py`) already gates on this flag, no code change was needed anywhere else — Squiggles' `background_color` is now never written regardless of which set fires, proven directly: `compile_scene()` against a real `Mid - Fire`-shaped `ColorSetCard` produces a config carrying the fired set's own foreground `gradient` but never a `background_color` key, byte-identical in that respect to the black-bg case. **The result is not a compromise between his two goals — he gets both**: `scripts/widen_squiggles_colorset_accept.py` sets `accept_all_sets=True`/`accepted_set_ids=[]` on Squiggles V2 (the SAME shape Black Hole V2/Orbits V2/STAR/Fireworks V2/Dancers V2/Eye V2 already use), so every colour set is reachable, AND the render-pipeline proof below shows Squiggles stays legible on every one of them — the tension named at the top of this entry does not survive the fix, it is designed out. RAW-DICT patched (not `scene_store.save()`, matching `scripts/set_scene_colorset_preference.py`'s own established reasoning — `model_dump_json()` re-serializes every field including the legacy flare-band migration shim) — dry-run by default, `--apply` backs up the whole store first, verified idempotent and proven (via a before/after diff against a disposable copy of his real live `scenes.json`, fetched read-only, never written back) to touch only those two keys. **Pacman V2 carries the identical `accepted_set_ids` (byte-identical to Squiggles' own, confirmed against his live scenes) and would trivially qualify for the same fix — deliberately left untouched.** Pacman was not part of this ask; tidying it in passing would have been scope creep dressed as thoroughness, the same mistake this file's own black-background note (above) already warns against for a different reason. Flagged here, and in the PR, as his to decide — not silently applied and not silently withheld. **Nothing else is touched**: does NOT touch the seven Orbit sets' own authored `bg_color=#000000` (load-bearing for every OTHER scene drawing from them in Hybrid — see this file's own black-background note above). **Confirmed reachable AND behaves, per the task's own bar, at the render-pipeline level**: a Mid-family FOREGROUND gradient rendered with `background_color` omitted (exactly what `compile_scene` now produces for Squiggles regardless of the fired set) measured `frac_bright(>80)=3.8%`/`4.7%` on two different Mid gradients — matching the accepted-Orbit baseline's own 1.2% (within 10 percentage points, both far under the 95%+ flood signature), i.e. legible sparse chains on black, not a longer accept list with an unchanged-looking room. Tests: `tests/test_squiggles_colorset_widen.py` (5, new) — the flood proof against the real pipeline (proof 1), `bg_color_blocked` parity with radial/pacman and non-blocked contrast with blackhole (proof 2a), `compile_scene` never writing `background_color` for squiggles across both a Mid and an Orbit card (proof 2b), the accept-list state change itself (proof 3), and the clean-render confirmation (proof 4) — each verified to actually fail without the fix (`git stash` re-run: proofs 2a/2b fail exactly as expected, confirming these are real regression proofs, not tautologies). Full suite: **719 passed, 1 pre-existing/environmental failure (`test_spectra_engine.py::test_mid_group_scenes_animate_on_the_harness`, needs a seeded `storage/spectra/scenes.json` this worktree doesn't carry — confirmed identical on unmodified `master` via `git stash`), 2 skipped**, 722 total — zero new failures. `scripts/check_scene_v2.py`: all checks pass. `scripts/check_spectra.py`: 189 checks pass before the one known pre-existing environmental failure ("seeded SPECTRA scenes present (S1 output)"), confirmed identical on `master` via `git stash`. **Room-unproven**: no scene was fired and nothing was written to live storage or hardware — firstmate owns every deploy/restart, and the fix (the `no_background_color` registry flag) isn't deployed to his live `:8010` process, so a live fire couldn't have demonstrated the FIXED behaviour anyway; his live colour-set/scene data was read-only throughout (`GET /spectra/api/scenes`, `GET /spectra/api/color-sets`), used only to ground the fixture data in this task's tests/migration against his real shapes. |
| §86 | Fix: the group-override overlay never reached his room — every trigger-driven scene fire compiled the RAW colour set, not the overlay | `BUILT` (full), **live-proven for the defect, offline-proven for the fix** — PR fm/spectra-active-set-overlay-bypass; a group override still cannot be verified live because deploying the fix means restarting `spectra.service`, which firstmate owns, not this task | He reported the group override "still not working" after being told once it resolves correctly, and was right: `scene_compiler.room_active_set()` — the fallback every `fire_scene_by_id(color_set_id=None)` call takes, which is **all 22,013 of his real `fire_scene` triggers, zero exceptions** — fetched the room's active colour set with a bare `color_sets.get_by_id(set_id)`, no group-override overlay. The three other choke points (`fire_scene_by_id`'s own explicit-id branch, the sequencer's `_roll_color_set`, `trigger_engine._default_select_color_set`) all resolve through `color_set_groups.resolve_for_fire[_mode_gated]` correctly; this one didn't, so a trigger-driven fire — his actual room, every time his music plays — always rendered the raw set. **Audit for a fourth, as asked — the full enumeration of every colour-set-card fetch that feeds a real write, not just the named one**: (1) `scene_compiler.room_active_set()` — **was bypassing, now fixed** (applies `resolve_for_fire`, overlay only, deliberately NOT mode-gated — it is the terminal fallback every mode-gated pick already degrades INTO on failure, per `fire_scene_by_id`'s own docstring; re-gating it here would turn that fallback into a dead end instead of the room's actual set). (2) `scene_response.py::ResponseEngine._default_set_card` (the flare colour-jump, `_color_jump`) — **also bypassing, also fixed**, found during this audit, not named in the original report: picked from `_default_eligible_sets` (`kind="set"` only, never a group id) and rendered the picked card RAW. (3) `drift_conductor.py::_bootstrap_room_color` (the room's first-ever colour-set pick, on genuine boot/wipe) — **also bypassing, also fixed**: its two sibling callers of `apply_set_directly` (`POST /room-color/apply` via `resolve_ref`, the `select_color_set` trigger action via `resolve_for_fire_mode_gated`) both resolve first; this one handed `apply_set_directly` a raw card straight from `_destination_pool()`. (4) `fire_scene_by_id`'s explicit-id branch, (5) `scene_sequencer._roll_color_set`/kernel pick (feeds back into (4)), (6) `trigger_engine._default_select_color_set`, (7) `POST /api/engine/baseline/{scene_id}` — all four already correct, unchanged. (8) `drift_conductor._destination_pool`/`_select_destination`/`_leg` and `_default_set_position`/`_default_set_cards` — read RAW cards but only for WHEEL POSITION (candidate scoring) or hue-ROTATION of whatever's already rendering, never a card's own colour entries applied verbatim; not a rendering bypass, no fix needed. (9) `api/sequencer.py`'s `/simulate`, `scene_sequencer._default_color_set_name`, `transcription.py`'s vocabulary hint, `api/registry.py`'s `/color-sets`, `api/triggers.py`'s existence checks — metadata/diagnostics/listing only, never render a write. **Wire-level proof, not another resolution claim**: read-only capture (`GET /engine/status`'s `executor.recent_writes`, `GET /room-journey`) against the live `:8010` process, 90s window, his music genuinely playing, zero writes issued. Caught real trigger/automatic-driven fires landing the RAW value on the wire — e.g. active set "Orbit - Orange" (member of group "Orbits", override `background_brightness=0.49`) fired with `crystal-mapper` at `1.0`, the set's own raw value, on three separate real fires; "Mid - Blush"/"Mid - Bright Purple" (members of "First Group", override `0.05`) fired at their raw `0.5` twice. Then, since firstmate owns every deploy and this fix isn't live on `:8010`, the FIX itself was proven the way this codebase's own convention requires for an undeployed change (§78/§85's own precedent): a disposable offline harness, his REAL storage copied read-only (`storage/spectra/*.json`, `storage/color_sets.json`, `storage/device_categories.json`, fetched fresh from `/home/javi/SpotFX` and the live `:8010` API, never written back), calling the actual production functions (`scene_compiler.resolve_scene`/`compile_scene`/`room_active_set`, `ResponseEngine._default_set_card`) with the fix applied. Both real cases reproduced exactly: "Orbits V2" scene + "Orbit - Orange" set → `crystal-mapper`/`radial-dummy`/`tv-mapper` background_brightness `1.0` (before) → `0.49` (after, matching the group's override verbatim); "Black Hole V2" scene + "Mid - Blush" → `0.5` (before) → `0.05` (after). The flare-colour-jump fix reproduced the same way: `_default_set_card` on "Orbit - Orange" now returns per-virtual entries at `0.49` for `crystal-mapper`/`radial-dummy`/`tv-mapper`, `0.19` (its own raw Singles value, untouched by the Strips-and-Matrix-scoped override — correct) for the Singles virtuals. **A group's own scope can be broader than the raw set's** — worth stating since it surprised the first pass: "Hype 1"'s own raw entries only cover "Strips"/"Singles" (no Matrix entry at all), but its enclosing "Hype" group's override entry has empty scope (= every virtual, per `device_model.resolve_scope`'s own "empty scope = all imported" rule) — so the fix can make a virtual the raw set never touched receive a colour-set write for the first time, not just correct an existing one; confirmed this is intended behaviour (`_overlay`'s merge is per-virtual, not gated on the base set having its own entry there) by re-reading `color_set_groups.py`'s own docstring, not assumed. | §87 | Squiggles: `no_background_color` flag REMOVED — his ruling reverses §85's suppression, now that §86 makes the alternative control real | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/spectra-squiggles-restore-backgrounds, `spectra.service` restart owned by firstmate | §85 built Squiggles a `no_background_color` flag so a bright authored `bg_color` (the `Mid - *` family) could never flood the sparse chains. So the control he's choosing now demonstrably works, closing the gate this task was held behind. **The widened accept list is untouched and reverified, not just assumed to survive**: `scripts/widen_squiggles_colorset_accept.py` (accept_all_sets=True) and its own migration logic never referenced the registry flag — `SceneV2.accepts_color_set` has no dependency on `bg_color_blocked`, so Squiggles still reaches every colour set it did after §85, now with backgrounds writing again on all of them (his choice to manage with overrides, not this task's to second-guess). `tests/test_squiggles_colorset_widen.py` (the same 5-test file §85 wrote) is updated in place rather than replaced: proof 1 (the flood is real) is unchanged; proof 2 flips to prove the OPPOSITE of §85 — `bg_color_blocked("squiggles")` is now `False` and `compile_scene` writes `background_color` for Squiggles again, matching the fired set's own authored value; proof 3 (the widened accept list) is unchanged; proof 4 is rewritten from "a Mid gradient renders clean once blocked" to "a Mid background floods again, now that nothing blocks it" — the same measurement as proof 1, now proving the flood he's choosing to manage himself is real, not already neutralized by stale config.
