| §88 | Flare-kind LANES: all lanes fire together, ONE kind picked per lane (even weights) — his reversal of the §81-addendum decision, driven by the colour-rotate placement | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/flare-lanes-pick-one, firstmate owns deploy; nothing attached to any band by this build | His words, verbatim: *"regarding the color rotate, I think this is the entire point of having lanes. The 'color lane' should fire one of the many options in that lane. I know you took lanes to mean something else. It should be more similar to spotfx, so we could have two different color changing flares in the lane and it would randomly pick one of them by some kind of weighting. In fact, later, we might use curves to handle the weighting. For now, just even weights."* And clarifying: *"yes, all lanes fire together, but pick on[e] action within each lane."* **This is his own reversal of the §81-addendum decision** (2026-08-19: offered exactly this mechanism, he kept all-attached-fires — "I think i like the current method better than mine") — the colour rotate changed his mind: declared on all nine scenes, attached to nothing, because band-attaching it meant it AND the Colour Jump both firing. The mechanism is the legacy MorphLane/`_pick_morph_lanes` shape (`models/music_event.py`, `services/trigger_engine.py`: each lane a pool of alternatives, one weighted pick per lane, all picks fire concurrently, re-resolved every fire) ported onto band attachments. **Model**: `FlareBand.kind_lanes: dict[str, str]` (`spectra/models/scene.py` — kind name → lane name; sharing a name = one pool; a dict keyed by kind name makes two-lane membership structurally impossible), validated `⊆ kinds` with non-empty lane names. **The blast-radius guarantee, the whole safety of the change**: a kind ABSENT from the map is its own implicit one-member lane — a pick-one over a pool of one is that one — so the empty default (`{}`) means every band predating the field fires ALL of its kinds byte-identically (25 of his 28 real bands hold >1 kind; grouping-by-inference was named in the dispatch as the failure mode and never considered). **ZERO data migration: no scene file was read, rewritten, or needs to be** — old JSON loads with `kind_lanes == {}`, and the legacy flare-band migration shim (`_migrate_flare_kinds`) passes an authored map through untouched (proven). **Engine**: `scene_response.resolve_lane_picks(band, rng)` — groups `band.kinds` keys by lane, `rng.choice` per >1-member pool (EVEN WEIGHTS; this one call is the seam a future per-member weight/curve plugs into, deferred by his own words), returns picked names in `kinds`' own insertion order — lanes decide WHO fires, never execution order, so the same-param precedence tie-break (§81) is untouched. Wired at the top of `_execute_band`, so `on_event` (real flares/charges/lulls/drops) and `on_update` (minimum dwell's placeholder double-intensity flare) share the pick by construction. Every fire with a genuine pool records `lane_picks` (`{lane, picked, pool}`) into the surge record/show log — "why didn't my other colour flare run" is a log lookup, not a mystery. **The three forward peeks (`band_trigger_offset_ms`, `momentary_switch_would_glide`, `color_rotate_lead_ms`) deliberately aggregate over the full POSSIBILITY SET** (every pool member): the pick is random at fire time, so no peek can know the winner — a losing member can contribute a lead the winner doesn't need (fires a hair early, the same documented max-of-contributors trade, never late); pinning the pick at peek time would be a new mechanism nobody asked for. **UI** (`FlareLaneRack.tsx`, `ResponseTab.tsx`, `flareKindOps.ts`): a lane renders its members stacked with "— or —" between them and a "⚄ picks 1 of N" badge; drop a kind ON an occupied lane to POOL it (fresh lane names minted per band, "lane 1"…); drop on the slim strip before a lane / an empty lane for a lane of its OWN — the §81 shift-not-swap insert gesture kept, because lane order still decides same-param precedence. Drop targets are anchored by kind NAME, not lane index (`LaneRef.mode`/`anchor`), so a same-band move can't mis-resolve when removing the dragged kind renumbers pools. Rename/delete/detach/move all cascade into `kind_lanes`, and every mutation prunes one-member pools (`prunedLanes`) so the stored map stays canonical (only genuine pools persist; the model still tolerates a singleton entry so a mid-edit save never refuses). `types.ts` also gained the missing `'color_rotate'` in `FlareKind.type`'s union (pre-existing gap — his real scenes already carry the type) and the rack/palette icon for it (🔄). **Deliberately NOT done**: nothing attached to any band (the rotate's placement is still his call — this makes it possible, not made); no weighting UI/storage (even weights only); no charge/lull/drop-specific behaviour (the pick applies wherever bands already execute). Help (Order 20): `tab-flares-lanes` rewritten for pick-one pools (the old "a lane is a POSITION, not a new stored idea" paragraph was made false by this build and is gone), `flare-kinds`' colour-rotate paragraph now points at pooling it with the Colour Jump. Tests: `tests/test_flare_lanes.py` (10, new — model default/validation/migration-passthrough, pick-function order+distribution proofs, and frame-level on the real vendored pipeline: a no-pool multi-kind band lands EVERY kind's write, a pooled band lands exactly the winner's write with the loser's param provably untouched, both members win across seeded fires, `on_update` shares the pick). `scripts/check_spectra.py` gained a LANES section (blast-radius + pick-one/re-resolve, 213 checks pass before the same pre-existing seeded-STAR environmental failure, confirmed byte-identical running `master`'s own copy of the script in this worktree). `scripts/check_flare_lane_ops.mjs` (new, the `check_flare_preview_frontend_loop.mjs` precedent, improved: esbuild — vite's own dependency — transpiles the REAL `flareKindOps.ts` verbatim instead of regex-extracting formulas) proves the UI's pure mutations: pool derivation/ordering, join minting/reusing lane names with contiguous placement and carried scale, insert leaving a pool with the leftover singleton pruned, cross-band prune, self-drop no-op, rename/delete cascading into `kind_lanes`. Full suite: **915 passed, 6 pre-existing/environmental failures, 2 skipped** (`test_crystal_activation_verify.py` ×3, `test_legacy_trigger_migration.py` real-corpus count vs the worktree's stale gitignored storage ×1, `test_settings_agent_cli.py` missing `mcp` package ×2), re-confirmed identical with this build's runtime files reverted to `master` in place — zero new failures. `spectra/web`: `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` clean. **Proven end-to-end on a disposable isolated instance** (§81's own pattern, this time with FULL network isolation per the flare-preview lesson: `SPECTRA_STORAGE_DIR`+`SPECTRA_PORT`+`SPECTRA_BRIDGE_WS_URL`+`LEDFX_HOST/PORT` all repointed/dead, recording executor, killed by exact PID — never `pkill -f`): a real headless-Chromium drag of a third kind ONTO the colour pool produced "⚄ picks 1 of 3" and Save persisted all three under one lane name in the isolated `scenes.json` through the real API/model; dragging it back out to an empty lane persisted the pool back to two — both gestures, both directions, storage-verified not just watched; phone width (390×844) shows the pooled rack with zero page-level horizontal scroll. His live `:8010` got exactly one read-only GET (post-teardown liveness confirm). **Room-unproven in the deploy sense**: his scenes carry no `kind_lanes` until he (or a directed follow-up) pools something — deploy and live verification are firstmate's. |
| §89 | FIREWORK BURST flare — a fifth `FlareKind` type: intensity-scaled payoff rockets explode ON the trigger, attached to every Fireworks V2 energy band | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/fireworks-burst-flare; code needs a `spectra.service` restart, and the data write (`scripts/add_fireworks_burst_flare.py --apply`) must run only AFTER that restart (an older process re-reading a store carrying the new type fails validation on load) — both firstmate's | His ask, verbatim: "make a flare meant to line up with a burst of fireworks on top of the standard ones in the fireworks scene. 3 for 0 intensity and 6 for 1 intensity, scaled linearly. put it in every energy intensity in the scene." **Mechanism — deliberately NOT `beat_burst`** (which his existing Fireworks patches drive): `beat_burst` only launches on the NEXT beat (`if self._beat_pending: bursts += self.beat_burst`), up to a beat-period after the mark — beat-quantised is not lined up. Instead both vendored fireworks effects gained a `burst_rockets` config key (int 0–12, default 0, ADVANCED — `fx/VENDOR.md` deviation #15): edge-detected in `config_updated` exactly like the phase key (a stale persisted count never explodes on a fresh instance, and is reset to an honest 0), consumed on the next draw, self-reset via the same sanctioned in-render `_apply_config` path the drop's phase auto-reset uses so an identical later count edges again. The spawn is the drop payoff's OWN shape, factored into `_payoff_burst_at()` and shared verbatim by `_rocket_payoff()` (unchanged) and the new `_flare_burst()`: fireworks1d's two staggered pairs per origin (PAYOFF_SPEED + 0.6× stagger, PAYOFF_LIFE, bright 1.0, `ignore_cap` — his strips' own `max_blobs=6` can never swallow it), fireworks' giant near-center burst per origin (`burst_size*2.5` min 24). Engine: `FlareKind.type="firework_burst"` (the color_rotate no-authored-knobs shape — model rejects params/gain/hold_ms/jump), `scene_response.firework_burst_rockets` = `_intensity_scaled(i, FIREWORK_BURST_ROCKETS_GENTLE=3, HARD=6)` rounded, `_firework_burst()` jumps the key onto every virtual whose live effect is in the new `fx.device_model.FIREWORK_BURST_EFFECTS` (`{fireworks, fireworks1d}` — like the phase keys, deliberately NOT in the param registry, so band patches/editor surfaces can never reach it and concurrency with every other kind is structural). **ADDS, never replaces** (the color_rotate endpoint precedent settles "on top of the standard ones"): 3→6 is what THIS flare adds; the spawn is purely additive — proven on the real vendored pipeline that the scene's own particles keep their lives/colours/ages and phase state through repeated bursts. **Anchor family: drop, not flare** — the write is an instant jump, the burst STARTS on the mark; lead=0 falls out naturally (no `kind_lead_ms` branch needed). NO release queue — nothing to drain at any of the four drain points (engine.fire_response_event / fire_scene_update_event / flare_preview.build_timeline / flare_preview_hold.open_hold); the particles age out on their own PAYOFF_LIFE clock. The scrubbing preview works unmodified (timeline shows the one instant jump; fire_at == anchor == mark). **Composition found while proving, correct by design**: his own "Flare patch …" kinds land `burst_size` 6/9/14 in the same fire BEFORE the burst executes (the fixed execution order), so a high-intensity burst's rockets are bigger — exactly as the real drop payoff would be after that patch (per-rocket 24/24/35 measured across his three bands). **Placement — his explicit instruction, unlike every prior kind migration**: `scripts/add_fireworks_burst_flare.py` (raw-dict patch, dry-run default, `--apply`, `--revert` exact inverse, idempotent) declares "Firework Burst" on Fireworks V2 AND attaches it to all three flare bands at ×1.0 — directly, NEVER in a lane (a lane would make it an alternative to the standard kinds instead of on top of them). His data contract is executable, not promised: the script takes a timestamped backup, then RE-READS the written file and structurally verifies the diff (every other scene byte-identical, only the declaration + three attachments on Fireworks V2, `kind_lanes` untouched) — demonstrated against a fresh copy of his real live store: byte-level diff shows exactly 18 changed lines (all intended; zero incidental reformatting — the store is already indent=2/ensure_ascii), re-apply is a no-op, revert is byte-identical to the original. UI: 🎆 icon + TYPE_HINT in ResponseTab/FlareLaneRack, `types.ts` union, help topic `flare-kinds` retitled "the five types" with its own paragraph (a `color_rotate` TYPE_HINT that was missing entirely was added in the same pass — incidental, reported). Tests: `tests/test_firework_burst.py` (8, new — model shape, his exact numbers, targeting/scale at the recording level, frame-level additive proofs on both real vendored effects incl. repeat-fire edge re-arm and the stale-persisted-count rule, concurrency with a shape kind). Executable spec: `scripts/check_firework_burst.py` (read-only against his real scenes.json; migrated-in-memory scene fired through the real ResponseEngine onto a real two-virtual headless host at his real shapes — crystal 72×37 + 17px strip). Full suite: passes with only the pre-existing environmental failures this file already logs (`test_crystal_activation_verify.py` ×3, `test_legacy_trigger_migration.py` ×1 — confirmed byte-identical re-running each at unmodified base `933c06c` in place). `spectra/web`: `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` clean. **Room-unproven**: nothing written to live storage, no live fire — deploy order (restart, THEN `--apply`) is firstmate's. |
| §90 | PARAM ORPHAN WATCHDOG — a periodic sweep that finds any effect param sitting away from its engine baseline with NOTHING holding it there and restores it, loudly | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/param-orphan-watchdog, `spectra.service` restart is firstmate's; no data touched | His ask, verbatim: "some kind of watchdog system to make sure that parameters are set correctly like that" — after finding his effect stuck running backwards (a momentary `reverse` flare whose release never landed) with no way back, the second time that evening his room was left in a state nobody was holding (the other: a preview hold left open, §"MAXIMUM HOLD CEILING"). The stuck-reverse CAUSE is a separate task (reverse-flare-glide-and-stuck); this is the net under it, not a replacement — a system that can only be left wrong is a different thing from one that notices and recovers. **Mechanism** (`spectra/services/param_watchdog.py`, own supervised task in `spectra/app.py`'s lifespan beside the frame watchdog/reconciler/ambient verifier/flare-preview sweep): every `SWEEP_INTERVAL_S`=10s it reads each engine-tracked virtual's LIVE effect config off the in-process render host (`Effect._config` + `_tweens`, under the effect's own lock — the same lock every engine write takes) and compares every param the conductor holds a baseline for (`VirtualState.param_baseline`, seeded from the scene fire's resolved writes) against the value a release would return it to (`ResponseEngine.release_target`, a thin public wrapper over the SAME `_carried_value` `flush_releases` resolves — one definition of baseline, never two). **"Nothing holding it" is three structural holders, each checked fresh per sweep**: (1) a pending momentary release for that (virtual, param) (`ResponseEngine.pending_release_keys`, new read-only accessor — a late release is never mistaken for a lost one, however long the loop stalls); (2) an active drift creep/follow owning it (`conductor.mechanisms`); (3) a tween in flight on the live effect for that key (a release glide, a permanent move's ease-in, an entry ramp). **The discriminator that makes it safe — established before the restore was written**: a PERMANENT kind (and a dice re-roll, held gain, colour jump's brightness) writes its landed value into the surge `carry` → `conductor.on_surge` MOVES `param_baseline`/`brightness_baseline` — a permanent move IS the baseline, live == baseline, nothing to restore; a MOMENTARY kind never touches carry/param_baseline, so live != baseline exactly for the spike + release — holder 1 then holder 3. The only way to be away from baseline with none of the three holders is a lost/skipped/rejected release. Proven on the real vendored pipeline, not argued: a real permanent `reverse: True` move + a permanent gain are left alone across a minute of sweeps; a real in-flight momentary hold is left alone for two minutes of stalled-release sweeps; a release glide mid-flight is a holder however far the wall clock runs ahead of the frames. **Out of scope by name, each a legitimate writer the engine's bookkeeping doesn't follow**: `background_brightness`/`background_color` (the conductor's own colour-set landings write them to the wire without moving `param_baseline` — a pre-existing gap, not closed here; Dark mode's `dark_lock` clamp; Light mode's forced background); string keys (`gradient` etc.); any param NOT in `param_baseline` (no baseline, no opinion — restoring to a schema default would reintroduce exactly the brightness carry-forward class of flash); a live effect whose TYPE differs from the conductor's picture (counted `type_mismatch`, never acted on — e.g. Dark→Hybrid's stale-snapshot repaint landing an older scene's type); `brightness` is compared against baseline × ANY `brightness_multiplier` seen since the last scene fire (a multiplier change doesn't rewrite live brightness until the next brightness write — pre-existing; the watchdog never acts on the dimmer's behalf). Stands down entirely (sweep skipped, suspicions dropped) while the engine is dark, the live stack is down, or a Colour Set Preview / flare-preview hold is active. **Threshold, stated and justified**: `ORPHAN_GRACE_S`=30s. Worst observed legitimate away-from-baseline window: authored hold (default 0.25s; the longest `hold_ms` in his real scenes, read live 2026-08-21 off `:8010`, is 500ms) + `PULSE_RELEASE_S` 1.5s + the measured live release overrun (up to ~1.9s vs 500ms authored, still unexplained) ≈ 3.5s — and every ms of it is also covered by holder 1 or 3, so the grace is margin on top of a structural check. 30s ≈ 8× that, longer than a whole 20s drift leg; a mismatch must persist across ≥2 consecutive sweeps with the SAME expected value (a baseline that moves mid-suspicion restarts the clock). A restore lands 30–40s after the last legitimate holder let go. A restore that doesn't take is retried on the same grace at most `RESTORE_ATTEMPT_LIMIT`=3 times, then GIVEN UP ON at CRITICAL, named in status, until the baseline changes or the next scene fire — never an every-sweep fight with a rejecting schema. **Loud, not silent**: every restore logs virtual/param/effect/found/restored-to/orphaned-for/method at WARNING ("PARAM ORPHAN RESTORED — … find what lost the release"), records to a NEW `fire_history` bucket `"watchdog"` (durable count + show-log entry — visible on the Review page timeline), appears in `engine.status()["param_watchdog"]` (Status page engine card shows the count, suspected, given-up, standing-down reason, with a HelpLink), and `GET /spectra/api/liveness` carries an additive `param_watchdog` summary (restores_total/suspected/given_up/last_restore — informational, never part of `healthy`: a recurring orphan is something to SEE, not something a restart fixes). **Not touched**: the stuck-reverse defect itself, his scenes/triggers/colour sets/devices, any authored hold value. Help (Order 20): `param-watchdog` under the ownership/lighting-state section, linked from the Status page. Timing quantities recorded in `docs/SPECTRA_TIMING_CONVENTIONS.md`. Tests: `tests/test_param_watchdog.py` (14 — real-pipeline orphan restore incl. log/status/show-log assertions; real permanent move + permanent gain left alone; real in-flight hold left alone 120s then releases normally; real release glide in flight as holder; fake-deps proofs of creep ownership, the gate forgetting suspicions, the multiplier history, the give-up at CRITICAL and its clear on the next scene fire, the out-of-scope keys/type mismatch/bool-vs-number, a baseline moving mid-suspicion restarting the clock; engine status + the real liveness route via TestClient; the production gate standing down on a dark process). `tests/conftest.py` gained an autouse `_isolated_param_watchdog` reset (module-global state, same shape as dwell/preview_pause). **Room-unproven**: nothing written to live storage, no live fire; the one live read was a GET of `/spectra/api/scenes` for the hold_ms maximum. |
| §91 | REVERSE FLARE: the 1160ms "500ms" hold ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED (hold timed from the spike, not from the end of the fire's write burst); releases owned per fire; toggles release instantly; a never-authored param's spike is never stranded; STAR's "stuck in reverse" named as a data shape | `FIXED`/`BUILT` (code), **not deployed** — PR fm/reverse-flare-glide-and-stuck; `spectra.service` restart is firstmate's; no data touched | His report, verbatim: *"if shape flares are too close together the momentary reverse gets tripped up and then it gets stuck in Reverse ... either find a way to prevent this or some kind of watchdog to make sure parameters are set correctly."* The watchdog half is §90 (PR #186, kept; its `pending_release_keys`/`release_target` accessors adapted to the new entry shape in the same commit). This is the PREVENTION half. **Root cause of the overrun (tenth cause, after nine eliminations — the bool-glide theory briefed for this task was checked first and does NOT hold: both tween engines, vendored `fx/` and `/home/javi/ledfx-src`, classify a bool target "instant", and the existing toggle test already proved the value lands within a frame of flush)**: `engine.fire_response_event` created its `sleep(hold_s)` tasks only AFTER `on_event` had finished its ENTIRE serial write burst; on his live room that burst is ~13 facade writes at ~30ms (read off `executor.recent_writes` seq spacing: spike seq 164 at t=50.597, gain jumps 166-170, colour glides 171-175 to t=50.944, reverse release seq 181 at t=51.614 — **1.02s after the spike, 500ms authored**). Fix: `scene_response.PendingRelease` entries (dataclass; `fire_seq`, `hold_s`, `instant`, `armed_at`/`due_at`, `return_to`, `scheduled`, `flushing`) are armed right after each virtual's spike write lands; `responses.take_release_schedule()` hands the engine one `ReleaseGroup(fire_seq, hold_s, due_at)` per distinct arm instant; `engine._release_group`/`flare_preview_hold._release_group` sleep `responses.seconds_until(due_at)` then `flush_releases(hold_s, fire_seq=, due_by=)`. **Ownership + supersession**: a fire's task drains only its own entries; a later spike on the same (virtual, param) supersedes an earlier pending release (the param returns when the LAST hold matures — overlapping holds extend, never cut short; the old by-hold_s drain let the first of two 500ms flares release the second mid-hold). **Toggle release = instant jump** (the sign-control rule generalised; the preview ruler's END for a toggle kind is now hold+1ms, not hold+1.5s — `scripts/check_flare_preview.py` polygon case updated). **Never stranded**: `return_to` resolved at spike time (`_resting_value`: carried baseline, else `fx.device_model.resting_default` = schema default → registry default, coerced to the registry kind; new `device_model.schema_default`/`resting_default`) is used when no baseline is carried at flush — his **Orbits V2 and Squiggles V2 Strips (`orbits1d`, registers `reverse`, unauthored by both entries)** were a real stuck-from-the-first-flare case (`_entry_config` compiles authored params only, so `param_baseline` never had the key). One virtual's failed release write no longer loses the others' (per-virtual try/except, logged; §90 is the backstop). A concurrent-flush double-write (entries re-selected by the next group's task mid-write) was caught by the instrumented five-virtual run and fixed with the `flushing` marker before ship. **STAR — data, reported**: live `:8010` shows STAR attaches the PERMANENT "Reverse Direction" to its 0.35-0.7 and 0.7-1.0 bands and the momentary to 0-0.35; a permanent carry moves the baseline, so after one mid/high flare every later momentary reverse releases to reversed — stuck by construction (`test_star_permanent_reverse_makes_every_later_momentary_reverse_stick`, real radial); Fireworks V2's "Reverse Direction" is momentary despite its name. His call whether that attachment is intended; the scene backups show both kinds declared unattached by script, the attachments came from a later UI save. **Measured** (`tests/test_reverse_flare_release.py`, real asyncio clock): real squiggles effect 536ms window for a 500ms hold at 30ms/write; five virtuals old shape 806ms every virtual vs new 532-563ms; two flares 250ms apart 773ms (the later hold's full length); never-authored `reverse` releases to False; flaky-virtual release drops only that virtual; STAR data shape. Help `flare-kinds` paragraphs updated (toggle release instant, hold timed from landing, per-fire ownership); `docs/SPECTRA_TIMING_CONVENTIONS.md` row. **Room-unproven** — the live trace above was read, nothing was fired. |
| §92 | TAKE-BACK FROM RELEASED TOLERATES A PARTIAL ACTIVATION — one unreachable/slow device no longer darkens the whole room; the skipped light is named where he looks and rechecked until it comes back | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/take-back-must-not-darken-the-whole-room-9a; `spectra.service` restart is firstmate's; no data touched | His ruling, verbatim: *"one unreachable device must not be able to keep his entire room dark."* Read off his live record/journal (read-only): 2026-08-21 19:21–19:26, SIX take-backs from `released` aborted on `dining-table` (WLED, `wled-8a3534.local` — "could not confirm live state: ValueError('WLED None: Failed to connect')", i.e. the mDNS name did not resolve), each abort tearing down the 20 devices that HAD come up, until he fixed the device himself; 2026-08-20 09:47 the same abort on `sconce-kitchen-left`/`-right` (VALID IPs, "Failed to connect" — merely slow; the retry 45 s later succeeded). **Where**: `handover.run_handover` step 2 — `verify_active()` False on ANY device gap → the except branch deactivated the whole to-side and `abort()` landed at `released`, which is darkness, not safety; aborting never saved the unreachable light. **The three questions answered before changing anything** — (1) *load-bearing devices?* none: the vendored render loop's `_flush_simple_segments` skips an inactive (never-resolved) device per flush and the rest of a shared virtual keeps painting; a resolved-but-silent DDP target just drops packets (`OSError` caught, `_online=False`); no virtual reads from another device; the audio hub is device-independent — one dark fixture can only DIM the room, never corrupt it (proven on the real pipeline: his `single-color-effect` shape, `porch-rail`+`dining-table` on one virtual, keeps driving the dummy half); (2) *why did it abort?* the strict rollback was deliberate for a handover FROM A RUNNING SHOW (a working show at risk, a real from-world to land on) and run_handover already special-cased `from_released` twice (skip quiesce, skip restore) — the verify/rollback policy was the one place the special case was missed; "unreachable" (no destination) vs "reachable but misbehaving" (`live=false`) are DISTINGUISHED in the report (`kind`: unresolved / unreachable / not-receiving, each its own sentence) but treated the same at the gate — neither can be repaired by darkening the rest, neither corrupts anything; a HARD failure (stack never up, or not one expected virtual driving) still aborts — owner=spectra over a wholly dark stack is the order-8 class; (3) *what does he see?* a new surface, `spectra/services/activation_report.py`. **Mechanism**: `SpectraSide.verify_active()` now also records a structured `ActivationOutcome` (`activation_outcome()`: stack_up, expected/up virtual ids, virtual_gaps, device_gaps; `.partial` = not ok AND stack up AND ≥1 expected virtual driving); `run_handover` consults it ONLY when `from_world == RELEASED` — partial → CRITICAL "PARTIAL TAKE-BACK", the to-side is NOT deactivated, `activation_report.record_from_live(...)`, `light_ownership.commit(token, detail="PARTIAL — …")` (new optional `detail` — the durable history note names the light); otherwise the raise/abort is byte-identical to before. `resume_own_room` records the same report (its policy was already tolerant; it had no UI surface). **Visible**: `POST /ownership/handover` → 200 `result:"committed-partial"` + `activation`; `GET /ownership` → `activation` (RoomOwnershipBar polls 4 s → `ActivationStrip`, amber, on EVERY page, each still-dark light by name + why + "rechecked Ns ago, retried ×N"; the take-back toast says it once); Status page "Lights at take-back" line (N/M up, each skipped light, recovered ones ✓); `GET /liveness` → `activation` (additive, informational, NEVER `healthy` — the frame watchdog's systemd dead-man reads `live.fresh()` only; a dark fixture must not restart-loop the service). **Status honesty** (the codebase's own rule): the verdict is a snapshot — `activation_report.run_supervised()` (own lifespan task, `RECHECK_INTERVAL_S`=30) re-asks each still-dark device via the SAME probe `device_gaps()` polls (`live_host.probe_device_live`, factored out — one definition of "confirmed driving"), stamps the age, marks `recovered` on confirm, refreshes WHY if a name resolves but the light stays silent, and for a driver with `_destination is None` retries the vendored driver's OWN `async_initialize()`+`activate()` (nothing in the render loop ever re-resolves an inactive device, so without this a fixed light needs a whole release+take-back cycle to collect one fixture; a resolved-but-silent device is only re-asked). The report self-gates on `live.active` and is cleared by `SpectraSide.activate()`/`deactivate()` — it can never outlive the stack. **Proved without touching his data**: `tests/test_take_back_partial.py` — policy on fakes (from-released partial commits + names; SAME outcome from a running world still rolls back strictly; hard failure from released still aborts), the REAL pipeline (real FxHost, real vendored WLED driver, a device in the exact SHAPE of his `dining-table` entry at `dining-table.invalid` — RFC 6761, never resolves, offline — through the real armed API route: 200 committed-partial, both working virtuals fresh incl. the shared one, report name/kind/address/why + the byte-identical raw reason his record carries, GET /ownership + history + liveness, recheck ages/retries, recovery activates the device), the "merely slow" class (valid loopback address, WLED client patched to time out — no port-80 dependency, no packets off-host), `tests/test_activation_report.py` (13 unit proofs), `scripts/check_ownership.py` §12b. **Help** (Order 20): new topic `take-back-skipped-light` linked from the strip and the Status page; `ownership-handover`/`ownership-resume`/`ownership-liveness`/`panic-release`/`status-page` updated. `docs/SPECTRA_HANDOVER.md` updated; timing row in `docs/SPECTRA_TIMING_CONVENTIONS.md`. **Not touched**: his scenes/triggers/colour sets/device list/fx-live config; the strict rollback for a handover from a running world; `healthy`. **Room-unproven** — built and proven offline; the live record was only read. |
| §93 | PHONE A/V-SYNC INSTRUMENT — his phone's mic + camera MEASURE the room's audio/visual offset (a number AND a stated confidence) instead of arguing it; live-feed-shaped; the camera/vision seam prepared, not built | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/phone-audio-video-capture-for-measured-a-7b; `spectra.service` restart + SPA rebuild are firstmate's; no scene/trigger/colour-set/device data touched; nothing measured is written into any setting | His words: *"use a phone to send audio and video to spectra for precise audio/visual offset... with the intention of eventually having live feeds... prepare for the ability to use this with aruco tags... Do not build, just prepare it to hook into the phone camera setup."* WHY: docs/SPECTRA_TIMING_CONVENTIONS.md failures 1-2 — a week arguing an audio delay against his ears (the 150 turned out to be SpotFX's `audio_latency_ms`, which SPECTRA's engine never reads — that doc's failure-1 entry is now corrected). **What is measured** (`spectra/services/av_sync_session.py`, one sign convention): `av_offset_ms = light_lag − audio_lag`; light lag = phone sees a light edge − server wrote it; audio lag = phone hears an onset − SPECTRA's OWN live audio hub heard it (`av_sync_audio_ref.py` taps `live.hub` — snapcast.monitor, i.e. speaker time — with server timestamps; never Spotify position, never the stored WAV, never the wandering `shape_offset_ms`); positive = lights BEHIND, negative = lights AHEAD; the phone↔server clock offset cancels in the difference (paired coarsely over WS ping/pong to centre the search). **Light reference**: (a) PATTERN — `av_sync_pattern.py` drives every live virtual white on/off with RANDOM 150-450 ms holds for 12 s over fx_seam (snapshot → flash → revert with transition_ms=1, preview_pause armed, bounded ≤30 s, snapshot persisted + recovered at startup like flare_preview_hold), recording each edge's server time; random holds are load-bearing — a periodic blink correlates at every multiple of its period and the correlator REFUSES it as ambiguous; (b) SHOW — the engine's own jump/short-glide writes as an impulse train, passive, weaker, the live-feed shape. **The arithmetic** (`av_sync_correlate.py`, pure numpy): phone mic log-energy envelope vs hub envelope → onset-flux xcorr; phone luminance (whole-frame mean + a 4×4 region grid, strongest detection wins) vs the pattern → signed-EDGE xcorr (the level-vs-level matched filter of a random telegraph wave has a mean-hold-wide peak that swamps its own noise floor — measured peak ratio ~4 vs ~10 with edges); a number is REFUSED by name (weak / ambiguous / unstable / overlap / no_audio_ref / clock) unless peak ratio ≥5 and the second peak ≤0.8 of the first and sub-window re-reads agree. **Confidence, two kinds, separately**: statistical ± from sub-window spread; SYSTEMATIC terms named with bound, direction and dependence (phone camera pipeline — much smaller when rVFC `captureTime` is present; phone mic pipeline — smaller when `MediaTrackSettings.latency` is reported; exposure integration = ½ frame at the reported fps; bulb rise time; the hub's own input latency, which makes the lights read LATER) — the statement says how far AHEAD or BEHIND the truth could be vs. the reading, and that a CHANGE between two runs on one phone is far tighter than either absolute. **Proof it produces a number** (offline — his room is released and firstmate holds the grant): `scripts/check_av_sync.py` pushes four simulated rooms (known light/audio latencies, phone clock offsets of ±hours/days, 24/30/60 fps cameras with full-frame exposure, sensor + mic noise, hub input latency) through the REAL Session/PatternDriver/AudioReference/correlator behind fake seams: every room lands within 0.5 ms of truth + the three named systematics (e.g. truth −320 → expected −273.3 → measured −273.0 ±3.5), correct sign, room reverted, snapshot file cleared. `tests/test_av_sync_{correlate,session,api}.py` (27 tests). **Phone side** (`spectra/web/src/avsync/capture.ts` + `AvSyncPage.tsx`, `/avsync`, nav "AV Sync"): raw audio/video NEVER leave the phone — AudioWorklet log-energy @ ~11 ms hops (ScriptProcessor fallback), rVFC luminance per frame, both on performance.now() (audio via `getOutputTimestamp()`), streamed as ~3 KB/s of numbers; camera+mic stay raw (no echo cancellation/AGC). **Privacy statement** (help topic `av-sync-privacy`, the session docstring, the welcome message, the page's own card): every hop named (phone → same-origin WS → :8000 proxy → :8010, his network only); the ONE file written is `storage/spectra/av_sync_measurements.json` (numbers + statement + capability flags, last 100), never media; in-memory streams dropped on disconnect. **The vision seam, prepared not built**: `FrameRing` in the session — OFF by default, `POST /api/av-sync/frame-tap` turns on JPEG stills (≤8 in memory, capture-timestamped on the phone, mapped to server time ±RTT/2), `GET /api/av-sync/frame/latest`, in-process `subscribe(cb)`; nothing decodes or recognises anything; phone side `FrameSource` has the luminance extractor as one consumer and the tap as the second. **THE DEPLOY BLOCKER, named**: he reaches SPECTRA over plain http (Tailscale, no `tailscale serve`, verified read-only on the host) and phone browsers hide camera/mic on an insecure origin — the page detects `!isSecureContext` and shows two exact fixes (Chrome's per-origin `unsafely-treat-insecure-origin-as-secure` flag with the origin string to paste — works tonight, no deploy; or HTTPS in front of :8000 via `tailscale serve` — firstmate's/his action, also unblocks the Settings voice mic, which has been silently dead on his phone for the same reason). Not in scope, named: applying the measured number (no SPECTRA knob exists — would be a LEAD-family trim at `bridge.effective_position_ms`), per-category pattern selection, the vision stage itself. |
| §94 | FISH — a new Matrix effect and a scene that is a wholesale copy of Orbits V2 | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed** — PR fm/fish-effect-and-scene; the scene seeder is DRY-RUN ONLY here, `--apply` and the `spectra.service` restart + SPA rebuild are firstmate's; no live storage written | His ask, verbatim (2026-08-25, corr=6dd10a8c3c5bd72a): *"create a new effect and scene that is almost a copy of orbits ... Call it Fish ... the shape of the blobs is a somewhat thin oval that can move like a fish from a birds-eye-view. its spine should flap as it moves, and it should leave a ripple trail ... it should point in the direction it's moving ... 1. They have to turn around in a tight but clear circle, they can't just reverse directions on the spot."* **What is COPIED, not authored**: the scene (`scripts/seed_fish_scene.py`) is a raw-dict deep copy of his live Orbits V2 — all seven flare kinds, all three flare bands with their kinds maps, every initial param, colour handling, journey, dwell curve, mode availability, colour-set acceptance, labels, ramps — plus Orbits' own sequencer SelectorEntry (curve, genre multipliers, dwell weight) re-keyed to the new scene id and any affinity edge naming Orbits mirrored onto Fish. The ONLY differences are the scene id, the name, the scene-local device entry ids, and the Matrix entry's `effect_type`. Nothing is round-tripped through `SceneV2.model_dump_json()` (that would rewrite his flare data via `_migrate_flare_kinds` — the rule `scripts/set_scene_colorset_preference.py` established), every pre-existing scene is byte-compared before the write AND re-read from disk after it, and a re-run upserts rather than duplicating. **What is NEW** (`fx/effects/fish.py`, `fx/VENDOR.md` deviation #21): Orbits' visual language — SoA particles, enter/leave lifecycle, audio impulse plumbing, trail buffer, the twod projection, the phase-key state machine, the whole particle-handoff protocol — carrying different kinematics. Per-fish position, speed and a SCREEN-space heading the oval is laid out along (so it points where it moves and is never sheared by the panel's aspect); a real turn RADIUS caps the turn rate, so an about-face is structurally an arc (measured: ≤3.8°/frame, an about-face never faster than the half-circle its radius implies); a spine drawn as a chain of splats with a wave travelling head-to-tail, its amplitude rising with acceleration and falling while slowing (measured 2.00 accelerating / 1.00 steady / 0.13 decelerating); an expanding ripple wake emitted on every tail beat, composited straight to the output rather than through the trail buffer (measured 0.07 calm → 0.22 loud against a body at ~0.8 — always subtle, stronger on faster, more often on faster, and capped so a ripple stays matched to the size of the fish that made it). **CHARGE/LULL/DROP, his numbers** (`docs/SPECTRA_RESPONSES.md` has the grammar): charge fills a school of up to 12 in unison — the camera follows them so the school holds station and the WATER streams past — then turns the whole school on every beat, never closer than 400ms, with small per-fish variation (measured 7.1° spread, nonzero and small); lull disperses everyone but the fish nearest centre, which holds the centre of view by `phase_progress` 0.5 (measured 1.6px; ~45% of the lull's true wall clock, the timing honesty `blackhole.py`'s `LULL_FILL_PROGRESS` records), then a 20-fish rush enters FROM THE DIRECTION that fish is heading, zooms past for 1.00s with real chaos (17° heading spread) and leaves exactly `particle_count` behind; drop is Orbits' payoff unchanged (centre burst + 2× ballistic ejecta on the phase's FIRST rendered frame, boost decaying, phase self-reset). **SCOPE CHANGES HE MADE MID-BUILD**: (1) mutual avoidance is OUT entirely — no code, no knob, no proof; fish swim through each other. (2) the population-cap bypass (`p_nocap`, the fireworks/blackhole flag) is authorised for the charge school and the lull rush ONLY; ordinary swimming always obeys `particle_count`, asserted across a full charge→lull→drop arc, and `CAP`=96 is proved to hold 16 fish + a 24 school + a 24 rush + a 32-ejecta explosion at once so a rush can never starve the ordinary render. **Cost**: 0.89 ms/frame idle (8 fish), 1.31 ms during a 23-fish rush, on the real headless pipeline at his 72×37 crystal shape. **Named non-copies**: the Strips entry keeps `orbits1d` (his spec is birds-eye 2D; there is no `fish1d` and one was not asked for), and the registry default for `orbit_radius` is fish's own 0.14 rather than Orbits' tuned 0.53 — the key now means TURN radius, and 0.53 is 17px on a 37px-tall panel, i.e. a fish that could not turn inside its own pond. Every other shared registry default is Orbits' own tuned value, asserted. **PENDING HIS EYES**: every one of the 15 new knobs is a considered first guess chosen against measurements, not a tuned value — the look is not done until he has watched it. Wiring: `fx/device_model.PHASE_EFFECTS`, `spectra/services/transition_phases._PARTICLES` (so every registered pair behaves exactly as Orbits' does; the legacy twin is deliberately untouched — fish exists only in the vendored pipeline), `radial.py`'s adopt whitelist (additive). Help (Order 20): new topic `fish-effect`, linked context-sensitively from the Initial Set tab whenever an entry runs fish, plus a Fish paragraph in `response-families`. Proof: `scripts/check_fish.py` (8 measured sections), `tests/test_fish.py` (20). **Room-unproven** — nothing was fired, nothing live was read or written. |
| §95 | THE PREVIEW + OFFSET SYSTEM, FINISHED AND EXTENDED — three audit gaps closed, §84's missing instrument built offline, and the same scrubbing preview given to SCENE TRANSITIONS and the CHARGE/LULL/DROP SEQUENCE | `BUILT` (full), **room-unproven by design** — his room was not granted for this work; every proof is offline (fx.headless renderer, the scratch-engine pattern, verbatim frontend-formula extraction) | His order (2026-08-27, corr=dee5132bf1a25c35): *"Work on the flare preview and offset system and get it finished and tested, then push it to transitions and to the drop sequence."* — the second half he himself deferred with "start with the flares, then we will do lull charge drop".<br><br>**HALF ONE — the audit.** Three honest gaps, each closed red-then-green. **(1) `SpectraTrigger.trigger_offset_ms` was silently inert on 3 of its 4 action kinds.** #172 wired it for `fire_scene` alone and the field's own docstring then had to say the other three "still ignore it" — against an ask that never scoped itself that way ("do events like flares and scene changes carry an offset value... they need to"). Nothing about an OFFSET is action-kind specific: unlike a LEAD, which must know what payoff it aligns and how long that payoff takes, an offset only RELOCATES the moment, so it composes with an instant apply exactly as with a crossfade. `tick()` now applies it uniformly and, on a `fire_response` trigger, ADDS it to the fired band's own `FlareKind.trigger_offset_ms` — legal because both are OFFSET family; an override rule would silently discard whichever he authored second. Both default 0, so provably inert against everything on disk. **(2) The flare preview ignored the settled DROP START anchor.** `kind_lead_ms` is class-BLIND and justified that with "a flare kind previewed in isolation is never a drop/explosion" — true of the kind's TYPE and a different question, because a momentary kind attached to a DROP band fires under the drop rule (`_response_switch_lead_ms` returns 0 unconditionally there). Such a kind previewed as firing `DICE_REROLL_GLIDE_MS` early while production fired it with zero lead: the preview lying about when his flare lands. Latent, not live, in his data; closed for the same reason that drop branch was made unconditional rather than resting on that fact. New `scene_response.kind_anchor_rule`/`kind_attached_classes`; the timeline reports `anchor_rule`/`attached_classes` and the overlay names the drop rule. **(3) §84's missing instrument, BUILT** — see the §84 row, now closed offline. **PLUS a proof-bar upgrade earned by this system's founding defect**: every hold is now proven FROM THE SHOW SIDE. That defect was a hold that REPORTED itself as set (the UI even said "deferred by preview") while his triggers kept firing underneath, because `fire_scene_by_id` never consulted `preview_pause`; every test that existed asked the preview side and passed throughout. `tests/test_preview_holds_the_show.py` drives the real trigger engine over a real position feed with a four-action-kind corpus while a hold is open and measures the ENGINE's output — zero writes at `fx_seam` (the one seam a light byte leaves through), zero response surges, and the deferrals present and NAMED in `fire_history` (a held room and a broken room must not look the same in the log) — replays the same sweep after release to prove the corpus was live, covers the abandoned-heartbeat self-heal, and includes a test that re-creates the ungated pre-fix world and proves the harness goes RED on it.<br><br>**HALF TWO — two new previews, ONE hold.** `flare_preview_hold` grew a `PreviewProgram` seam rather than a second bespoke hold: everything genuinely hard (the once-per-session snapshot, the self-lapsing deadline, the independent sweep, the absolute 3-minute ceiling a heartbeating client cannot push out, the persisted snapshot a restart lands back, both release queues, the 1ms tween-safe revert) was learned expensively — 13m54s of his room, 85 refused scene changes — and stays in one implementation; `open_hold()` is now the thinnest such program, unchanged in behaviour. **TRANSITIONS** (`spectra/services/transition_preview.py`, `/api/preview/transition/*`, `TransitionPreviewOverlay.tsx`): the ruler shows the REAL intensity-scaled crossfade (the same fallback chain `scene_compiler.fire_scene` resolves, so his live `global_transition_ms == 0` reads as the scaled default, not "instant") and marks where its MIDDLE lands — the settled anchor family — as the plain 0.5 midpoint or a registered phased pair's own 0.45. All three numbers come from the NEW `scene_transition_lead.py`, which `trigger_engine._scene_transition_lead_ms_for` now CALLS: the preview asks production's own function rather than re-deriving it. The drag writes the new `SceneV2.trigger_offset_ms` (same family, same clamp, same sign law), honoured on the firing path by `tick()`'s own `_scene_offset_ms`; its one honest bound (an unresolved pick's scene isn't known until the LOOKAHEAD pin commits ~5s ahead, so a larger negative offset degrades to the un-relocated mark — late, never wrong) is recorded in the field docstring and the help copy. **THE DROP SEQUENCE** (`spectra/services/phase_preview.py`, `/api/preview/sequence/*`, `SequencePreviewOverlay.tsx`): each ramp is `scene_response._phase_ramp_ms` — the show's own function — so the dynamic STRETCH to ~90% of the real gap is what the ruler draws, with the remaining ~10% as a separate HANG band (his spec verbatim: "the single blob waiting in lull should reach the center just and hang for just a moment, maybe 10% of the lull time"); two gap sliders move the stretch so the hang can be judged by eye rather than guessed at from a number. The drop is never stretched and BEGINS on its mark. Marks are deliberately NOT draggable — a band's offset is an aggregate over its attached kinds, so a drag would have to pick one, and the per-kind preview that already authors it is named in the panel instead. The live half drives the REAL vendored phase machinery, proven by blackhole's own charge choreography taking `reverse` over mid-preview (behaviour nothing in `spectra/` could have faked). `release_phases()` gained `force=` for the preview's per-lap release (a fresh scratch pair per step, and a drop arms nothing by production's own rule — exactly when a sequence ends); every production call site keeps the guard. No new release/revert queue, so the four-drain-point rule is untouched. Server computes every anchor and fire moment; the frontend schedules against `cues[].at_s` and draws against returned markers. Proofs: `tests/test_transition_preview.py`, `tests/test_phase_preview.py` (both asserting the preview's numbers against `trigger_engine`'s OWN methods), `tests/test_preview_programs_live.py` (real headless fixture), `tests/test_preview_holds_the_show.py` (both new holds at the show-side bar), `scripts/check_preview_cue_loop.mjs` (multi-cue scheduling formulas extracted verbatim, fake clock). Help: two new topics, each `topic=`'d from the component that opens it. Full suite: **zero new failures** — the 4 that fail are the documented environmental set (`test_crystal_activation_verify` ×3, `test_legacy_trigger_migration`), confirmed identical with these new test files excluded. || §96 | AMBIENT IS ONE BINARY TOGGLE AGAIN, its transition SAYS which way it is going, and INTERRUPTING SNAPS | `BUILT` (full), **not deployed and room-unproven by design** — a live-room behaviour change held for his word; `spectra.service` restart + SPA rebuild are firstmate's; no scene/trigger/colour-set data touched, and his stored `ambient_mode` migrates on first load with every other key byte-preserved | His words (2026-08-30): *"let's only ever toggle between Off and On. Off can have a separate setting for if music pause turns ambient on, set it to false for now. The main issue is when I turn on ambient in a room, there is a lag between when i turn it on and when it finishes, so I don't know if it has started or not, and I keep accidentally toggling it multiple times... let's give the butt[on]s in HA and in spectra some clarity that they are 'turning on' or 'Turning off' and let's also give spectra a clear ability to handle what happens if the turn on or off sequence gets interrupted. Interrupting should snap the state. So if is gradually turning ambient off, and I turn it back on, it should just snap to being full brightness."* **MEASURED FIRST, designed against — not re-derived**: turn-OFF 22.6s end to end (the deliberate two-phase ease: `AMBIENT_TRANSITION_MS` 3000 dim + `AMBIENT_CATCHUP_MS` 8000 catch-up, plus 300ms-staggered confirmed writes), turn-ON ~15s across his 17 bulbs, and a press MID-transition took **38s** to win because it queued behind `services.ambient`'s own I/O lock with nothing able to interrupt the sequence inside it. **(1) THE MODEL.** `RoomControlState.ambient_mode` (`"off"/"always"/"auto"`, §52/§53) is REPLACED by `ambient_enabled: bool` + `ambient_on_music_pause: bool`. Migration on load: `"always"` → enabled True; `"off"` and `"auto"` → enabled False; `ambient_on_music_pause` **False in every case**, his explicit "set it to false for now" — so an "auto" room deploys quiet in both halves rather than silently keeping the automatic behaviour he is trying to stop being surprised by. The auto BEHAVIOUR is preserved in code (`ambient_music_gate._desired_hold` keeps its playing-aware branch verbatim, confirmed/unknown asymmetry and all) and gated behind the new switch — disabled, not removed. Sonic's registry swaps the one enum key for the two bools. **(2) THE PHASE CONTRACT — FROZEN**, because another captain builds Home Assistant against it: the `ambient` key on `GET /api/engine/status` and on the SPECTRA websocket (`{"type": "ambient_status", ...}`) carries `intent: "on"|"off"` and `phase: "on"|"off"|"turning_on"|"turning_off"|"unavailable"`. `phase` updates within 1s of any change because the gate PUSHES at every transition start, end and cancel — the 3s poll is the backstop, no longer the mechanism (`spectra/web/src/api/spectraWs.ts` folds the push into the same react-query cache entry the poll writes, so there is no second source of truth). `phase` deliberately reports the TRANSITION's outcome, not the bulbs': a hold broken out of band still reads `on` and the pre-existing `held`/`mode`/`verify`/`verified_age_s` keys carry that honesty exactly as before — collapsing the two would make "is Ambient on" unanswerable. **(3) INTERRUPTION SNAPS.** At most ONE transition task exists (`_transition`, generation-stamped). A new intent cancels the in-flight one at its next safe write boundary — never mid-write to one bulb; `spectra/services/ambient.py`'s new `CancelToken` owns where those boundaries are, and its interruptible `sleep` is what lets a RAMP be abandoned rather than waited out — and applies the new end state with every ramp DROPPED (`snap=True`: no colour glide on a hold, no dim fade and no catch-up on a release). The 300ms write stagger stays: that is zigbee physics, the ramps are choreography. The queue is abolished; a superseded run never writes again and never touches the landed-state bookkeeping (`_transition is tr`), so a slow cancelled turn-off cannot flip `_held` back while the room is genuinely lit. **An UNINTERRUPTED turn-off keeps its two-phase ease in full** — his complaint was the interruption, not the fade. **(4) THE PRESS RETURNS IMMEDIATELY.** `reconcile_ambient_if_changed` now STARTS the transition and returns `{"status": "turning_on"/"turning_off", intent, phase}`; the PUT blocking for the whole sequence is where "I don't know if it has started" began. **(5) THE RELEASED ROOM is never a silent nothing**: a press while `phase == "unavailable"` starts no transition and records nothing as landed, returns `{"status": "dark", "phase": "unavailable", "stored": true}`, and the stored intent (it IS `ambient_enabled`) is applied on the next take-back — app.py's startup/resume lifespan as before, and now `handover.run_handover`'s own commit when SPECTRA becomes the owner. **UI**: the room bar's 💡 button reads "Turning on…"/"Turning off…" while in flight and is NEVER disabled — a press during a transition is the interrupt, not a no-op to prevent — and it toggles against the ROOM's phase, not against this tab's last save. The three-setting dropdown is replaced by an on/off control plus a "When music pauses" checkbox. **PROOF BAR** (`tests/test_ambient_transition.py`, a timestamping mock bridge at his real 17-bulb count with the pacing constants scaled 1/20th so a sequence still has real duration to interrupt; every assertion is a ratio or an ordering against those constants, never a bare second count): the **38s shape reproduced RED** against the pre-rework call shape (two overlapping un-cancellable `ambient.reconcile()` calls — the interrupting write lands only after both ramps) and then **green** through the new owner (first write within one write slot of the cancel, total bounded by bulb count, no `dynamics` on the snapped body); a rapid triple-press landing exactly the final intent through one surviving generation; a superseded run never landing its state; phase walking `off→turning_on→on` and `on→turning_off→off` with the pushes observed in order; the cancel push carrying the NEW direction; `unavailable` end to end through take-back; the music-pause branch inert at the shipped default and alive once switched on; the uninterrupted two-phase ease still intact; and the whole thing through the REAL routes (`PUT /api/room-controls` → "turning_on", `GET /api/engine/status` → the frozen contract, settling to "on" without the press having waited). Migration proof (each old mode → the specified pair, every other key byte-preserved) is `tests/test_room_controls.py::test_ambient_mode_migrates_to_the_binary_toggle`. Help topic `ambient` rewritten and linked from the panel (not orphaned). **NOT touched**: `ambient.py`'s two-phase release choreography for uninterrupted turn-offs, the status-honesty verifier and its `verified_age_s`, the Hue-area selection, `docs/SPECTRA_TIMING_CONVENTIONS.md` (no timing family changed — nothing here is a lead or an offset). **HA-side work is explicitly another captain's**, against the frozen contract above. |
| §97 | THE LIGHT-FIELD MAP GOES BELOW THE DEVICE — a strip can be mapped in PARTS, and a wave runs ALONG it | `BUILT` (full), **room-unproven by design** — his fixtures were not granted for this work; every proof is offline (fx.headless renderer, a synthetic camera, verbatim ground truth declared before each run). Not deployed by this build. | **His own correction to §-the-light-field slice, verbatim (2026-08-31):** *"A single device that spans the direction of the wave should be able to show the effect. the tv mapper is wrapped around a tv. It should be able to run a dimness wave vertically."* His original spec had said "individual and grouped leds" all along; the slice's device-only fence cut that off and he caught it. **Nothing proven was invalidated** — the mapping session, the per-emitter protocol, the held-room chain, the footprint derivation, `per_emitter_scalar` (already emitter-agnostic), the pages and the bounded writer's compose+tick shape all carry over untouched. Two pieces were missing and both are built. **(1) RANGE LIGHTING FOR CAPTURE.** `fx/effects/pixelRange.py` (VENDOR.md deviation #26) renders white over a configured inclusive pixel range and black elsewhere — registry-exempt on the phase-key/`burst_rockets` precedent, so it is an instrument and never an authoring surface — driven through the SAME one write seam inside the SAME held-room step. `spectra/services/emitters.py` makes enumeration granularity-aware: per CAPTURE RUN, `auto` (the default, resolved PER DEVICE — segments for a strip, whole fixture for a Hue bulb, his "grouped" answered without a global setting) / `device` / `segment` / `block`-of-N. A sub-device emitter's id is the NEW SHAPE `spectra/models/room_map.py` always said a finer granularity would be — `tv-mapper:seg1[20-39]` — plus a structured `ranges` field, both additive, so every footprint already on disk keeps working and device granularity is byte-identical to the shipped slice. **(2) THE PER-PIXEL GAIN MASK.** `fx/virtual_gain_mask.py` + ONE multiply in `Virtual.assemble_frame` (VENDOR.md deviation #25), right after the two the fork already does — the layer the driver reads and the preview taps. Pushed, never pulled (`fx/` may not import `spectra/`), general by construction: a float array per virtual, which is also what implode/explode will need. `room_effects` fills it from per-emitter gains via each emitter's range; a virtual driven only by whole-device emitters keeps the single-scalar path and NO mask is installed anywhere, so the multiply is not reached at all. **THE GUARDRAIL DID NOT MOVE**: a range is an ADDRESSING FACT from the device configuration (the same kind of fact `virtual_ids` already was), in the virtual's own effect-pixel space — the identical space the lamp lights and the mask indexes, so capture and render address the same pixels with nothing to convert. No coordinate, no metre, no fixture position is stored or derived anywhere in this work. **HONEST BOUNDS, stated not hidden**: sub-device capture AND a sub-device wave both need SPECTRA to own the lights (the lamp and the mask live in this process) and are refused BY NAME with nothing written otherwise; a copy-mapping virtual cannot light one segment alone and says so; a run past 120 emitters is capped and named; re-mapping a device drops its previous granularity first, so a fixture is never driven twice. **PROOF BAR, all offline.** `scripts/check_light_field_granularity.py`: the range lamp measured on the REAL render pipeline (its range lit, everything else EXACTLY zero, an inverted range lighting nothing); a synthetic three-segment TV wrap captured at segment granularity yielding THREE footprints, each equal cell-for-cell to its own segment's painted region and pairwise DISJOINT; the NEGATIVE CONTROL that the same room at device granularity yields ONE footprint equal to their union; and the ownership refusal writing nothing. `scripts/check_room_effect_mask.py`: a vertical wave across ONE wrapped device, phase lag between its bottom and top pixel ranges measured off `assemble_frame()` and matching the wave's own travel to **0.0°**, with depth-0 (exactly flat) and speed-0 (standing, three runs at three different constant levels) controls, the bit-identity control (no mask installed at ANY tick on a whole-device room; an explicit all-ones mask renders a byte-identical frame; a wrong-length mask is skipped and counted, never resampled), and the room coming back BYTE-IDENTICAL after stop. **WRITE COST re-measured at the new granularity**, real clock, against the 15 Hz (66.7 ms) tick: whole device **p50 4.0 ms / p95 7.3 ms**, three ranges masked **p50 0.010 ms / p95 0.031 ms**, twenty ranges masked (a real TV wrap's shape) **p50 0.009 ms / p95 0.020 ms** — the target holds with large margin, and finer granularity is CHEAPER, not dearer: a masked virtual needs no seam write at all (0 writes/s), so the plan's named risk runs the other way. Also proven: twenty emitters resolve into ONE mask, so the render sees one multiply however fine it gets. Help: `room-mapping-granularity` and `room-effects-along-a-strip`, both linked from the components that open them. Sonic: `list_rooms` now reports each device's emitter count and ranges, and `set_room_effect(device_ids=...)` validates against DEVICES (a real bug the finer granularity exposed — `mapped_ids()` returns emitter ids and would have rejected every legitimate selection); choosing a granularity stays excluded, as an argument to the mapping run Sonic already cannot start. Full suite: **zero new failures** — the documented environmental set only (`test_crystal_activation_verify` ×3, `test_legacy_trigger_migration`, plus an order-flaky blackhole/blob-rush threshold that passes in isolation), confirmed identical on the pristine base tree in this same worktree. |
| §98 | THE COMMISSIONING INSTRUMENT'S FIRST FIELD RUNS FAILED TOTALLY, AND THE CAUSE IS RESOLUTION — the camera cannot tell his 736 pixels apart from where it stands, and now says so in four seconds instead of forty-two | `BUILT` (fix + reproduction), **room-unproven and not deployed** — every proof is offline against the evidence kept from his own two runs (`data/commissioning-field-evidence/`); the frozen table's five tolerances are UNTOUCHED | **What happened** (2026-09-01, twice, identically): §8's gray-code commissioning ran mechanically clean on his real `tv-mapper` — 22 captures, ~42 s, the substitution right, the room restored — and judged itself FAIL with 0 of 736 pixels decoded, ~3,165 lit camera pixels ALL undecodable, 0 out of range (run 2: 0 lit pixels at all). Offline the same decoder recovered 76/76. **The cause, from the evidence and not from the hypothesis on file**: the raw frame kept from that pose is 320x180 with all but 66 pixels exactly zero — the whole composition arrives as THREE compact glows of ~8x4 camera pixels, peaking at 99 of 255. At ~0.09 camera pixels per composition index, every pattern and its inverse land on the SAME camera pixels and cancel: `|p-q|` goes to zero, no bit is confident, and every lit pixel is undecodable however much light is in the frame. **The timing hypothesis is ruled out, not merely unconfirmed**: a mistimed stack compares two DIFFERENT patterns, which differ — the low bits keep real contrast and pixels decode CONFIDENTLY to wrong indices (out-of-range > 0). His runs showed the opposite shape: zero confident bits, low bits at ~0.01 against a 0.20 bar while the HIGH bits sat at 0.9. **Three fixes, all offline-proven**: (1) `gray_code.resolution_report` answers "can this camera resolve this composition at all" from the dark/full reference pair alone, so the run REFUSES BY NAME two captures in (`mapping_refusals.unresolvable_composition`) rather than spending the room's dark time to reach a verdict about the wrong thing — the table is never handed a stack the camera could not read; (2) the lit gate's peak is the mean of the brightest few pixels, not the 99th percentile, which silently assumed the composition covers >1% of the frame and produced BOTH field numbers (3,165 pixels of averaging noise, then zero) — plus a one-grey-level floor, the sensor's own quantisation, not a scene assumption; (3) every decode carries per-bit contrast (`Decode.bit_contrast`) and the resolution report, so a future failure says WHERE it died in its own response instead of needing frames nobody kept. **The honest bound, stated rather than buried**: 736 pixels need ~1,472 camera pixels of imaged strip (Nyquist on gray bit 0, which alternates in runs of two), and the entire border of the 320x180 frame the phone sends is ~1,000 — so NO pose fixes this at the current frame size; the phone captures at 1280x720 and downsamples before sending. A retest at his own pose is still worth ~4 s (it confirms the diagnosis on his hardware); a run that can DECODE needs a decision about the wire's frame size, which is a contract the mapping instrument shares. Proofs: `scripts/check_commissioning.py` §3c (the field regime reproduced on demand, the discriminator, the refusal, and the same dim room decoding 88 pixels), `tests/test_gray_code.py`, `tests/test_commissioning.py`. |
| §99 | THE ROOM'S OWN LIGHT IS MEASURED ACROSS EVERY COMMISSIONING STACK, and a moving window REFUSES BY NAME instead of producing an instrument-indicted fail somebody has to interpret | `BUILT`, **room-unproven and not deployed** — PR fm/ambient-stability-gate; every proof is offline through the real decode path; the frozen table's five tolerances and the exposure-lock refusal are UNTOUCHED | **What happened** (2026-09-01, his first per-fixture run, the right sconce): the phone was pointed at the fixture WITH A WINDOW IN VIEW, in daylight, with cloud moving. §98's resolution gate passed honestly — 5.375 camera px/index, peak 49.3, floor 7.4 — so the pose could read that fixture. The decode still came back 34 of 88, 22 of those 34 out of order, 392 of 473 lit camera pixels unconfident, and `out_of_range_pixels = 30`: §98's own CONFIDENT-WRONG signature, which only happens when a stack compared two DIFFERENT SCENES. Cloud changed the daylight between a pattern and its inverse, and lit-minus-dark cannot subtract a reference that moved underneath it. **The captain's ruling**: an instrument that cannot name its own defeated condition makes every future user depend on someone remembering today. **What was built**: `spectra/services/ambient_stability.py` (its docstring is the binding statement) fixes a background set ONCE from the reference pair — the dimmer half of `full - dark`, a QUANTILE not a threshold, so an ambient step between those two captures cannot collapse the set exactly when it is needed — and measures the SAME camera pixels in every later capture, whole-frame and per tile of a 4x4 grid (a window is a REGION). **Only LAMP-FREE comparisons are gated, and that took a redesign**: the PAIR delta (a pattern against its own inverse — complementary halves, so the fixture's spill cancels exactly, and it is also the quantity that lands in the bit's arithmetic) and DARK-AGAINST-DARK (a CLOSING dark capture, now the 23rd of every pass, ~1.5 s of ~37). A lamp-ON capture's distance from the opening dark carries the fixture's own spill and is REPORTED but never gated — gating it would refuse a room whose fixture lights the walls, which is a wall, not a gate. **The bound**: `max(2.0 grey levels, 0.10 x peak)` — the fraction is half `gray_code.BIT_CONFIDENCE`, so drift alone reaches half the bar a bit must clear and cannot manufacture a confident wrong bit; the floor is twice `MIN_BRIGHT_LEVELS`, the sensor's own quantisation, so a dim frame does not refuse on noise. **Timing of the refusal matches the harm**: a change that MOVES wrecks the decode and is refused on the first bad pair, mid-stack, before the rest of the room's dark time is spent; a change that arrives and STAYS is invisible to every pair — correctly, it cancels in the bit too — corrupts no bit, and is caught by the closing dark. **The cross-check** (`gray_code.confident_wrong_signature`, §98's dichotomy read off a decode that already exists) CONFIRMS a refusal that already stands on its own measurement, and never carries one: when the ambient is measured STEADY and the signature appears anyway, the frozen table's fail stands exactly as before with a note saying the room's light has been ruled out. **Both directions proven**, the bar the marginal boundary set: today's cloud reproduced through the REAL decoder (out-of-range > 0 with real low-bit contrast, resolution verdict still `ok`) and refused by name; a steady room and a shaded one that genuinely moves by 0.6x the bound both run through to a judged table; the bound is proven at 0.7x (passes) and 1.6x (refuses); and a monkeypatched no-gate run reproduces his outcome, so the harness can fail on the defect it was written for. **Deferred and stated**: mapping runs do not use the gate — their per-emitter dark/lit pairs are a different shape and the deadline scope was commissioning. Spec: `tests/test_ambient_stability.py`, `scripts/check_commissioning.py` §3. |

---
| §99 | COMMISSIONING RUNS PER FIXTURE (or per segment), AND A MARGINAL POSE REFUSES — the captain's answer to §98: ask a question the camera can actually answer, and make the boundary conservative enough that it never answers confidently and wrongly | `BUILT` (full), **room-unproven and not deployed** — every proof is offline; the frozen table's five tolerances are UNTOUCHED and the wire's 320x180 frame contract is UNTOUCHED | **The ruling** (2026-09-01, PR fm/per-fixture-commissioning): §98 proved no pose can decode 736 pixels through the frame the phone sends (0.09 camera px per index against the 2.0 Nyquist needs), so the instrument stops asking that question. `POST /api/rooms/{id}/commission` now takes `targets` — `["fixtures"]` (`per_fixture: true`), `["segments"]`, or explicit `"device:<id>"` / `"segment:<n>"`; omitting it commissions the whole composition exactly as before, which on his tv-mapper still refuses in four seconds with the arithmetic. **What a target is, and what it is not**: `commissioning.slice_composition` selects a subset of the STORED mapper's own segments, keeps their stored numbers and stored order, and remembers each pixel's place in the whole (`global_indices`) — while re-addressing them 0..N-1 for the gray code, which is the entire point: one sconce is 88 pixels, 7 patterns and ~176 camera pixels where the stitched whole is 736, 10 patterns and ~1,472 of a frame whose whole border is ~1,000. The stored layout is always derived at the composition's OWN size and sliced afterwards (`slice_layout`); a layout that does not carry one of a slice's pixels reports UNMEASURED rather than a rectangle fitted at the slice's size. **The aggregate cannot read greener than the whole run it replaces** (`commission_compare.aggregate`): every target is judged by the SAME frozen table, a field is as bad as its worst target, and a target that produced no decode contributes UNMEASURED to every row with its own sentence — never dropped from the denominator. A target the camera cannot read ends that TARGET, not the run, so the next fixture is still asked. **MARGINAL REFUSES, and it is the whole point**, in his words: "marginal is the state that produces a confident wrong answer". `gray_code.RESOLUTION_SAFETY_FACTOR` (1.25) sits on top of MIN_CAMERA_PX_PER_INDEX and `resolution_report` reports three states — `ok`, `marginal` (above Nyquist, inside the margin), `impossible` (below it) — with BOTH refusing states refusing and the sentence saying which. `resolvable` follows `verdict == ok`, so every existing caller inherits the conservative boundary rather than opting into it. The reason a margin is needed is gray code's own guarantee working against you: a low bit flipped by a fraction of a camera pixel decodes to a NEIGHBOUR, so a marginal pose returns a plausible wrong arrangement rather than a visible failure. His ring alone — 560 px, ~1,120 needed and ~1,400 to be trusted against ~1,000 available — is the case it exists to refuse. It is NOT a knob for getting a run to pass. **Proofs**: `scripts/check_commissioning.py` §7 (a per-fixture pose at his own composition's real sizes: both 88-pixel sconces decode 88/88 at ~25 camera px each while the 560-pixel ring refuses from the same pose, and the aggregate comes out `incomplete` with the ring named; then the boundary proven on BOTH sides against a box-integrating camera whose reported `camera_px_per_index` IS the number under test), plus §3c unchanged and still red on the stitched whole; `tests/test_commissioning_per_fixture.py`, `tests/test_commissioning.py`, `tests/test_gray_code.py`. |

| §100 | UNATTENDED CAPTURE — a client that establishes the camera session on its own and a queue runner that executes a declared list of map/commissioning runs end to end, with an honest ledger of what still needs his hands | `BUILT` (full), **room-unproven and not deployed** — every proof is offline against a synthetic camera and an isolated local instance; the exposure-lock refusal, the 320x180 wire-frame contract and the frozen commissioning table are all UNTOUCHED | **The bottleneck** (2026-09-01): a capture run needed a person at every step — open the page on a device with a camera, grant it, wait for the lock, aim, keep the tab alive, press Start, press the next one. He ran one overnight queue and it worked only because he personally set the session up before bed, so every capture experiment queued behind his availability. **Three pieces.** `spectra/capture_client/` is a process on any machine with a webcam (`python -m spectra.capture_client --url ... --queue ...`): ffmpeg for pixels at the wire's own 320x180 grey8, `v4l2-ctl` for the controls, reconnect with backoff, and a POSE TOKEN minted INSIDE `camera.open()` so a reconnecting client may assert its pose across a dropped socket (one measurement, not two) and structurally cannot assert it across a camera reopen. `spectra/services/capture_queue.py` walks a declared list, waits for a session that is present AND LOCKED, keeps partials, carries on past a refusal, retries only a `partial` and only when declared, names a pose change, and rewrites its record after EVERY item. `spectra/services/capture_runs.py` is the new single seam both the page's button and the queue execute through — the run lock, the session gate and the runs themselves moved there, so a gate added once is added to both. **THE HONESTY IS STRUCTURAL, and it is the whole reason this is acceptable**: automating the lock REQUEST is the point; automating the lock CONFIRMATION would forge the instrument's signature. `camera.apply_lock()` asks the driver and then READS THE CONTROL BACK, `read_lock()` only ever returns what `v4l2-ctl --get-ctrl` printed, and a driver that ignores the write reports auto — proven with a fake ctl that accepts a set and keeps its old value (`tests/test_capture_client.py`). The synthetic camera used by every proof has NO locked default, so a proof cannot pass without exercising the gate. A machine with no camera CONNECTS ANYWAY and reports `camera_error`, because dying quietly on a laptop nobody is looking at is the failure this exists to remove. **Three new sentences in `mapping_refusals`** (no camera / session lost / queue stopped) plus one FACT (`pose_changed_note`), and `lock_refusal` reworded once to speak to both client kinds rather than growing a second wording. **The ledger is the deliverable** — `docs/UNATTENDED_CAPTURE.md` states what now runs with zero human involvement, what needs a human once (aim the camera and choose the pose, camera permission, ffmpeg/v4l2-ctl, confirming that camera can lock at all, declaring the queue), and what still needs his hands per run (SPECTRA owning the lights, a second pose for unseen emitters, judging a `findings` verdict, deciding what to do with a `marginal` refusal). **Proofs**: `scripts/check_capture_queue_e2e.py` (real server, real WebSocket, the REAL client, a synthetic camera: a five-item queue with no human action after start, a mid-queue refusal it carries on past, a dropped socket whose partial is kept and whose declared retry completes, the pose held across the drop and NAMED across a reopen, the gate refusing an automated client, and a blind machine saying so — 42 checks, run from pytest), `tests/test_capture_queue.py`, `tests/test_capture_client.py`. |

own proposed fix turned out to be unsafe in Hybrid mode, and he replaced the underlying idea
with a different, per-scene colour-set preference design — see §72's note, not a row here.
