| OQ-13 | Intensity-bucket MANAGEMENT — his ask ("propose clean solutions to how to manage the intensity bucketing"), asked but deliberately not decided or built this task. Three options, each anchored to the bands he already has (§81 established the bands ARE the bucketing mechanism — none of these replace or duplicate it): **(A) Ship §81 as the floor, nothing more.** Cost: every scene still hand-authors its own band breakpoints from a blank strip — checked live, not assumed: all 9 of his real scenes' `flare` response class use the IDENTICAL shape today, `(0.0,0.35) (0.35,0.7) (0.7,1.0)` (`GET /spectra/api/scenes`, read-only) — reached independently, nine separate times, with nothing keeping them in sync; retuning the middle breakpoint everywhere is 9 manual edits today. Buys: zero new concepts, zero migration, nothing to hold two mental models of. **(B) A per-band `label: str|None` field** (e.g. "Quiet"/"Building"/"Peak"), cosmetic only — `select_band`'s numeric-range logic untouched, shown in the BandStrip/lane-rack headers instead of raw `[0.35–0.70)`. Cost: one small additive schema field (old scenes load unchanged, absent = today's numbers-only display). Buys: legibility once a band holds up to 4 lanes' worth of kinds, and an easier name to use when telling Sonic "retune the Peak bucket" — does not address the 9-scenes-diverging-silently cost (A) leaves open. **(C) A shared, named band-SHAPE profile** (boundaries only — `{intensity_min, intensity_max}` pairs, never the kinds/scale inside them, which stay scene-local by construction per §81's scoping finding) — the same "named profile, one edit retunes every scene using it" pattern already proven for curve profiles and drift profiles, with the same Detach/Promote UX `SequencingTab.tsx` already built and this task's own brief pointed at. Cost: a THIRD profile concept alongside curve/drift profiles for him to hold in mind; a real Detach/Promote UI to design (non-trivial — the curve-profile version was its own dedicated pass); a legibility risk if two scenes share boundaries but not kinds ("why did retuning THIS scene's Peak bucket range also move THAT scene's Peak bucket, when the flares inside are totally different?"). Buys: the one-edit-many-scenes retune (A)'s cost names directly — worth it only if he actually wants his 9 scenes' shared shape ENFORCED going forward, not merely observed as a current coincidence. **Recommendation, not a decision**: (A) is the honest default until he says otherwise — his 9 scenes converging without a shared object suggests convention, not an unmet need for enforcement; (B) is cheap enough to fold into a later pass regardless; (C) should wait for him to actually hit the "I retuned it 8 times and missed one" pain before building the third profile concept for a problem observed, not reported. | §81 (the built bands/lanes this option set builds on top of, not around); `data/spectra-design-decisions.md`'s existing curve-profile Detach/Promote precedent this task cites as (C)'s template. |

## Confidence & scope of this document

- Ledger states are grounded in the 2026-08-14 capability audit (46 capabilities, code-cited,
  `data/spotfx-capability-audit/report.md`) plus a direct code/backlog re-check run while writing
  this spec (2026-08-15) to catch same-day drift, updated again the same day after PR #57, #67,
  and #69 merged, and refreshed again 2026-08-16 against git log, PR bodies (gh-axi), and
  `data/backlog.md` to close the gap that had opened since PR #68 (§43–§66, D6, the day-one bar,
  and §9's ambient-composition ruling all touched in that pass), and refreshed again 2026-08-17
  against git log through PR #113 (§5's status corrected from "PR open" to merged, §63's stale
  "PR #91 open" day-one-bar reference corrected to merged, §68–§70 added for per-item mode
  availability, the Colour Set/Group preview gesture, and the SPA cache-header defect), and
  again 2026-08-17 after §71 (top-bar regrouping, PR #116) and §72 (the "Black Hole ignores
  Light mode" investigation — his own proposed data fix found unsafe in Hybrid mode and
  superseded by a different design, no data or code changed, finding kept as a standing
  warning). **Where the two disagreed, the code won** — those rows are tagged
  "DELTA since audit" (currently: §6).
- **What this document does not cover:** settings/config minutiae the audit itself deliberately
  grouped into parent capabilities rather than itemising (its own stated limitation, inherited
  here); anything outside the original 46 + the day-one bar + backlog captain-decision items as
  of 2026-08-15; exact on-screen animation feel or gesture nuance — the audit is source-only, no
  live UI runs, and this spec carries that same caveat forward rather than re-verifying it;
  **deployed state vs. landed state** — see [Landed vs. deployed](#landed-vs-deployed-a-real-gap-this-document-does-not-close),
  a limitation this document names but does not close.
- **Confidence:** MEDIUM-HIGH on rows carried straight from the audit (inherited, not
  re-derived). HIGH on the rows independently re-checked against current code, PR bodies, and
  cited source docs (not just the backlog's own summary of them) while writing and then updating
  this spec: §6, §7, §9, §10, §44, §49, §51 — §44 in particular corrected a stale-looking backlog
  checkbox (the scene-id pool decision reads "open" there, but `RULING.md` and the merged code
  both show it was actually answered — code and the cited primary source won over the tracker).
  §1/§19/§20/§22/§23 (2026-08-15, PR #71) are also HIGH: §1's root-cause
  claim is grounded in a live measurement against the Admiral's own room (7 timestamped samples,
  all confirmed `is_playing=true`, over ~40s of real playback), not inference from code alone —
  and explicitly separates that measured evidence from his own "still adapting" hypothesis, which
  the stable-across-the-window offset argues against rather than confirms.
  §56/§62 (2026-08-16, re-checked against `docs/spectra-star-edges-freeze.md`, PR bodies, and git
  log rather than trusted from their own stale in-PR text) and D6/§61 (2026-08-16, re-checked
  against live status-log lines and PR #91/#93's own bodies — including a live correction that
  caught and removed an over-claimed drop-rate finding before it reached this document) are also
  HIGH for the same reason.
- This spec deliberately does **not** track day-to-day PR review status, CI results, or
  room-window scheduling — see [Maintenance protocol](#maintenance-protocol). That's
  `data/backlog.md`.
- **A stale row here is worse than a missing one.** If a `BUILT` marking looks surprising, check
  the code before repeating it in front of the Admiral — do not trust this document blindly, the
  same failure that made it necessary in the first place.

## Where this lives, and why

`docs/SPECTRA_SPEC.md`, in the SpotFX repository, versioned with the code — same convention as
`docs/SPECTRA_HANDOVER.md`, `docs/SPECTRA_PROCESS_SPLIT.md`, `docs/SPECTRA_RESPONSES.md`. Every
contributor and agent shares one copy through normal git history; no separate wiki or fleet-home
doc to fall out of sync with the code it describes. Referenced from `AGENTS.md`
(`CLAUDE.md` is a symlink to it) so a fresh agent finds it without being told.

---
| §100 | THE WIRE FRAME IS PER RUN, AND THE CAMERA HAS TWO MANUAL LEVERS — the commissioning read moves to 1920x1080 by arithmetic, ordinary footprint mapping stays at 320x180, and a manual integration time and gain are per-run requests that are always read back | `BUILT` (full), **room-unproven and not deployed** — every proof is offline (synthetic frames, `fx.headless`, a real uvicorn+WebSocket e2e); the frozen table's five tolerances are UNTOUCHED, and the resolution REFUSAL boundary moves only because its INPUT arithmetic did | **The owner's words** (2026-09-01): "raise video frame size and tweak whatever settings help", toward a thorough camera test the same evening. **The arithmetic, which chose the number rather than the number being picked** (`spectra/services/capture_settings.py` is the binding statement): a gray-code decode needs about `gray_code.MIN_CAMERA_PX_PER_INDEX` (2) camera pixels per composition index, so his 736-pixel `tv-mapper` needs ~1,472 of imaged strip and ~1,840 to clear `RESOLUTION_SAFETY_FACTOR`; a strip wrapped once round a screen images as a PERIMETER, and the whole perimeter of a 320x180 frame — the television filling it edge to edge — is 2x(320+180) = 1,000. **No pose could ever have worked**, which is §98's finding restated as a property of the wire rather than of his room. `commission_profile_for()` picks the SMALLEST rung carrying 736 with `COMMISSION_POSE_MARGIN` (2x) at a `REFERENCE_FILL` (77%-of-frame-width) pose: the bare minimum is 960x540 (~924 indices, 1.26x — which would land ON the marginal boundary in a real hand-held shot), and 1920x1080 carries ~1,848. **The stored 64x36 map grid is UNCHANGED**: every rung of the ladder (320x180, 640x360, 960x540, 1280x720, 1920x1080) is 16:9 and an exact whole multiple of it, so `light_field.downsample` stays a box mean with no interpolation to explain and a grid derived from a 1080p frame is directly comparable with one from 320x180. Pixels stay `image/grey8`, uncompressed, at every rung — the no-lossy-codec rule is absolute and has nothing to do with size. **A MAP STILL SENDS 320x180**: a footprint is a 64x36 grid, so more pixels would cost bandwidth for nothing and night runs stay cheap; `run_mapping` ASSERTS that size rather than assuming it, since a commissioning read earlier in the same session borrowed the big one. **NEVER UPSCALE, asserted on both sides**: `capture_settings.choose` picks the largest rung no bigger than both the request and the camera's own image, both clients report `source_width`/`source_height` on every frame, and a frame larger than its source is dropped and NAMED (`mapping_refusals.upscaled_frame`) — interpolated pixels would inflate `resolution_report`'s camera-pixel count and let an unreadable target report that it is readable, which is the MARGINAL confident-wrong-answer arriving through a side door. A camera that tops out at 720p still runs, at 720p, and the run reports the rung it got: an honest downgrade is a RESULT, never a refusal. **THE TWO LEVERS**: `exposure_time` in 100-MICROSECOND units on BOTH paths (V4L2 `exposure_time_absolute`, W3C `exposureTime`) so nothing converts, and `gain` passed through verbatim (V4L2 `gain` / the browser's `iso`, device-specific scales with no shared meaning). Both optional; asking for neither is converge-then-freeze exactly as shipped. Both are READ BACK — the browser out of `getSettings()`, the capture client out of `v4l2-ctl --get-ctrl` — and a lever the camera did not take refuses BY NAME before any light (`manual_camera_unavailable`), including the dangerous case where the camera answered with a DIFFERENT number. **FRAME-RATE HONESTY**: a sensor integrating for E seconds gives at most 1/E fps and a capture averages whatever ARRIVED in its window, so `capture_windows`/`capture_window` widen the CAPTURE windows to still buy `MIN_FRAMES`, `run_estimate_s` prices the widened run, and an exposure no legal window can average refuses (`exposure_too_long`); with no manual exposure both are an EXACT pass-through of the shipped protocol. **THE EXPOSURE COMPARISON** (`spectra/services/exposure_test.py`, `POST /api/rooms/{id}/exposure-test`, a panel on the Rooms page) turns "his ten cannot resolve" into "could not, AT DEFAULT CAMERA SETTINGS" or confirms it: one emitter, one pose, two regimes back to back, `better`/`ratio`/`summary`. It stores NOTHING (throwaway room copy, no `save_room`) and puts the camera back in a `finally`; the two weights are deliberately on different byte scales and the summary says so; a default regime that saw nothing is a RESULT. **ONE IMPLEMENTATION OF THE NEGOTIATION**: `capture_settings.CameraNegotiation`, inherited by `MappingSession` AND by every test double (`SessionCameraDouble`) — seven fake sessions live across `tests/`/`scripts/`, and a gate they MODEL is a gate no proof exercises. **KNOWN AND OPEN, NOT FIXED BY THIS**: `resolution_report` counts lit AREA, so a strip thick enough for its LEDs' images to overlap sideways reports more camera pixels per index than it linearly resolves — measured at 5.7 where the perimeter supports ~1.4 — and the gate can therefore pass a pose that decodes to NEIGHBOURS. His own field frames were thin glows, so the gate was right about them; measuring linear extent from the reference pair alone is separate work, and inventing a boundary for it in passing is exactly what a pre-registered instrument must not do. **Proofs**: `scripts/check_commissioning.py` §3d — his own composition at the reference pose, where 320x180 decodes 732 of 736 CONFIDENTLY AND WRONG (a median 0.76 LED spacings out, p95 1.21) and 1920x1080 lands at 0.12, with ACCURACY rather than a count as the assertion; `tests/test_capture_settings.py`, `tests/test_camera_levers_in_runs.py`, `tests/test_exposure_test.py`, and `scripts/check_capture_queue_e2e.py` §5, which proves the negotiation over the REAL client, WebSocket and server (asked 1920x1080, got 320x180 from a camera that has no more, said so). |
